    40→def _save_state_or_exit(state: dict, state_file: str) -> None:
    41→    """Persist state with a consistent CLI error boundary."""
    42→    try:
    43→        state_mod.save_state(state, state_file)
    44→    except OSError as exc:
    45→        print_error(f"could not save state: {exc}")
    46→        sys.exit(1)

    55→        sys.exit(1)

    58→def cmd_resolve(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    59→    """Resolve finding(s) matching one or more patterns."""
    60→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    61→    _validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)
    62→
    63→    state_file = state_path(args)
    64→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    65→    _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation(
    66→        state,
    67→        args,
    68→        attestation=attestation,
    69→        resolve_all_patterns_fn=_resolve_all_patterns,
    70→    )
    71→    prev = _previous_score_snapshot(state)
    72→    prev_subjective_scores = {
    73→        str(dim): (coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0)
    74→        for dim, payload in (state.get("subjective_assessments") or {}).items()
    75→        if isinstance(dim, str)
    76→    }
    77→
    78→    all_resolved = _resolve_all_patterns(state, args, attestation=attestation)
    79→    if not all_resolved:
    80→        status_label = "resolved" if args.status == "open" else "open"
    81→        print(
    82→            colorize(
    83→                f"No {status_label} findings matching: {' '.join(args.patterns)}",
    84→                "yellow",
    85→            )
    86→        )
    87→        return
    88→
    89→    _save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)
    90→
    91→    # Remove resolved items from the living plan queue (best-effort)
    92→    try:
    93→        from desloppify.engine.plan import has_living_plan, load_plan, purge_ids, save_plan
    94→
    95→        if has_living_plan():
    96→            plan = load_plan()
    97→            purged = purge_ids(plan, all_resolved)
    98→            if purged:
    99→                save_plan(plan)
   100→                print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: {purged} item(s) removed from queue.", "dim"))
   101→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
   102→        print(colorize("  Warning: could not update living plan.", "yellow"), file=sys.stderr)
   103→

   105→    _print_wontfix_batch_warning(

   109→    )
   110→    has_review_findings = any(

   112→        for fid in all_resolved

   128→    )

   132→    narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(

   135→    )
   136→    if narrative.get("milestone"):

   138→
   139→    next_command = _print_next_command(state)
   140→    _write_resolve_query_entry(

   157→def cmd_ignore_pattern(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   158→    """Add a pattern to the ignore list."""
   159→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   160→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):

   162→        sys.exit(1)
   163→
   164→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   165→    state_file = runtime.state_path
   166→    state = runtime.state
   167→    prev = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
   168→
   169→    config = runtime.config
   170→    config_mod.add_ignore_pattern(config, args.pattern)
   171→    config["needs_rescan"] = True
   172→    _save_config_or_exit(config)
   173→
   174→    removed = state_mod.remove_ignored_findings(state, args.pattern)
   175→    state.setdefault("attestation_log", []).append(
   176→        {
   177→            "timestamp": state.get("last_scan"),
   178→            "command": "ignore",
   179→            "pattern": args.pattern,
   180→            "attestation": attestation,
   181→            "affected": removed,
   182→        }
   183→    )
   184→    _save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)
   185→
   186→    print(colorize(f"Added ignore pattern: {args.pattern}", "green"))
   187→    if removed:
   188→        print(f"  Removed {removed} matching findings from state.")
   189→    config_warning = check_config_staleness(config)
   190→    if config_warning:
   191→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
   192→    print_score_update(state, prev)
   193→

   195→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None

   199→    )
   200→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)

1→"""Plan override subcommand handlers: describe, note, skip, unskip, done, reopen, focus."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import sys
     7→
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.plan._resolve import resolve_ids_from_patterns
    11→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize

    21→
    22→def cmd_plan_describe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    23→    """Set augmented description on findings."""
    24→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    25→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    26→        return
    27→
    28→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    29→    text: str = getattr(args, "text", "")
    30→
    31→    from desloppify.engine.plan import describe_finding
    32→
    33→    plan = load_plan()
    34→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    35→    if not finding_ids:
    36→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    37→        return
    38→
    39→    for fid in finding_ids:
    40→        describe_finding(plan, fid, text or None)
    41→    save_plan(plan)
    42→    print(colorize(f"  Set description on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
    43→
    44→
    45→def cmd_plan_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    46→    """Set note on findings."""
    47→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    48→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    49→        return
    50→
    51→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    52→    text: str | None = getattr(args, "text", None)
    53→
    54→    from desloppify.engine.plan import annotate_finding
    55→
    56→    plan = load_plan()
    57→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    58→    if not finding_ids:
    59→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    60→        return
    61→
    62→    for fid in finding_ids:
    63→        annotate_finding(plan, fid, text)
    64→    save_plan(plan)
    65→    print(colorize(f"  Set note on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
    66→
    67→
    68→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    69→# Skip / unskip
    70→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    71→
    72→def cmd_plan_skip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    73→    """Skip findings — unified command for temporary/permanent/false-positive."""
    74→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.selection import (
    75→        show_attestation_requirement,
    76→        validate_attestation,
    77→    )
    78→    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    79→
    80→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    81→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    82→        return
    83→
    84→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    85→    reason: str | None = getattr(args, "reason", None)
    86→    review_after: int | None = getattr(args, "review_after", None)
    87→    permanent: bool = getattr(args, "permanent", False)
    88→    false_positive: bool = getattr(args, "false_positive", False)
    89→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
    90→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    91→
    92→    # Determine skip kind
    93→    if false_positive:
    94→        kind = "false_positive"
    95→    elif permanent:
    96→        kind = "permanent"
    97→    else:
    98→        kind = "temporary"
    99→
   100→    # Validate requirements for permanent/false_positive
   101→    if kind in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   102→        if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   103→            show_attestation_requirement(
   104→                "Permanent skip" if kind == "permanent" else "False positive",
   105→                attestation,
   106→                ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
   107→            )
   108→            return
   109→        if kind == "permanent" and not note:
   110→            print(
   111→                colorize("  --permanent requires --note to explain the decision.", "yellow"),
   112→                file=sys.stderr,
   113→            )
   114→            return
   115→
   116→    plan = load_plan()
   117→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
   118→    if not finding_ids:
   119→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   120→        return
   121→
   122→    # For permanent/false_positive: delegate to state layer for score impact
   123→    if kind in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   124→        from desloppify import state as state_mod
   125→        from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   126→
   127→        state_file = state_path(args)
   128→        state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   129→        status = "wontfix" if kind == "permanent" else "false_positive"
   130→        resolved: list[str] = []
   131→        for fid in finding_ids:
   132→            resolved.extend(
   133→                state_mod.resolve_findings(
   134→                    state_data, fid, status, note or "", attestation=attestation
   135→                )
   136→            )
   137→        if resolved:
   138→            state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)
   139→
   140→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   141→    count = skip_items(
   142→        plan,
   143→        finding_ids,
   144→        kind=kind,
   145→        reason=reason,
   146→        note=note,
   147→        attestation=attestation,
   148→        review_after=review_after,
   149→        scan_count=scan_count,
   150→    )
   151→    save_plan(plan)
   152→
   153→    label = {"temporary": "Skipped", "permanent": "Wontfixed", "false_positive": "Marked false positive"}
   154→    print(colorize(f"  {label[kind]} {count} item(s).", "green"))
   155→    if review_after:
   156→        print(colorize(f"  Will re-surface after {review_after} scan(s).", "dim"))
   157→
   158→
   159→def cmd_plan_unskip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   160→    """Unskip findings — bring back to queue."""
   161→    state = command_runtime(args).state
   162→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
   163→        return
   164→
   165→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   166→
   167→    plan = load_plan()
   168→    # For unskip we need to match against all statuses (skipped items may be wontfix/fp)
   169→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan, status_filter="all")
   170→    if not finding_ids:
   171→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
   172→        return
   173→
   174→    count, need_reopen = unskip_items(plan, finding_ids)
   175→    save_plan(plan)
   176→
   177→    # Reopen permanent/false_positive items in state
   178→    if need_reopen:
   179→        from desloppify import state as state_mod
   180→        from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   181→
   182→        state_file = state_path(args)
   183→        state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   184→        reopened: list[str] = []
   185→        for fid in need_reopen:
   186→            reopened.extend(state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, fid, "open"))
   187→        if reopened:
   188→            state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)
   189→        print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s) in state.", "dim"))
   190→
   191→    print(colorize(f"  Unskipped {count} item(s) — back in queue.", "green"))
   192→
   193→
   194→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   195→# Done / reopen
   196→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   197→
   198→def cmd_plan_done(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   199→    """Mark findings as fixed from plan context."""
   200→    from desloppify import state as state_mod
   201→    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   202→    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.selection import (
   203→        show_attestation_requirement,
   204→        validate_attestation,
   205→    )
   206→    from desloppify.engine.plan import purge_ids
   207→    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
   208→
   209→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   210→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   211→
   212→    if not _validate_attestation(attestation):
   213→        _show_attestation_requirement("Done", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
   214→        return
   215→
   216→    state_file = state_path(args)
   217→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   218→
   219→    resolved: list[str] = []
   220→    for pattern in patterns:
   221→        resolved.extend(
   222→            state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "fixed", attestation=attestation)
   223→        )
   224→
   225→    if not resolved:
   226→        print(colorize("  No open findings matching: " + " ".join(patterns), "yellow"))
   227→        return
   228→
   229→    state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)
   230→
   231→    plan = load_plan()
   232→    purged = purge_ids(plan, resolved)
   233→    if purged:
   234→        save_plan(plan)
   235→
   236→    print(colorize(f"  Marked {len(resolved)} finding(s) as fixed.", "green"))
   237→    if purged:
   238→        print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: {purged} item(s) purged.", "dim"))
   239→
   240→
   241→def cmd_plan_reopen(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   242→    """Reopen resolved findings from plan context."""
   243→    from desloppify import state as state_mod
   244→    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   245→
   246→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   247→
   248→    state_file = state_path(args)
   249→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   250→
   251→    reopened: list[str] = []
   252→    for pattern in patterns:
   253→        reopened.extend(
   254→            state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "open")
   255→        )
   256→
   257→    if not reopened:
   258→        print(colorize("  No resolved findings matching: " + " ".join(patterns), "yellow"))
   259→        return
   260→
   261→    state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)
   262→
   263→    # Remove from skipped if present, and ensure all reopened IDs are in queue
   264→    plan = load_plan()
   265→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
   266→    count = 0
   267→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   268→    for fid in reopened:
   269→        if fid in skipped:
   270→            skipped.pop(fid)
   271→            count += 1
   272→        if fid not in order:
   273→            plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   274→            order.add(fid)
   275→            count += 1
   276→    if count:
   277→        save_plan(plan)
   278→
   279→    print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s).", "green"))
   280→    if count:
   281→        print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: items moved back to queue.", "dim"))
   282→
   283→
   284→def cmd_plan_focus(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   285→    """Set or clear the active cluster focus."""
   286→    clear_flag = getattr(args, "clear", False)
   287→    cluster_name: str | None = getattr(args, "cluster_name", None)
   288→
   289→    plan = load_plan()
   290→    if clear_flag:
   291→        clear_focus(plan)
   292→        save_plan(plan)
   293→        print(colorize("  Focus cleared.", "green"))
   294→        return
   295→
   296→    if not cluster_name:
   297→        active = plan.get("active_cluster")
   298→        if active:
   299→            print(f"  Focused on: {active}")
   300→        else:
   301→            print("  No active focus.")
   302→        return
   303→
   304→    try:
   305→        set_focus(plan, cluster_name)
   306→    except ValueError as ex:
   307→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   308→        return
   309→    save_plan(plan)
   310→    print(colorize(f"  Focused on: {cluster_name}", "green"))
   311→
   312→
   313→__all__ = [
   314→    "cmd_plan_describe",
   315→    "cmd_plan_done",
   316→    "cmd_plan_focus",
   317→    "cmd_plan_note",
   318→    "cmd_plan_reopen",
   319→    "cmd_plan_skip",
   320→    "cmd_plan_unskip",
   321→]
   322→

1→"""State-application helpers for resolve command flows."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.query import write_query_best_effort as _write_query_best_effort
     9→from desloppify.core.output_contract import OutputResult
    10→
    11→from .selection import ResolveQueryContext
    12→
    13→
    14→def _try_expand_cluster(pattern: str) -> list[str] | None:
    15→    """If pattern matches a cluster name, return its finding IDs."""
    16→    try:
    17→        from desloppify.engine.plan import has_living_plan, load_plan
    18→
    19→        if not has_living_plan():
    20→            return None
    21→        plan = load_plan()
    22→        cluster = plan.get("clusters", {}).get(pattern)
    23→        if cluster and cluster.get("finding_ids"):
    24→            return list(cluster["finding_ids"])
    25→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
    26→        pass
    27→    return None
    28→
    29→
    30→def _resolve_all_patterns(
    31→    state: dict,
    32→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    33→    *,
    34→    attestation: str | None,
    35→) -> list[str]:
    36→    all_resolved: list[str] = []
    37→    for pattern in args.patterns:
    38→        # Check if pattern is a cluster name — expand to member IDs
    39→        cluster_ids = _try_expand_cluster(pattern)
    40→        if cluster_ids:
    41→            for fid in cluster_ids:
    42→                resolved = state_mod.resolve_findings(
    43→                    state,
    44→                    fid,
    45→                    args.status,
    46→                    args.note,
    47→                    attestation=attestation,
    48→                )
    49→                all_resolved.extend(resolved)
    50→            continue
    51→
    52→        resolved = state_mod.resolve_findings(
    53→            state,
    54→            pattern,
    55→            args.status,
    56→            args.note,
    57→            attestation=attestation,
    58→        )
    59→        all_resolved.extend(resolved)
    60→    return all_resolved
    61→
    62→
    63→def write_query(payload: dict) -> OutputResult:
    64→    """Backward-compatible resolve query writer seam."""
    65→    return _write_query_best_effort(
    66→        payload,
    67→        context="resolve query payload update",
    68→    )
    69→
    70→
    71→def _write_resolve_query_entry(context: ResolveQueryContext) -> None:
    72→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(context.state)
    73→    write_query(
    74→        {
    75→            "command": "resolve",
    76→            "patterns": context.patterns,
    77→            "status": context.status,
    78→            "resolved": context.resolved,
    79→            "count": len(context.resolved),
    80→            "next_command": context.next_command,
    81→            "overall_score": scores.overall,
    82→            "objective_score": scores.objective,
    83→            "strict_score": scores.strict,
    84→            "verified_strict_score": scores.verified,
    85→            "prev_overall_score": context.prev_overall,

    89→            "attestation": context.attestation,
    90→            "narrative": context.narrative,
    91→        }
    92→    )

    24→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    25→# Helpers
    26→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    27→
    28→def _finding(fid: str, detector: str = "unused", tier: int = 1,
    29→             file: str = "test.py", detail: dict | None = None) -> dict:
    30→    return {
    31→        "id": fid,
    32→        "detector": detector,
    33→        "file": file,
    34→        "tier": tier,
    35→        "confidence": "high",
    36→        "summary": f"Finding {fid}",
    37→        "status": "open",
    38→        "detail": detail or {},
    39→    }
    40→
    41→
    42→def _state_with(*findings: dict) -> dict:
    43→    fmap = {}
    44→    for f in findings:
    45→        fmap[f["id"]] = f
    46→    return {"findings": fmap, "scan_count": 5}
    47→
    48→
    49→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    50→# Grouping key tests
    51→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    52→
    53→def test_grouping_key_auto_fix():
    54→    from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
    55→    f = _finding("a", "unused")
    56→    meta = DETECTORS.get("unused")
    57→    key = _grouping_key(f, meta)
    58→    assert key == "auto::unused"
    59→
    60→
    61→def test_grouping_key_review():
    62→    from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
    63→    f = _finding("a", "review", detail={"dimension": "abstraction_fitness"})
    64→    meta = DETECTORS.get("review")
    65→    key = _grouping_key(f, meta)

    68→

    72→    meta = DETECTORS.get("dict_keys")
    73→    key = _grouping_key(f, meta)
    74→    assert key == "typed::dict_keys::phantom_read"
    75→
    76→

    80→    meta = DETECTORS.get("structural")

1→"""State resolution operations (match + resolve)."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import copy
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "coerce_assessment_score",
     9→    "match_findings",
    10→    "resolve_findings",
    11→]
    12→
    13→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import is_numeric
    14→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import _matches_pattern
    15→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    16→    StateModel,
    17→    ensure_state_defaults,
    18→    utc_now,
    19→    validate_state_invariants,
    20→)
    21→from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
    22→
    23→
    24→def coerce_assessment_score(value: object) -> float | None:
    25→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float.
    26→
    27→    Returns ``None`` when the value cannot be interpreted as a numeric score
    28→    (e.g. bools, non-numeric strings, missing keys).
    29→    """
    30→    if is_numeric(value):
    31→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value))), 1)
    32→    if isinstance(value, dict):
    33→        raw = value.get("score")
    34→        if not is_numeric(raw):
    35→            return None
    36→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw))), 1)
    37→    return None
    38→
    39→
    40→def _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
    41→    state: StateModel,
    42→    *,
    43→    status: str,
    44→    resolved_findings: list[dict],
    45→    now: str,
    46→) -> None:
    47→    """Mark subjective assessments as stale when review findings are resolved.
    48→
    49→    The assessment score is preserved (not zeroed) — only a fresh review import
    50→    should change dimension scores.  The stale marker tells the UI to prompt
    51→    for a re-review.
    52→    """
    53→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    54→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    55→        return
    56→
    57→    touched_dimensions: set[str] = set()
    58→    for finding in resolved_findings:
    59→        if finding.get("detector") != "review":
    60→            continue
    61→        dimension = str(finding.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).strip()
    62→        if dimension:
    63→            touched_dimensions.add(dimension)
    64→
    65→    for dimension in sorted(touched_dimensions):
    66→        if dimension not in assessments:
    67→            continue
    68→
    69→        payload = assessments.get(dimension)
    70→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
    71→            payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    72→            payload["refresh_reason"] = f"review_finding_{status}"
    73→            payload["stale_since"] = now
    74→        else:
    75→            assessments[dimension] = {
    76→                "score": coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0,
    77→                "needs_review_refresh": True,
    78→                "refresh_reason": f"review_finding_{status}",
    79→                "stale_since": now,
    80→            }
    81→
    82→
    83→def match_findings(
    84→    state: StateModel, pattern: str, status_filter: str = "open"
    85→) -> list[dict]:
    86→    """Return findings matching *pattern* with the given status."""
    87→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    88→    return [
    89→        finding
    90→        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
    91→        if not finding.get("suppressed")
    92→        if (status_filter == "all" or finding["status"] == status_filter)
    93→        and _matches_pattern(finding_id, finding, pattern)
    94→    ]
    95→
    96→
    97→def resolve_findings(
    98→    state: StateModel,
    99→    pattern: str,
   100→    status: str,
   101→    note: str | None = None,
   102→    attestation: str | None = None,
   103→) -> list[str]:
   104→    """Set finding status for matches and return affected finding IDs."""
   105→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   106→    now = utc_now()
   107→    resolved: list[str] = []
   108→    resolved_findings: list[dict] = []
   109→    status_filter = "all" if status == "open" else "open"
   110→    for finding in match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter):
   111→        previous_status = str(finding.get("status", "open")).strip() or "open"
   112→        if status == "open" and previous_status == "open":
   113→            continue
   114→
   115→        extra_updates: dict[str, object] = {}
   116→        if status == "wontfix":
   117→            snapshot_scan_count = int(state.get("scan_count", 0) or 0)
   118→            extra_updates["wontfix_scan_count"] = snapshot_scan_count
   119→            extra_updates["wontfix_snapshot"] = {
   120→                "captured_at": now,
   121→                "scan_count": snapshot_scan_count,
   122→                "tier": finding.get("tier"),
   123→                "confidence": finding.get("confidence"),
   124→                "detail": copy.deepcopy(finding.get("detail", {})),
   125→            }
   126→        if status == "open":
   127→            finding["reopen_count"] = int(finding.get("reopen_count", 0) or 0) + 1
   128→            finding.pop("wontfix_scan_count", None)
   129→            finding.pop("wontfix_snapshot", None)
   130→            previous_note = finding.get("note")
   131→            next_note = note if note is not None else previous_note
   132→            extra_updates["resolved_at"] = None
   133→            extra_updates["note"] = next_note
   134→            extra_updates["resolution_attestation"] = {
   135→                "kind": "manual_reopen",
   136→                "text": attestation or note,
   137→                "attested_at": now,
   138→                "scan_verified": False,
   139→                "previous_status": previous_status,
   140→            }
   141→
   142→        updates: dict[str, object] = {
   143→            "status": status,
   144→            "note": note,
   145→            "resolved_at": now,
   146→            "suppressed": False,
   147→            "suppressed_at": None,
   148→            "suppression_pattern": None,
   149→            "resolution_attestation": {
   150→                "kind": "manual",
   151→                "text": attestation,
   152→                "attested_at": now,
   153→                "scan_verified": False,
   154→            },
   155→        }
   156→        updates.update(extra_updates)
   157→        finding.update(updates)
   158→        resolved.append(finding["id"])
   159→        resolved_findings.append(finding)
   160→
   161→    _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
   162→        state,
   163→        status=status,
   164→        resolved_findings=resolved_findings,
   165→        now=now,
   166→    )
   167→
   168→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   169→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   170→    return resolved
   171→
```

1→"""Pure mutation operations on the plan model."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
     6→    Cluster,
     7→    PlanModel,
     8→    SkipEntry,
     9→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    10→)
    11→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    12→
    13→
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→# Helpers
    16→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17→
    18→def _remove_id_from_lists(plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str) -> None:
    19→    """Remove a finding ID from queue_order and skipped."""
    20→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    21→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
    22→    if finding_id in order:
    23→        order.remove(finding_id)
    24→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    25→
    26→
    27→def _resolve_position(
    28→    order: list[str],
    29→    position: str,
    30→    target: str | None = None,
    31→    offset: int | None = None,
    32→    finding_ids: list[str] | None = None,
    33→) -> int:
    34→    """Resolve a position specifier to an insertion index.
    35→
    36→    *finding_ids* are the IDs being moved — used to calculate relative
    37→    positions when they already exist in the list.
    38→    """
    39→    moving = set(finding_ids or [])
    40→
    41→    if position == "top":
    42→        return 0
    43→    if position == "bottom":
    44→        return len(order)
    45→
    46→    if position == "before" and target:
    47→        for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
    48→            if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
    49→                return i
    50→        return 0
    51→
    52→    if position == "after" and target:
    53→        for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
    54→            if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
    55→                return i + 1
    56→        return len(order)
    57→
    58→    if position == "up" and offset is not None:
    59→        if not finding_ids:
    60→            return 0
    61→        # Find current position of first item
    62→        first_id = finding_ids[0]
    63→        current_idx = None
    64→        clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
    65→        for i, item_id in enumerate(clean_order):
    66→            if item_id == first_id:
    67→                current_idx = i
    68→                break
    69→        if current_idx is None:
    70→            # Item not yet in order; treat as "insert at position offset from top"
    71→            return max(0, len(clean_order) - offset)
    72→        return max(0, current_idx - offset)
    73→
    74→    if position == "down" and offset is not None:
    75→        if not finding_ids:
    76→            return len(order)
    77→        first_id = finding_ids[0]
    78→        clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
    79→        current_idx = None
    80→        for i, item_id in enumerate(clean_order):
    81→            if item_id == first_id:
    82→                current_idx = i
    83→                break
    84→        if current_idx is None:
    85→            return len(clean_order)
    86→        return min(len(clean_order), current_idx + offset)
    87→
    88→    return len(order)
    89→
    90→
    91→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    92→# Move items
    93→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    94→
    95→def move_items(
    96→    plan: PlanModel,
    97→    finding_ids: list[str],
    98→    position: str,
    99→    target: str | None = None,
   100→    offset: int | None = None,
   101→) -> int:
   102→    """Move finding IDs to a position in queue_order. Returns count moved."""
   103→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   104→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   105→
   106→    # Remove from skipped if present
   107→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
   108→    for fid in finding_ids:
   109→        skipped.pop(fid, None)
   110→
   111→    # Remove from current position in order
   112→    for fid in finding_ids:
   113→        if fid in order:
   114→            order.remove(fid)
   115→
   116→    # Resolve insertion point
   117→    idx = _resolve_position(order, position, target, offset, finding_ids)
   118→
   119→    # Insert in original order
   120→    for i, fid in enumerate(finding_ids):
   121→        order.insert(idx + i, fid)
   122→
   123→    return len(finding_ids)
   124→
   125→
   126→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   127→# Skip / unskip
   128→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   129→
   130→def skip_items(
   131→    plan: PlanModel,
   132→    finding_ids: list[str],
   133→    *,
   134→    kind: str = "temporary",
   135→    reason: str | None = None,
   136→    note: str | None = None,
   137→    attestation: str | None = None,
   138→    review_after: int | None = None,
   139→    scan_count: int = 0,
   140→) -> int:
   141→    """Move finding IDs to the skipped dict. Returns count skipped."""
   142→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   143→    now = utc_now()
   144→    count = 0
   145→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   146→    for fid in finding_ids:
   147→        _remove_id_from_lists(plan, fid)
   148→        skipped[fid] = {
   149→            "finding_id": fid,
   150→            "kind": kind,
   151→            "reason": reason,
   152→            "note": note,
   153→            "attestation": attestation,
   154→            "created_at": now,
   155→            "review_after": review_after,
   156→            "skipped_at_scan": scan_count,
   157→        }
   158→        count += 1
   159→    return count
   160→
   161→
   162→def unskip_items(
   163→    plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]
   164→) -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
   165→    """Bring finding IDs back from skipped to the end of queue_order.
   166→
   167→    Returns ``(count_unskipped, permanent_ids_needing_state_reopen)``
   168→    where the second list contains IDs that were permanent or false_positive
   169→    and need their state-layer status reopened by the caller.
   170→    """
   171→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   172→    count = 0
   173→    need_reopen: list[str] = []
   174→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   175→    for fid in finding_ids:
   176→        entry = skipped.pop(fid, None)
   177→        if entry is not None:
   178→            if entry.get("kind") in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   179→                need_reopen.append(fid)
   180→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   181→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   182→            count += 1
   183→    return count, need_reopen
   184→
   185→
   186→def resurface_stale_skips(
   187→    plan: PlanModel, current_scan_count: int
   188→) -> list[str]:
   189→    """Move temporary skips past their review_after threshold back to queue.
   190→
   191→    Returns list of resurfaced finding IDs.
   192→    """
   193→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   194→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   195→    resurfaced: list[str] = []
   196→    for fid in list(skipped):
   197→        entry = skipped[fid]

   216→def describe_finding(
   217→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, description: str | None
   218→) -> None:
   219→    """Set or clear an augmented description on a finding."""

   222→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   223→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   224→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}

   245→
   246→def create_cluster(
   247→    plan: PlanModel,
   248→    name: str,
   249→    description: str | None = None,
   250→    action: str | None = None,
   251→) -> Cluster:
   252→    """Create a named cluster. Raises ValueError if it already exists."""
   253→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   254→    if name.startswith("auto/"):
   255→        raise ValueError(
   256→            f"Cluster names starting with 'auto/' are reserved for auto-clusters: {name!r}"
   257→        )
   258→    if name in plan["clusters"]:
   259→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} already exists")
   260→    now = utc_now()
   261→    cluster: Cluster = {
   262→        "name": name,
   263→        "description": description,
   264→        "finding_ids": [],
   265→        "created_at": now,
   266→        "updated_at": now,
   267→        "auto": False,
   268→        "cluster_key": "",
   269→        "action": action,
   270→        "user_modified": False,
   271→    }
   272→    plan["clusters"][name] = cluster
   273→    return cluster
   274→
   275→
   276→def add_to_cluster(
   277→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   278→) -> int:
   279→    """Add finding IDs to a cluster. Returns count added."""
   280→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   281→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   282→    if cluster is None:
   283→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   284→
   285→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   286→    count = 0
   287→    now = utc_now()
   288→    for fid in finding_ids:
   289→        if fid not in member_ids:
   290→            member_ids.append(fid)
   291→            count += 1
   292→        # Update override to track cluster membership
   293→        overrides = plan["overrides"]
   294→        if fid not in overrides:
   295→            overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   296→        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = cluster_name
   297→        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   298→
   299→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   300→    return count
   301→
   302→
   303→def remove_from_cluster(
   304→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   305→) -> int:
   306→    """Remove finding IDs from a cluster. Returns count removed."""
   307→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   308→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   309→    if cluster is None:
   310→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   311→
   312→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   313→    now = utc_now()
   314→    count = 0
   315→    for fid in finding_ids:
   316→        if fid in member_ids:
   317→            member_ids.remove(fid)
   318→            count += 1
   319→        # Clear cluster from override
   320→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   321→        if override and override.get("cluster") == cluster_name:
   322→            override["cluster"] = None
   323→            override["updated_at"] = now
   324→
   325→    # Mark auto-clusters as user_modified when items are manually removed
   326→    if count > 0 and cluster.get("auto"):
   327→        cluster["user_modified"] = True
   328→
   329→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   330→    return count
   331→
   332→
   333→def delete_cluster(plan: PlanModel, name: str) -> list[str]:
   334→    """Delete a cluster and clear cluster refs from overrides. Returns orphaned IDs."""
   335→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   336→    cluster = plan["clusters"].pop(name, None)
   337→    if cluster is None:
   338→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} does not exist")
   339→
   340→    orphaned = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   341→    now = utc_now()
   342→    for fid in orphaned:
   343→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   344→        if override and override.get("cluster") == name:
   345→            override["cluster"] = None
   346→            override["updated_at"] = now
   347→
   348→    if plan.get("active_cluster") == name:
   349→        plan["active_cluster"] = None
   350→
   351→    return orphaned
   352→
   353→
   354→def move_cluster(
   355→    plan: PlanModel,
   356→    cluster_name: str,
   357→    position: str,
   358→    target: str | None = None,
   359→    offset: int | None = None,
   360→) -> int:
   361→    """Move all cluster members as a contiguous block. Returns count moved."""
   362→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   363→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   364→    if cluster is None:
   365→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   366→
   367→    member_ids = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   368→    if not member_ids:
   369→        return 0
   370→
   371→    return move_items(plan, member_ids, position, target, offset)
   372→
   373→
   374→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   375→# Focus
   376→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   377→
   378→def set_focus(plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str) -> None:
   379→    """Set the active cluster focus."""
   380→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   381→    if cluster_name not in plan["clusters"]:
   382→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   383→    plan["active_cluster"] = cluster_name
   384→
   385→
   386→def clear_focus(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   387→    """Clear the active cluster focus."""
   388→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   389→    plan["active_cluster"] = None
   390→
   391→
   392→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   393→# Reset
   394→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   395→
   396→def reset_plan(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   397→    """Reset plan to empty state, preserving version and created timestamp."""
   398→    created = plan.get("created", utc_now())
   399→    plan.clear()
   400→    from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan as _empty
   401→    for k, v in _empty().items():
   402→        plan[k] = v
   403→    plan["created"] = created
   404→
   405→
   406→def purge_ids(plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]) -> int:
   407→    """Remove finding IDs from the plan entirely.
   408→
   409→    Cleans queue_order, skipped, and all cluster memberships.
   410→    Does NOT touch overrides (descriptions/notes are kept for history).
   411→    Returns count of IDs that were actually present somewhere.
   412→    """
   413→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   414→    found = 0
   415→
   416→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   417→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   418→    for fid in finding_ids:
   419→        was_present = False
   420→        if fid in order:
   421→            order.remove(fid)
   422→            was_present = True
   423→        if fid in skipped:
   424→            skipped.pop(fid)
   425→            was_present = True
   426→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   427→            ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   428→            if fid in ids:
   429→                ids.remove(fid)
   430→                was_present = True
   431→        if was_present:
   432→            found += 1
   433→
   434→    return found
   435→
   436→
   437→__all__ = [
   438→    "add_to_cluster",
   439→    "annotate_finding",
   440→    "clear_focus",
   441→    "create_cluster",
   442→    "delete_cluster",
   443→    "describe_finding",
   444→    "move_cluster",
   445→    "move_items",
   446→    "purge_ids",

   449→    "resurface_stale_skips",
   450→    "set_focus",
   451→    "skip_items",
   452→    "unskip_items",
   453→]

   100→def _render_cluster_item(item: dict) -> None:

   142→    print(colorize(f"  Drill in:      desloppify next --cluster {cluster_name} --count 10",
   143→                   "dim"))

# Build cluster meta-items
    result: list[dict] = list(non_cluster_items)
    for cname, members in cluster_members.items():
        # Don't collapse singletons — show them as individual findings
        if len(members) < 2:
            result.extend(members)
            continue
        cluster_data = clusters.get(cname, {})
        detector = members[0].get("detector", "") if members else ""
        action = cluster_data.get("action") or ""
        # Derive action_type from the actual cluster action, not just the detector
        if "desloppify fix" in action:
            action_type = "auto_fix"
        elif "desloppify move" in action:
            action_type = "reorganize"
        else:
            action_type = _action_type_for_detector(detector)
            # If detector says auto_fix but cluster has no fix command, it's really refactor
            if action_type == "auto_fix" and "desloppify fix" not in action:
                action_type = "refactor"

        # Use stored description but patch the count if it differs from visible members
        stored_desc = cluster_data.get("description") or ""
        total_in_cluster = len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
        if stored_desc and total_in_cluster != len(members):
            # Replace the stored count with the visible count
            summary = stored_desc.replace(str(total_in_cluster), str(len(members)))
        else:
            summary = stored_desc or f"{len(members)} findings"

        primary_command = cluster_data.get("action")
        if not primary_command:
            primary_command = f"desloppify next --cluster {cname} --count 10"

        meta_item: dict = {
            "id": cname,
            "kind": "cluster",
            "action_type": action_type,
            "summary": summary,
            "members": members,
            "member_count": len(members),
            "primary_command": primary_command,
            "cluster_name": cname,
            "cluster_auto": True,
            "confidence": "high",
            "detector": detector,
            "file": "",
        }
        result.append(meta_item)

    result.sort(key=item_sort_key)

     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import copy
     7→import sys
     8→from dataclasses import dataclass
     9→
    10→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    11→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    12→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    13→
    14→_REQUIRED_ATTESTATION_PHRASES = ("i have actually", "not gaming")
    15→_ATTESTATION_KEYWORD_HINT = ("I have actually", "not gaming")
    16→
    17→
    18→def _emit_warning(message: str) -> None:
    19→    """Write resolve preflight warnings to stderr consistently."""
    20→    print(colorize(message, "yellow"), file=sys.stderr)
    21→
    22→
    23→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    24→class ResolveQueryContext:
    25→    patterns: list[str]
    26→    status: str
    27→    resolved: list[str]
    28→    next_command: str
    29→    prev_overall: float | None
    30→    prev_objective: float | None
    31→    prev_strict: float | None
    32→    prev_verified: float | None
    33→    attestation: str | None
    34→    narrative: dict
    35→    state: dict
    36→
    37→
    38→def _missing_attestation_keywords(attestation: str | None) -> list[str]:
    39→    normalized = " ".join((attestation or "").strip().lower().split())
    40→    return [
    41→        phrase for phrase in _REQUIRED_ATTESTATION_PHRASES if phrase not in normalized
    42→    ]
    43→
    44→
    45→def validate_attestation(attestation: str | None) -> bool:
    46→    return not _missing_attestation_keywords(attestation)
    47→
    48→
    49→def show_attestation_requirement(
    50→    label: str,
    51→    attestation: str | None,
    52→    example: str,
    53→    *,
    54→    patterns: list[str] | None = None,
    55→    status: str | None = None,
    56→) -> None:
    57→    missing = _missing_attestation_keywords(attestation)
    58→    if not attestation:
    59→        _emit_warning(f"{label} requires --attest.")
    60→    elif missing:
    61→        missing_str = ", ".join(f"'{keyword}'" for keyword in missing)
    62→        _emit_warning(
    63→            f"{label} attestation is missing required keyword(s): {missing_str}."
    64→        )
    65→    _emit_warning(
    66→        f"Required keywords: '{_ATTESTATION_KEYWORD_HINT[0]}' and '{_ATTESTATION_KEYWORD_HINT[1]}'."
    67→    )
    68→    print(colorize(f'Example: --attest "{example}"', "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    69→    if patterns and status:
    70→        pattern_args = " ".join(f'"{p}"' for p in patterns)
    71→        print(
    72→            colorize(
    73→                f'\nCopy-paste:\n  desloppify resolve {status} {pattern_args} --attest "{example}"',
    74→                "dim",
    75→            ),
    76→            file=sys.stderr,
    77→        )
    78→
    79→
    80→def _validate_resolve_inputs(args: argparse.Namespace, attestation: str | None) -> None:
    81→    if args.status == "wontfix" and not args.note:
    82→        _emit_warning(
    83→            "Wontfix items become technical debt. Add --note to record your reasoning for future review."
    84→        )
    85→        sys.exit(1)
    86→    if args.status == "open":
    87→        return
    88→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
    89→        show_attestation_requirement(
    90→            "Manual resolve",
    91→            attestation,
    92→            ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
    93→            patterns=getattr(args, "patterns", None),
    94→            status=args.status,
    95→        )
    96→        sys.exit(1)
    97→
    98→
    99→def _previous_score_snapshot(state: dict) -> state_mod.ScoreSnapshot:
   100→    """Load a score snapshot for comparison after resolve operations."""
```

def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
    for item in items:
        if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
            # Count each member at its own tier
            for member in item.get("members", []):
                tier = int(member.get("effective_tier", member.get("tier", 3)))
                counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
            continue
        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
        counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
    return counts
```

def _item_matches_tier(item: dict, tier: int) -> bool:
    """Check if an item (or any of its members for clusters) matches a tier."""
    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
        return any(
            int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) == tier
            for m in item.get("members", [])
        )
    return int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))) == tier
```

def item_sort_key(item: dict) -> tuple:
    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
        # Clusters sort before individual findings, ordered by action type
        # (auto_fix first, then reorganize, refactor, manual_fix),
        # then bigger clusters first within the same action type.
        action_pri = _CLUSTER_ACTION_PRIORITY.get(
            item.get("action_type", "manual_fix"), 3
        )
        return (
            0,  # All clusters before all individual findings
            action_pri,
            -int(item.get("member_count", 0)),
            item.get("id", ""),
        )

    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
        return (
            int(item.get("effective_tier", 4)),
            1,  # Subjective items sort after mechanical items within T4.
            subjective_score_value(item),
            item.get("id", ""),
        )

    detail = detail_dict(item)
    review_weight = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
    return (
        int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))),
        0,
        CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(item.get("confidence", "low"), 9),
        -review_weight,
        -int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
        item.get("id", ""),
    )
```

80→def _validate_resolve_inputs(args: argparse.Namespace, attestation: str | None) -> None:
    81→    if args.status == "wontfix" and not args.note:
    82→        _emit_warning(
    83→            "Wontfix items become technical debt. Add --note to record your reasoning for future review."
    84→        )
    85→        sys.exit(1)
    86→    if args.status == "open":
    87→        return
    88→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
    89→        show_attestation_requirement(

    91→            attestation,
    92→            ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
    93→            patterns=getattr(args, "patterns", None),
    94→            status=args.status,
    95→        )
    96→        sys.exit(1)
    97→
    98→
    99→def _previous_score_snapshot(state: dict) -> state_mod.ScoreSnapshot:
   100→    """Load a score snapshot for comparison after resolve operations."""
   101→    return state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
   102→
   103→
   104→def _preview_resolve_count(state: dict, patterns: list[str]) -> int:
   105→    """Count unique open findings matching the provided patterns."""
   106→    matched_ids: set[str] = set()
   107→    for pattern in patterns:
   108→        for finding in state_mod.match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter="open"):
   109→            finding_id = finding.get("id")
   110→            if finding_id:
   111→                matched_ids.add(finding_id)
   112→    return len(matched_ids)
   113→
   114→
   115→def _estimate_wontfix_strict_delta(
   116→    state: dict,
   117→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   118→    *,
   119→    attestation: str | None,
   120→    resolve_all_patterns_fn,
   121→) -> float:
   122→    """Estimate strict score drop if this resolve command is applied as wontfix."""
   123→    before = state_mod.get_strict_score(state)
   124→    if before is None:
   125→        return 0.0
   126→
   127→    preview_state = copy.deepcopy(state)
   128→    resolve_all_patterns_fn(preview_state, args, attestation=attestation)
   129→    after = state_mod.get_strict_score(preview_state)
   130→    if after is None:
   131→        return 0.0
   132→    return max(0.0, before - after)
   133→
   134→
   135→def _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation(
   136→    state: dict,
   137→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   138→    *,
   139→    attestation: str | None,
   140→    resolve_all_patterns_fn,
   141→) -> None:
   142→    if args.status != "wontfix":
   143→        return
   144→
   145→    preview_count = _preview_resolve_count(state, args.patterns)
   146→    if preview_count <= 10:
   147→        return
   148→    if getattr(args, "confirm_batch_wontfix", False):
   149→        return
   150→
   151→    strict_delta = _estimate_wontfix_strict_delta(
   152→        state,
   153→        args,
   154→        attestation=attestation,
   155→        resolve_all_patterns_fn=resolve_all_patterns_fn,
   156→    )
   157→    _emit_warning(f"Large wontfix batch detected ({preview_count} findings).")
   158→    if strict_delta > 0:
   159→        _emit_warning(f"Estimated strict-score debt added now: {strict_delta:.1f} points.")
   160→    _emit_warning(
   161→        "Re-run with --confirm-batch-wontfix if this debt is intentional."
   162→    )
   163→    sys.exit(1)
   164→
```

    26→        kwargs.setdefault("nargs", 0)

    28→        kwargs.setdefault("default", False)
    29→        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
    30→
    31→    def __call__(self, parser, namespace, values, option_string=None):
    32→        print(
    33→            f"Warning: {option_string} is deprecated and will be removed in a future version.",
    34→            file=sys.stderr,
    35→        )
    36→        setattr(namespace, self.dest, True)

    39→def _add_detect_parser(sub, detector_names: list[str]) -> None:
    40→    p_detect = sub.add_parser(
    41→        "detect",
    42→        help="Run a single detector directly (bypass state)",
    43→        epilog=f"detectors: {', '.join(detector_names)}",
    44→    )
    45→    p_detect.add_argument("detector", type=str, help="Detector to run")

    47→    p_detect.add_argument("--path", type=str, default=None, help="Project root directory (default: auto-detected)")
    48→    p_detect.add_argument("--json", action="store_true", help="Output as JSON")
    49→    p_detect.add_argument(
    50→        "--fix",
    51→        action="store_true",
    52→        help="Auto-fix detected issues (logs detector only)",
    53→    )
    54→    p_detect.add_argument(
    55→        "--category",
    56→        choices=["imports", "vars", "params", "all"],
    57→        default="all",
    58→        help="Filter unused by category",

    61→        "--threshold",

    63→        default=None,
    64→        help="LOC threshold (large) or similarity (dupes)",

    66→    p_detect.add_argument(
    67→        "--file", type=str, default=None, help="Show deps for specific file"

    73→        metavar="KEY=VALUE",
    74→        help="Language runtime option override (repeatable)",

## Comprehensive Analysis: Work Queue Item Data Shape & Cluster Meta-Items

### 1. **Cluster Meta-Item Creation** (`_collapse_clusters` in `core.py` lines 172-251)

When a cluster meta-item is created, it has the following fields:

```python
meta_item: dict = {
    "id": cname,                          # cluster name (e.g., "auto/unused")
    "kind": "cluster",                    # marks it as a cluster
    "action_type": action_type,           # "auto_fix", "reorganize", "refactor", or "manual_fix"
    "summary": summary,                   # description of the cluster
    "members": members,                   # full list of member finding dicts
    "member_count": len(members),         # count of members
    "primary_command": primary_command,   # shell command to interact with cluster
    "cluster_name": cname,                # cluster name (redundant with id)
    "cluster_auto": True,                 # marks it as auto-created
    "confidence": "high",                 # hardcoded confidence
    "detector": detector,                 # detector type from first member
    "file": "",                           # empty string for clusters
}
```

### 2. **Tier and effective_tier Fields**

**CRITICAL: Cluster meta-items DO NOT have tier or effective_tier fields.**

- **Individual findings** get `effective_tier` assigned in `build_finding_items` (ranking.py:55-57):
  - `effective_tier = 4` if subjective, else `effective_tier = int(finding.get("tier", 3))`
- **Subjective dimensions** get hardcoded `effective_tier = 4` (helpers.py:277)
- **Cluster meta-items** never get either field assigned

Instead, clusters use **member-aware tier calculations**:

### 3. **Tier Counting via `tier_counts()`** (ranking.py:173-184)

```python
def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
    for item in items:
        if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
            # Count EACH MEMBER at its own tier
            for member in item.get("members", []):
                tier = int(member.get("effective_tier", member.get("tier", 3)))
                counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
            continue
        # Non-cluster items use their own tier
        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
        counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
    return counts
```

**Key insight:** When a cluster meta-item appears in the list, `tier_counts()` doesn't count the cluster itself as 1 item—it **unpacks the cluster and counts each member independently at its own tier**. This means:
- A cluster with 5 members (3 T1, 2 T2) contributes: `{1: 3, 2: 2, 3: 0, 4: 0}`
- The cluster itself is not counted as a top-level item in tier statistics

### 4. **Tier Filtering via `_item_matches_tier()`** (core.py:147-154)

When filtering by a requested tier, clusters are included if **any of their members** matches that tier:

```python
def _item_matches_tier(item: dict, tier: int) -> bool:
    """Check if an item (or any of its members for clusters) matches a tier."""
    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
        return any(
            int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) == tier
            for m in item.get("members", [])
        )
    return int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))) == tier
```

**Behavior:**
- Request `--tier 1` → includes clusters that have at least 1 T1 member
- Request `--tier 3` → includes clusters that have at least 1 T3 member
- A cluster with mixed tiers (T1 + T2 + T3) appears in results for all three tier requests

### 5. **queue_position Assignment** (core.py:120-134)

`queue_position` is assigned in `_apply_plan_order()` to **all items**, including cluster meta-items:

```python
for pos, item in enumerate(result):
    item["queue_position"] = pos + 1  # 1-indexed position in final list
    if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
        item["plan_skipped"] = True
        # ... additional skip metadata ...
```

**Key points:**
- Every item in the result list (cluster or finding) gets a `queue_position` field
- It's 1-indexed: first item gets position 1, second gets position 2, etc.
- Clusters can be at any position depending on plan order and sorting
- Skipped clusters also get `plan_skipped = True` and optional `plan_skip_reason`

### 6. **Sorting Behavior** (ranking.py:86-118)

Cluster meta-items sort **before** all individual findings:

```python
def item_sort_key(item: dict) -> tuple:
    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
        return (
            0,  # All clusters before all individual findings (key 0)
            action_pri,  # "auto_fix"(0) < "reorganize"(1) < "refactor"(2) < "manual_fix"(3)
            -int(item.get("member_count", 0)),  # Larger clusters first
            item.get("id", ""),  # Alphabetical tiebreaker
        )
    
    # Individual findings start with key 1+, so they sort after clusters
    return (
        int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))),  # Higher key = sorts later
        0,
        # ... confidence, review_weight, count, id ...
    )
```

**Result:** Clusters always appear at the top of results, ordered by: action type → member count (desc) → ID. Individual findings appear below, ordered by tier.

### 7. **Plan Metadata Annotation** (core.py:84-134)

When a cluster meta-item's ID appears in the plan's overrides, it gets annotated with:
- `plan_description` (if override has description)
- `plan_note` (if override has note)
- `plan_cluster` (if the item itself was marked as belonging to a cluster—note: clusters don't belong to clusters)

When a cluster is in skipped items:
- `plan_skipped = True`
- `plan_skip_kind` ("temporary" or "permanent")
- `plan_skip_reason` (optional explanation)

### 8. **Clustering Collapse Conditions** (core.py:305-313)

Clusters are **collapsed into meta-items** only when:
1. `collapse_clusters=True` (option)
2. `plan` is provided
3. **Not** drilling into a specific cluster (`cluster=None`)
4. **Not** in active_cluster mode (`plan.get("active_cluster")` is None/falsy)

When drilling into a cluster (e.g., `--cluster auto/unused`), the members are returned as individual findings, not as a collapsed meta-item.

### 9. **Summary Table: Cluster Meta-Item Fields**

| Field | Type | Set By | Value |
|-------|------|--------|-------|
| `id` | str | `_collapse_clusters` | cluster name (e.g., "auto/unused") |
| `kind` | str | `_collapse_clusters` | "cluster" |
| `action_type` | str | `_collapse_clusters` | "auto_fix" \| "reorganize" \| "refactor" \| "manual_fix" |
| `summary` | str | `_collapse_clusters` | description with member count |
| `members` | list[dict] | `_collapse_clusters` | full finding dicts (each has own `effective_tier`) |
| `member_count` | int | `_collapse_clusters` | len(members) |
| `primary_command` | str | `_collapse_clusters` | shell command for interaction |
| `cluster_name` | str | `_collapse_clusters` | same as `id` |
| `cluster_auto` | bool | `_collapse_clusters` | True |
| `confidence` | str | `_collapse_clusters` | "high" |
| `detector` | str | `_collapse_clusters` | from first member |
| `file` | str | `_collapse_clusters` | "" (empty string) |
| `tier` | N/A | Never set | (undefined) |
| `effective_tier` | N/A | Never set | (undefined) |
| `queue_position` | int | `_apply_plan_order` | 1-indexed position in final result |
| `plan_description` | str | `_apply_plan_order` | (if override exists) |
| `plan_note` | str | `_apply_plan_order` | (if override exists) |
| `plan_skipped` | bool | `_apply_plan_order` | (if in skipped set) |
| `plan_skip_kind` | str | `_apply_plan_order` | "temporary" \| "permanent" |
| `plan_skip_reason` | str | `_apply_plan_order` | (if provided) |

### 10. **Key Architectural Insights**

1. **Cluster meta-items have no tier field by design.** Their tier is determined by examining their members' tiers at counting/filtering time.

2. **tier_counts() unpacks clusters.** This means the `tier_counts` dict in `WorkQueueResult` reflects member tiers, not the count of top-level items.

3. **Clusters participate in tier filtering via member inspection.** When you request `--tier 1`, a cluster with mixed members appears if it has any T1 items.

4. **queue_position is universal.** Both clusters and findings get positions in the final ordered result from `_apply_plan_order()`.

5. **Members are preserved.** The `members` field on a cluster meta-item contains the full finding dicts, allowing rendering code to access member `effective_tier`, `confidence`, `detector`, etc.

6. **Plan overrides work on cluster IDs.** The plan can override cluster meta-items (by their cluster ID) just like any other work queue item.

7. **Singletons don't collapse.** If auto-clustering would result in a cluster with <2 members, those findings remain as individual items (lines 204-206 in core.py).

## Analysis Report: `resolve fixed` vs `plan done` Code Path Comparison

I've thoroughly traced both code paths. Here are my findings:

---

### CRITICAL BUG FOUND

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py`  
**Lines:** 212-213  
**Issue:** The function uses undefined names `_validate_attestation` and `_show_attestation_requirement`, but the imports at lines 202-205 define them **without** underscores.

```python
# Lines 202-205: IMPORT (correct)
from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.selection import (
    show_attestation_requirement,    # no underscore
    validate_attestation,             # no underscore
)

# Lines 212-213: USAGE (WRONG - has underscores)
if not _validate_attestation(attestation):           # NameError!
    _show_attestation_requirement("Done", ...)       # NameError!
```

These should be `validate_attestation` and `show_attestation_requirement` (without leading underscores).

---

### Architecture Comparison: `cmd_resolve` vs `cmd_plan_done`

#### **1. `cmd_resolve` (lines 58-155 in `/resolve/cmd.py`)**

**State Mutations:**
1. Calls `_resolve_all_patterns(state, args)` which invokes `state_mod.resolve_findings()` for each pattern
2. Saves state to disk via `_save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)` 
3. **Tries to update the living plan (best-effort try/except):**
   - Loads plan via `load_plan()` if `has_living_plan()` is True
   - Calls `purge_ids(plan, all_resolved)` to remove resolved items
   - Saves plan via `save_plan(plan)` if items were purged

**Resolution Statuses Accepted:** Only handles "open", "fixed", "wontfix", "false_positive" (defined in parser at line 265 of `parser_groups.py`)

**Validation Flow:**
- Calls `_validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)` which:
  - Checks `args.status == "wontfix"` requires `--note`
  - For non-"open" statuses: validates attestation via `validate_attestation()`, calls `show_attestation_requirement()` on failure, and exits with code 1
  - Exits preemptively, so execution never continues without valid inputs

**Additional Validation:**
- Calls `_enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation()` to warn about large wontfix batches (>10 items)

**Plan Interaction:** Optional, wrapped in try/except, doesn't block command flow

---

#### **2. `cmd_plan_done` (lines 198-238 in `/plan/override_handlers.py`)**

**State Mutations:**
1. Loads state via `state_mod.load_state(state_file)`
2. Calls `state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "fixed", attestation=attestation)` for each pattern
   - **Hard-coded status: "fixed"** (not a command-line parameter)
3. Saves state via `state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)` if any resolved
4. Loads plan and calls `purge_ids(plan, resolved)` to remove resolved items
5. Saves plan via `save_plan(plan)` if items were purged

**Resolution Status:** Only "fixed" (hard-coded in line 222)

**Validation Flow (BUGGY):**
- Attempts to validate attestation via `_validate_attestation(attestation)` **[UNDEFINED - should be `validate_attestation`]**
- If invalid, tries to call `_show_attestation_requirement(...)` **[UNDEFINED - should be `show_attestation_requirement`]**
- Returns early (doesn't exit, so scope persists) if attestation invalid
- **No try/except wrapping** — will crash with NameError on invalid attestation

**No Additional Validation:** Doesn't enforce `--note` requirement or batch warnings

---

### Key Differences: Are They Redundant or Distinct?

| Aspect | `resolve fixed` | `plan done` |
|--------|-----------------|-----------|
| **Status** | "fixed" (or other statuses via CLI arg) | "fixed" only (hard-coded) |
| **State Layer** | Yes — calls `resolve_findings()` | Yes — calls `resolve_findings()` |
| **Plan Layer** | Yes, best-effort try/except | Yes, mandatory |
| **Validation** | Strong: input validation + batch checks | Weak: attestation check only (broken) |
| **Workflow** | General-purpose resolution tool | Plan-specific resolution path |

**Conclusion:** These are **genuinely different operations**, not redundant:

1. **`resolve fixed`** is the **general CLI-facing resolution command** that accepts any status and includes comprehensive validation
2. **`plan done`** is a **plan subcommand** meant for marking items "done from the plan perspective" with hard-coded "fixed" status

However, **they both perform the same core mutations**:
- Both call `state_mod.resolve_findings()` with matching status/pattern
- Both save state
- Both purge from the living plan

The intent is: `plan done` is a **convenience shortcut** for plan-aware workflows, while `resolve fixed` is the **full-featured command** that handles all statuses and includes safeguards.

---

### What Each Operation Mutates

**`cmd_resolve` when status="fixed":**
1. ✅ `state["findings"][id].status` → "fixed"
2. ✅ `state["findings"][id].resolved_at` → now (timestamp)
3. ✅ `state["findings"][id].resolution_attestation` → attestation dict
4. ✅ `state` recalculation (scores via `_recompute_stats`)
5. ✅ Plan queue_order (removes from queue)
6. ✅ Plan skipped dict (removes from skipped if present)
7. ✅ Plan cluster memberships (removes from all clusters)
8. ✅ Plan overrides preserved (descriptions/notes/cluster refs kept for history)

**`cmd_plan_done`:**
1. ✅ `state["findings"][id].status` → "fixed" (same as resolve)
2. ✅ `state["findings"][id].resolved_at` → now (same as resolve)
3. ✅ `state["findings"][id].resolution_attestation` → attestation dict (same as resolve)
4. ✅ `state` recalculation (scores via `_recompute_stats`, same as resolve)
5. ✅ Plan queue_order (removes from queue)
6. ✅ Plan skipped dict (removes from skipped if present)
7. ✅ Plan cluster memberships (removes from all clusters)
8. ✅ Plan overrides preserved (same as resolve)

**Identical mutations** — the difference is purely in the **control flow** and **validation** before mutation.

---

- **`resolve` implementation:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` (lines 58-155)
- **`plan done` implementation:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py` (lines 198-238)
- **State layer:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/resolution.py` (lines 97-170)
- **Plan operations:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/operations.py` (lines 406-434)

                        no items
  --cluster NAME        Filter to a specific plan cluster
  --include-skipped     Include skipped items in the queue
  --output FILE         Write JSON/Markdown to file (with --format json|md)

tiers (highest to lowest priority):
  T1  critical    security issues, data loss risks
  T2  important   bugs, correctness, major smells
  T3  moderate    code quality, duplication, dead code
  T4  cosmetic    style, subjective review dimensions

examples:
  desloppify next                       # single highest-priority item
  desloppify next --count 10            # top 10 items
  desloppify next --tier 1              # only tier 1
  desloppify next --group file          # group by file
  desloppify next --cluster my-cluster  # items in a cluster
```

usage: desloppify resolve [-h] [--note NOTE] [--attest ATTEST]
                          [--confirm-batch-wontfix] [--state STATE]
                          {open,fixed,wontfix,false_positive} PATTERN
                          [PATTERN ...]

positional arguments:
  {open,fixed,wontfix,false_positive}
  PATTERN               Finding ID(s), prefix, detector name, file path, or
                        glob

options:
  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  --note NOTE           Explanation (required for wontfix)
  --attest ATTEST       Required anti-gaming attestation. Must include BOTH
                        keywords 'I have actually' and 'not gaming'. Example:
                        --attest "I have actually [DESCRIBE THE CONCRETE
                        CHANGE YOU MADE] and I am not gaming the score by
                        resolving without fixing."
  --confirm-batch-wontfix
                        Confirm large wontfix batch (>10 findings)
  --state STATE         Path to state file

examples:
  desloppify resolve fixed "unused::src/foo.tsx::React" \
    --note "removed import" --attest "I have actually ..."
  desloppify resolve wontfix deprecated \
    --note "tracked in migration" --attest "I have actually ..."
  desloppify resolve fixed "logs::src/foo.tsx::*" --note "removed debug logs" \
    --attest "I have actually ..."
```

> TOOL

tool_result Bash
```
usage: desloppify plan [-h] [--state STATE] [--output FILE]
                       {show,queue,reset,move,describe,note,skip,unskip,done,reopen,focus,cluster}
                       ...

Manage the living plan — a persistent layer on top of the work queue.
Track custom ordering, clusters, skips, and per-finding annotations.
Run with no subcommand to generate a full prioritized markdown plan.

positional arguments:
  {show,queue,reset,move,describe,note,skip,unskip,done,reopen,focus,cluster}
    show                Show plan metadata summary
    queue               Compact table of upcoming queue items
    reset               Reset plan to empty
    move                Move findings in the queue
    describe            Set augmented description
    note                Set note on findings
    skip                Skip findings: temporary (default), --permanent
                        (wontfix), or --false-positive
    unskip              Bring skipped findings back to queue (reopens
                        permanent/fp in state)
    done                Mark findings as fixed (requires attestation, purges
                        from plan)
    reopen              Reopen resolved findings and move back to queue
    focus               Set or clear active cluster focus
    cluster             Manage finding clusters

options:
  -h, --help            show this help message and exit
  --state STATE         Path to state file
  --output FILE         Write to file instead of stdout

typical workflow:
  desloppify scan                       # detect findings
  desloppify plan                       # full prioritized markdown
  desloppify plan queue                 # compact table of all items
  desloppify plan cluster create ...    # group related findings
  desloppify plan focus <cluster>       # narrow scope
  desloppify next                       # work on the next item
  desloppify plan done <id> --attest .. # mark as fixed

subcommands:
  show       Show plan metadata summary
  queue      Compact table of upcoming queue items
  reset      Reset plan to empty
  move       Move findings in the queue
  describe   Set augmented description
  note       Set note on findings
  skip       Skip findings (temporary/permanent/false_positive)
  unskip     Bring skipped findings back to queue
  done       Mark findings as fixed (requires attestation)
  reopen     Reopen resolved findings
  focus      Set or clear active cluster focus
  cluster    Manage finding clusters
```
