1→"""Pure mutation operations on the plan model."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
     6→    Cluster,
     7→    PlanModel,
     8→    SkipEntry,
     9→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    10→)
    11→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    12→
    13→
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→# Helpers
    16→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17→
    18→def _remove_id_from_lists(plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str) -> None:
    19→    """Remove a finding ID from queue_order and skipped."""
    20→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    21→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
    22→    if finding_id in order:
    23→        order.remove(finding_id)
    24→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    25→
    26→
    27→def _resolve_position(
    28→    order: list[str],
    29→    position: str,
    30→    target: str | None = None,
    31→    offset: int | None = None,
    32→    finding_ids: list[str] | None = None,
    33→) -> int:
    34→    """Resolve a position specifier to an insertion index.
    35→
    36→    *finding_ids* are the IDs being moved — used to calculate relative
    37→    positions when they already exist in the list.
    38→    """
    39→    moving = set(finding_ids or [])
    40→
    41→    if position == "top":
    42→        return 0
    43→    if position == "bottom":
    44→        return len(order)
    45→
    46→    if position == "before" and target:
    47→        for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
    48→            if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
    49→                return i
    50→        return 0
    51→
    52→    if position == "after" and target:
    53→        for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
    54→            if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
    55→                return i + 1
    56→        return len(order)
    57→
    58→    if position == "up" and offset is not None:
    59→        if not finding_ids:
    60→            return 0
    61→        # Find current position of first item
    62→        first_id = finding_ids[0]
    63→        current_idx = None
    64→        clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
    65→        for i, item_id in enumerate(clean_order):
    66→            if item_id == first_id:
    67→                current_idx = i
    68→                break
    69→        if current_idx is None:
    70→            # Item not yet in order; treat as "insert at position offset from top"
    71→            return max(0, len(clean_order) - offset)
    72→        return max(0, current_idx - offset)
    73→
    74→    if position == "down" and offset is not None:
    75→        if not finding_ids:
    76→            return len(order)
    77→        first_id = finding_ids[0]
    78→        clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
    79→        current_idx = None
    80→        for i, item_id in enumerate(clean_order):
    81→            if item_id == first_id:
    82→                current_idx = i
    83→                break
    84→        if current_idx is None:
    85→            return len(clean_order)
    86→        return min(len(clean_order), current_idx + offset)
    87→
    88→    return len(order)
    89→
    90→
    91→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    92→# Move items
    93→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    94→
    95→def move_items(
    96→    plan: PlanModel,
    97→    finding_ids: list[str],
    98→    position: str,
    99→    target: str | None = None,
   100→    offset: int | None = None,
   101→) -> int:
   102→    """Move finding IDs to a position in queue_order. Returns count moved."""
   103→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   104→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   105→
   106→    # Remove from skipped if present
   107→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
   108→    for fid in finding_ids:
   109→        skipped.pop(fid, None)
   110→
   111→    # Remove from current position in order
   112→    for fid in finding_ids:
   113→        if fid in order:
   114→            order.remove(fid)
   115→
   116→    # Resolve insertion point
   117→    idx = _resolve_position(order, position, target, offset, finding_ids)
   118→
   119→    # Insert in original order
   120→    for i, fid in enumerate(finding_ids):
   121→        order.insert(idx + i, fid)
   122→
   123→    return len(finding_ids)
   124→
   125→
   126→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   127→# Skip / unskip
   128→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   129→
   130→def skip_items(
   131→    plan: PlanModel,
   132→    finding_ids: list[str],
   133→    *,
   134→    kind: str = "temporary",
   135→    reason: str | None = None,
   136→    note: str | None = None,
   137→    attestation: str | None = None,
   138→    review_after: int | None = None,
   139→    scan_count: int = 0,
   140→) -> int:
   141→    """Move finding IDs to the skipped dict. Returns count skipped."""
   142→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   143→    now = utc_now()
   144→    count = 0
   145→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   146→    for fid in finding_ids:
   147→        _remove_id_from_lists(plan, fid)
   148→        skipped[fid] = {
   149→            "finding_id": fid,
   150→            "kind": kind,
   151→            "reason": reason,
   152→            "note": note,
   153→            "attestation": attestation,
   154→            "created_at": now,
   155→            "review_after": review_after,
   156→            "skipped_at_scan": scan_count,
   157→        }
   158→        count += 1
   159→    return count
   160→
   161→
   162→def unskip_items(
   163→    plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]
   164→) -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
   165→    """Bring finding IDs back from skipped to the end of queue_order.
   166→
   167→    Returns ``(count_unskipped, permanent_ids_needing_state_reopen)``
   168→    where the second list contains IDs that were permanent or false_positive
   169→    and need their state-layer status reopened by the caller.
   170→    """
   171→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   172→    count = 0
   173→    need_reopen: list[str] = []
   174→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   175→    for fid in finding_ids:
   176→        entry = skipped.pop(fid, None)
   177→        if entry is not None:
   178→            if entry.get("kind") in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   179→                need_reopen.append(fid)
   180→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   181→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   182→            count += 1
   183→    return count, need_reopen
   184→
   185→
   186→def resurface_stale_skips(
   187→    plan: PlanModel, current_scan_count: int
   188→) -> list[str]:
   189→    """Move temporary skips past their review_after threshold back to queue.
   190→
   191→    Returns list of resurfaced finding IDs.
   192→    """
   193→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   194→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   195→    resurfaced: list[str] = []
   196→    for fid in list(skipped):
   197→        entry = skipped[fid]
   198→        if entry.get("kind") != "temporary":
   199→            continue
   200→        review_after = entry.get("review_after")
   201→        if review_after is None:
   202→            continue
   203→        skipped_at = entry.get("skipped_at_scan", 0)
   204→        if current_scan_count >= skipped_at + review_after:
   205→            skipped.pop(fid)
   206→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   207→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   208→            resurfaced.append(fid)
   209→    return resurfaced
   210→
   211→
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→# Describe / annotate
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→
   216→def describe_finding(
   217→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, description: str | None
   218→) -> None:
   219→    """Set or clear an augmented description on a finding."""
   220→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   221→    now = utc_now()
   222→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   223→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   224→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}
   225→    overrides[finding_id]["description"] = description
   226→    overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
   227→
   228→
   229→def annotate_finding(
   230→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, note: str | None
   231→) -> None:
   232→    """Set or clear a note on a finding."""
   233→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   234→    now = utc_now()
   235→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   236→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   237→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}
   238→    overrides[finding_id]["note"] = note
   239→    overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
   240→
   241→
   242→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   243→# Clusters
   244→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   245→
   246→def create_cluster(
   247→    plan: PlanModel,
   248→    name: str,
   249→    description: str | None = None,
   250→    action: str | None = None,
   251→) -> Cluster:
   252→    """Create a named cluster. Raises ValueError if it already exists."""
   253→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   254→    if name.startswith("auto/"):
   255→        raise ValueError(
   256→            f"Cluster names starting with 'auto/' are reserved for auto-clusters: {name!r}"
   257→        )
   258→    if name in plan["clusters"]:
   259→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} already exists")
   260→    now = utc_now()
   261→    cluster: Cluster = {
   262→        "name": name,
   263→        "description": description,
   264→        "finding_ids": [],
   265→        "created_at": now,
   266→        "updated_at": now,
   267→        "auto": False,
   268→        "cluster_key": "",
   269→        "action": action,
   270→        "user_modified": False,
   271→    }
   272→    plan["clusters"][name] = cluster
   273→    return cluster
   274→
   275→
   276→def add_to_cluster(
   277→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   278→) -> int:
   279→    """Add finding IDs to a cluster. Returns count added."""
   280→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   281→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   282→    if cluster is None:
   283→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   284→
   285→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   286→    count = 0
   287→    now = utc_now()
   288→    for fid in finding_ids:
   289→        if fid not in member_ids:
   290→            member_ids.append(fid)
   291→            count += 1
   292→        # Update override to track cluster membership
   293→        overrides = plan["overrides"]
   294→        if fid not in overrides:
   295→            overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   296→        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = cluster_name
   297→        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   298→
   299→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   300→    return count
   301→
   302→
   303→def remove_from_cluster(
   304→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   305→) -> int:
   306→    """Remove finding IDs from a cluster. Returns count removed."""
   307→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   308→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   309→    if cluster is None:
   310→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   311→
   312→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   313→    now = utc_now()
   314→    count = 0
   315→    for fid in finding_ids:
   316→        if fid in member_ids:
   317→            member_ids.remove(fid)
   318→            count += 1
   319→        # Clear cluster from override
   320→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   321→        if override and override.get("cluster") == cluster_name:
   322→            override["cluster"] = None
   323→            override["updated_at"] = now
   324→
   325→    # Mark auto-clusters as user_modified when items are manually removed
   326→    if count > 0 and cluster.get("auto"):
   327→        cluster["user_modified"] = True
   328→
   329→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   330→    return count
   331→
   332→
   333→def delete_cluster(plan: PlanModel, name: str) -> list[str]:
   334→    """Delete a cluster and clear cluster refs from overrides. Returns orphaned IDs."""
   335→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   336→    cluster = plan["clusters"].pop(name, None)
   337→    if cluster is None:
   338→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} does not exist")
   339→
   340→    orphaned = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   341→    now = utc_now()
   342→    for fid in orphaned:
   343→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   344→        if override and override.get("cluster") == name:
   345→            override["cluster"] = None
   346→            override["updated_at"] = now
   347→
   348→    if plan.get("active_cluster") == name:
   349→        plan["active_cluster"] = None
   350→
   351→    return orphaned
   352→
   353→
   354→def move_cluster(
   355→    plan: PlanModel,
   356→    cluster_name: str,
   357→    position: str,
   358→    target: str | None = None,
   359→    offset: int | None = None,
   360→) -> int:
   361→    """Move all cluster members as a contiguous block. Returns count moved."""
   362→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   363→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   364→    if cluster is None:
   365→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   366→
   367→    member_ids = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   368→    if not member_ids:
   369→        return 0
   370→
   371→    return move_items(plan, member_ids, position, target, offset)
   372→
   373→
   374→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   375→# Focus
   376→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   377→
   378→def set_focus(plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str) -> None:
   379→    """Set the active cluster focus."""
   380→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   381→    if cluster_name not in plan["clusters"]:
   382→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   383→    plan["active_cluster"] = cluster_name
   384→
   385→
   386→def clear_focus(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   387→    """Clear the active cluster focus."""
   388→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   389→    plan["active_cluster"] = None
   390→
   391→
   392→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   393→# Reset
   394→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   395→
   396→def reset_plan(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   397→    """Reset plan to empty state, preserving version and created timestamp."""
   398→    created = plan.get("created", utc_now())
   399→    plan.clear()
   400→    from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan as _empty
   401→    for k, v in _empty().items():
   402→        plan[k] = v
   403→    plan["created"] = created
   404→
   405→
   406→def purge_ids(plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]) -> int:
   407→    """Remove finding IDs from the plan entirely.
   408→
   409→    Cleans queue_order, skipped, and all cluster memberships.
   410→    Does NOT touch overrides (descriptions/notes are kept for history).
   411→    Returns count of IDs that were actually present somewhere.
   412→    """
   413→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   414→    found = 0
   415→
   416→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   417→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   418→    for fid in finding_ids:
   419→        was_present = False
   420→        if fid in order:
   421→            order.remove(fid)
   422→            was_present = True
   423→        if fid in skipped:
   424→            skipped.pop(fid)
   425→            was_present = True
   426→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   427→            ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   428→            if fid in ids:
   429→                ids.remove(fid)
   430→                was_present = True
   431→        if was_present:
   432→            found += 1
   433→
   434→    return found
   435→
   436→
   437→__all__ = [
   438→    "add_to_cluster",
   439→    "annotate_finding",
   440→    "clear_focus",
   441→    "create_cluster",
   442→    "delete_cluster",
   443→    "describe_finding",
   444→    "move_cluster",
   445→    "move_items",
   446→    "purge_ids",
   447→    "remove_from_cluster",
   448→    "reset_plan",
   449→    "resurface_stale_skips",
   450→    "set_focus",
   451→    "skip_items",
   452→    "unskip_items",
   453→]
   454→

1→"""Output helpers for the `next` command."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→from collections.abc import Mapping, Sequence
     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→from desloppify.core.fallbacks import print_write_error
    10→
    11→
    12→def serialize_item(item: Mapping[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    13→    """Build a serializable output dict from a queue item."""
    14→    # Cluster meta-items get their own serialization
    15→    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
    16→        members_raw = item.get("members", [])
    17→        serialized_members = [serialize_item(m) for m in members_raw]
    18→        return {
    19→            "id": item.get("id"),
    20→            "kind": "cluster",
    21→            "action_type": item.get("action_type", "manual_fix"),
    22→            "summary": item.get("summary"),
    23→            "member_count": item.get("member_count", len(serialized_members)),
    24→            "members": serialized_members,
    25→            "primary_command": item.get("primary_command"),
    26→            "cluster_name": item.get("cluster_name", item.get("id")),
    27→            "cluster_auto": item.get("cluster_auto", True),
    28→            "detector": item.get("detector"),
    29→        }
    30→
    31→    serialized: dict[str, Any] = {
    32→        "id": item.get("id"),
    33→        "kind": item.get("kind", "finding"),
    34→        "tier": item.get("tier"),
    35→        "effective_tier": item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier")),
    36→        "confidence": item.get("confidence"),
    37→        "detector": item.get("detector"),
    38→        "file": item.get("file"),
    39→        "summary": item.get("summary"),
    40→        "detail": item.get("detail", {}),
    41→        "status": item.get("status"),
    42→        "primary_command": item.get("primary_command"),
    43→    }
    44→    explain = item.get("explain")
    45→    if explain is not None:
    46→        serialized["explain"] = explain
    47→
    48→    # Plan metadata
    49→    if item.get("queue_position"):
    50→        serialized["queue_position"] = item["queue_position"]
    51→    if item.get("plan_description"):
    52→        serialized["plan_description"] = item["plan_description"]
    53→    if item.get("plan_note"):
    54→        serialized["plan_note"] = item["plan_note"]
    55→    if item.get("plan_cluster"):
    56→        serialized["plan_cluster"] = item["plan_cluster"]
    57→    if item.get("plan_skipped"):
    58→        serialized["plan_skipped"] = True
    59→        serialized["plan_skip_kind"] = item.get("plan_skip_kind", "temporary")
    60→        if item.get("plan_skip_reason"):
    61→            serialized["plan_skip_reason"] = item["plan_skip_reason"]
    62→
    63→    return serialized
    64→
    65→
    66→def build_query_payload(
    67→    queue: Mapping[str, Any],
    68→    items: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]],
    69→    *,
    70→    command: str,
    71→    narrative: Mapping[str, Any] | None,
    72→    plan: Mapping[str, Any] | None = None,
    73→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    74→    """Build JSON payload for query.json and non-terminal output modes."""
    75→    serialized = [serialize_item(item) for item in items]
    76→    payload: dict[str, Any] = {
    77→        "command": command,
    78→        "items": serialized,
    79→        "queue": {
    80→            "tier_counts": queue.get("tier_counts", {}),
    81→            "requested_tier": queue.get("requested_tier"),
    82→            "selected_tier": queue.get("selected_tier"),
    83→            "fallback_reason": queue.get("fallback_reason"),
    84→            "available_tiers": queue.get("available_tiers", []),
    85→            "total": queue.get("total", len(items)),
    86→        },
    87→        "narrative": narrative,
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    if plan and (
    91→        plan.get("queue_order")
    92→        or plan.get("skipped")
    93→        or plan.get("clusters")
    94→    ):
    95→        clusters_summary = []
    96→        for name, cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).items():
    97→            member_ids = set(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
    98→            clusters_summary.append({
    99→                "name": name,
   100→                "description": cluster.get("description"),
   101→                "item_count": len(member_ids),
   102→            })
   103→        payload["plan"] = {
   104→            "active": True,
   105→            "focus": plan.get("active_cluster"),
   106→            "clusters": clusters_summary,
   107→            "total_ordered": len(plan.get("queue_order", [])),
   108→            "total_skipped": len(plan.get("skipped", {})),
   109→            "plan_overrides_narrative": True,
   110→        }
   111→
   112→    return payload
   113→
   114→
   115→def render_markdown(items: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]]) -> str:
   116→    """Render queue items as markdown."""
   117→    lines = [
   118→        "# Desloppify Next Queue",
   119→        "",
   120→        "| Kind | Tier | Confidence | Summary | Command |",
   121→        "|------|------|------------|---------|---------|",
   122→    ]
   123→    for item in items:
   124→        kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
   125→        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   126→        conf = item.get("confidence", "medium")
   127→        summary = item.get("summary", "").replace("|", "\\|")
   128→        command = (item.get("primary_command", "") or "").replace("|", "\\|")
   129→        lines.append(f"| {kind} | T{tier} | {conf} | {summary} | {command} |")
   130→    lines.append("")
   131→    return "\n".join(lines)
   132→
   133→
   134→def write_output_file(
   135→    output_file: str,
   136→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   137→    item_count: int,
   138→    *,
   139→    safe_write_text_fn,
   140→    colorize_fn,
   141→) -> bool:
   142→    """Persist payload to file and print success/failure hints."""
   143→    try:
   144→        safe_write_text_fn(output_file, json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n")
   145→        print(colorize_fn(f"Wrote {item_count} items to {output_file}", "green"))
   146→    except OSError as exc:
   147→        payload["output_error"] = str(exc)
   148→        print_write_error(output_file, exc, label="next output")
   149→        return False
   150→    return True
   151→
   152→
   153→def emit_non_terminal_output(
   154→    output_format: str,
   155→    payload: dict[str, Any],
   156→    items: Sequence[Mapping[str, Any]],
   157→) -> bool:
   158→    """Render JSON/markdown output variants."""
   159→    renderers = {
   160→        "json": lambda: print(json.dumps(payload, indent=2)),
   161→        "md": lambda: print(render_markdown(items)),
   162→    }
   163→    renderer = renderers.get(output_format)
   164→    if renderer is None:
   165→        return False
   166→    renderer()
   167→    return True
   168→
   169→
   170→__all__ = [
   171→    "build_query_payload",
   172→    "emit_non_terminal_output",
   173→    "render_markdown",
   174→    "serialize_item",
   175→    "write_output_file",
   176→]
   177→

     2→

     8→from desloppify.app.commands.next_parts import output as next_output_mod
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.next_parts import render as next_render_mod
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.query import write_query
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.score import target_strict_score_from_config

    24→from desloppify.core.tooling import check_config_staleness, check_tool_staleness
    25→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import safe_write_text
    26→from desloppify.intelligence.narrative import NarrativeContext, compute_narrative
    27→
    28→
    29→def _scorecard_subjective(
    30→    state: dict,
    31→    dim_scores: dict,
    32→) -> list[dict]:
    33→    """Return scorecard-aligned subjective entries for current dimension scores."""
    34→    return next_render_mod.scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores)
    35→
    36→
    37→def _low_subjective_dimensions(
    38→    state: dict,
    39→    dim_scores: dict,
    40→    *,
    41→    threshold: float = 95.0,
    42→) -> list[tuple[str, float, int]]:
    43→    """Return assessed scorecard-subjective entries below the threshold."""
    44→    low: list[tuple[str, float, int]] = []
    45→    for entry in _scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores):
    46→        if entry.get("placeholder"):
    47→            continue
    48→        strict_val = float(entry.get("strict", entry.get("score", 100.0)))
    49→        if strict_val < threshold:
    50→            low.append(
    51→                (
    52→                    str(entry.get("name", "Subjective")),
    53→                    strict_val,
    54→                    int(entry.get("issues", 0)),
    55→                )
    56→            )
    57→    low.sort(key=lambda item: item[1])
    58→    return low
    59→
    60→
    61→def cmd_next(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    62→    """Show next highest-priority queue items."""
    63→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    64→    state = runtime.state
    65→    config = runtime.config
    66→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    67→        return
    68→
    69→    stale_warning = check_tool_staleness(state)
    70→    if stale_warning:
    71→        print(colorize(f"  {stale_warning}", "yellow"))
    72→    skill_warning = check_skill_version()
    73→    if skill_warning:
    74→        print(colorize(f"  {skill_warning}", "yellow"))
    75→    config_warning = check_config_staleness(config)
    76→    if config_warning:
    77→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
    78→
    79→    _get_items(args, state, config)
    80→
    81→
    82→def _get_items(args, state: dict, config: dict) -> None:
    83→    tier = getattr(args, "tier", None)
    84→    count = getattr(args, "count", 1) or 1
    85→    scope = getattr(args, "scope", None)
    86→    status = getattr(args, "status", "open")
    87→    group = getattr(args, "group", "item")
    88→    output_format = getattr(args, "format", "terminal")
    89→    explain = bool(getattr(args, "explain", False))
    90→    no_tier_fallback = bool(getattr(args, "no_tier_fallback", False))
    91→    cluster_arg = getattr(args, "cluster", None)
    92→    include_skipped = bool(getattr(args, "include_skipped", False))
    93→
    94→    target_strict = target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0)
    95→
    96→    # Load the living plan
    97→    from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan
    98→    plan = load_plan()
    99→    plan_data: dict | None = None
   100→    if (
   101→        plan.get("queue_order")
   102→        or plan.get("overrides")
   103→        or plan.get("clusters")
   104→    ):
   105→        plan_data = plan
   106→
   107→    # Auto-scope to focus cluster if set and no explicit scope/cluster
   108→    effective_cluster = cluster_arg
   109→    if plan_data and not cluster_arg and not scope:
   110→        active_cluster = plan_data.get("active_cluster")
   111→        if active_cluster:
   112→            effective_cluster = active_cluster
   113→
   114→    queue = build_work_queue(
   115→        state,
   116→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
   117→            tier=tier,
   118→            count=count,
   119→            scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
   120→            scope=scope,
   121→            status=status,
   122→            include_subjective=True,
   123→            subjective_threshold=target_strict,
   124→            no_tier_fallback=no_tier_fallback,
   125→            explain=explain,
   126→            plan=plan_data,
   127→            include_skipped=include_skipped,
   128→            cluster=effective_cluster,
   129→        ),
   130→    )
   131→    items = queue.get("items", [])
   132→
   133→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   134→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   135→    narrative = compute_narrative(
   136→        state,
   137→        context=NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="next", plan=plan_data),
   138→    )
   139→
   140→    payload = next_output_mod.build_query_payload(
   141→        queue, items, command="next", narrative=narrative, plan=plan_data
   142→    )
   143→    payload["overall_score"] = state_mod.get_overall_score(state)
   144→    payload["objective_score"] = state_mod.get_objective_score(state)
   145→    payload["strict_score"] = state_mod.get_strict_score(state)
   146→    payload["scorecard_dimensions"] = scorecard_dimensions_payload(
   147→        state,
   148→        dim_scores=state.get("dimension_scores", {}),
   149→    )
   150→    payload["subjective_measures"] = [
   151→        row for row in payload["scorecard_dimensions"] if row.get("subjective")
   152→    ]
   153→    write_query(payload)
   154→
   155→    output_file = getattr(args, "output", None)
   156→    if output_file:
   157→        if next_output_mod.write_output_file(
   158→            output_file,
   159→            payload,
   160→            len(items),
   161→            safe_write_text_fn=safe_write_text,
   162→            colorize_fn=colorize,
   163→        ):
   164→            return
   165→        raise SystemExit(1)
   166→
   167→    if next_output_mod.emit_non_terminal_output(output_format, payload, items):
   168→        return
   169→
   170→    next_render_mod.render_queue_header(queue, explain)
   171→    strict_score = state_mod.get_strict_score(state)
   172→    if next_render_mod.show_empty_queue(queue, tier, strict_score):
   173→        return
   174→
   175→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
   176→    findings_scoped = state_mod.path_scoped_findings(

   184→    except (ImportError, TypeError, ValueError):

1→"""Compact queue table renderer for ``plan queue``."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
     9→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize, print_table
    10→from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan
    11→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue
    12→
    13→
    14→def _truncate(text: str, width: int) -> str:
    15→    if len(text) <= width:
    16→        return text
    17→    return text[: width - 1] + "\u2026"
    18→
    19→
    20→def _cluster_tier_label(item: dict) -> str:
    21→    """Compute a tier label from cluster members, e.g. 'T2' or 'T1-T3'."""
    22→    members = item.get("members", [])
    23→    if not members:
    24→        return ""
    25→    tiers = {int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) for m in members}
    26→    lo, hi = min(tiers), max(tiers)
    27→    if lo == hi:
    28→        return f"T{lo}"
    29→    return f"T{lo}-T{hi}"
    30→
    31→
    32→def cmd_plan_queue(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    33→    """Render a compact table of all upcoming queue items."""
    34→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    35→    state = runtime.state
    36→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    37→        return
    38→
    39→    top = getattr(args, "top", 30)
    40→    tier_filter = getattr(args, "tier", None)
    41→    cluster_filter = getattr(args, "cluster", None)
    42→    include_skipped = bool(getattr(args, "include_skipped", False))
    43→
    44→    plan = load_plan()
    45→    plan_data: dict | None = None
    46→    if plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("overrides") or plan.get("clusters"):
    47→        plan_data = plan
    48→
    49→    # Auto-scope to focus cluster
    50→    effective_cluster = cluster_filter
    51→    if plan_data and not cluster_filter:
    52→        active_cluster = plan_data.get("active_cluster")
    53→        if active_cluster:
    54→            effective_cluster = active_cluster
    55→
    56→    queue = build_work_queue(
    57→        state,
    58→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
    59→            tier=tier_filter,
    60→            count=None,
    61→            scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
    62→            status="open",
    63→            include_subjective=True,
    64→            plan=plan_data,
    65→            include_skipped=include_skipped,
    66→            cluster=effective_cluster,
    67→            collapse_clusters=True,
    68→        ),
    69→    )
    70→    items = queue.get("items", [])
    71→    tier_counts = queue.get("tier_counts", {})
    72→
    73→    # Header
    74→    total = len(items)
    75→    skipped_count = sum(1 for it in items if it.get("plan_skipped"))
    76→    non_skipped = total - skipped_count
    77→    tc_parts = " ".join(f"T{t}:{tier_counts.get(t, 0)}" for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))
    78→    print(colorize(f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} items  ({tc_parts})", "bold"))
    79→
    80→    focus = plan.get("active_cluster") if plan_data else None
    81→    if focus:
    82→        print(colorize(f"  Focus: {focus}", "cyan"))
    83→
    84→    if not include_skipped and skipped_count == 0:
    85→        # Check plan for skipped items not in queue
    86→        plan_skipped_count = len(plan.get("skipped", {})) if plan_data else 0
    87→        if plan_skipped_count:
    88→            print(colorize(
    89→                f"  ({plan_skipped_count} skipped item{'s' if plan_skipped_count != 1 else ''}"
    90→                " hidden \u2014 use --include-skipped)",
    91→                "dim",
    92→            ))
    93→
    94→    if not items:
    95→        print(colorize("\n  Queue is empty.", "green"))
    96→        return
    97→
    98→    # Determine which items to show
    99→    display_items = items
   100→    if top > 0 and len(items) > top:
   101→        display_items = items[:top]
   102→
   103→    # Build rows
   104→    headers = ["#", "Tier", "Detector", "Summary", "Cluster"]
   105→    rows: list[list[str]] = []
   106→    for idx, item in enumerate(display_items, 1):
   107→        pos = str(idx)
   108→        kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
   109→
   110→        if kind == "cluster":
   111→            tier_str = _cluster_tier_label(item)
   112→            member_count = item.get("member_count", 0)
   113→            detector = item.get("detector", "cluster")
   114→            summary = f"[{member_count} items] {item.get('summary', '')}"
   115→            cluster_name = item.get("cluster_name", item.get("id", ""))
   116→        else:
   117→            tier_val = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   118→            tier_str = f"T{tier_val}"
   119→            detector = item.get("detector", "")
   120→            summary = item.get("summary", "")
   121→            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   122→            cluster_name = plan_cluster.get("name", "") if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else ""
   123→
   124→        suffix = ""
   125→        if item.get("plan_skipped"):
   126→            suffix = " [skip]"
   127→
   128→        summary_display = _truncate(summary, 48) + suffix
   129→        rows.append([pos, tier_str, detector, summary_display, cluster_name])
   130→
   131→    print()
   132→    widths = [4, 4, 12, 50, 16]
   133→    print_table(headers, rows, widths=widths)
   134→
   135→    if top > 0 and len(items) > top:
   136→        remaining = len(items) - top
   137→        print(colorize(
   138→            f"\n  ... and {remaining} more (use --top 0 to show all)", "dim"
   139→        ))
   140→    print()
   141→
   142→
   143→__all__ = ["cmd_plan_queue"]
   144→

1→"""Post-scan plan reconciliation — handle finding churn."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     6→from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel, SupersededEntry, ensure_plan_defaults
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX
    10→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, utc_now
    11→
    12→
    13→SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS = 90
    14→
    15→
    16→@dataclass
    17→class ReconcileResult:
    18→    """Summary of changes made during reconciliation."""
    19→
    20→    superseded: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    21→    pruned: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    22→    resurfaced: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    23→    changes: int = 0
    24→
    25→
    26→def _find_candidates(
    27→    state: StateModel, detector: str, file: str
    28→) -> list[str]:
    29→    """Find open findings that could be remaps for a disappeared finding."""
    30→    candidates: list[str] = []
    31→    for fid, finding in state.get("findings", {}).items():
    32→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    33→            continue
    34→        if finding.get("detector") == detector and finding.get("file") == file:
    35→            candidates.append(fid)
    36→    return candidates
    37→
    38→
    39→def _is_finding_alive(state: StateModel, finding_id: str) -> bool:
    40→    """Return True if the finding exists and is open."""
    41→    finding = state.get("findings", {}).get(finding_id)
    42→    if finding is None:
    43→        return False
    44→    return finding.get("status") == "open"
    45→
    46→
    47→def _supersede_id(
    48→    plan: PlanModel,
    49→    state: StateModel,
    50→    finding_id: str,
    51→    now: str,
    52→) -> bool:
    53→    """Move a disappeared finding to superseded. Returns True if changed."""
    54→    finding = state.get("findings", {}).get(finding_id)
    55→    detector = ""
    56→    file = ""
    57→    summary = ""
    58→    if finding:
    59→        detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    60→        file = finding.get("file", "")
    61→        summary = finding.get("summary", "")
    62→
    63→    candidates = _find_candidates(state, detector, file) if detector else []
    64→    # Don't include the original in candidates
    65→    candidates = [c for c in candidates if c != finding_id]
    66→
    67→    entry: SupersededEntry = {
    68→        "original_id": finding_id,
    69→        "original_detector": detector,
    70→        "original_file": file,
    71→        "original_summary": summary,
    72→        "status": "superseded",
    73→        "superseded_at": now,
    74→        "remapped_to": None,
    75→        "candidates": candidates[:5],
    76→    }
    77→
    78→    # Preserve any existing override note
    79→    override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(finding_id)
    80→    if override and override.get("note"):
    81→        entry["note"] = override["note"]
    82→
    83→    plan["superseded"][finding_id] = entry
    84→
    85→    # Remove from queue_order, skipped, cluster finding_ids
    86→    order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    87→    skipped: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
    88→    if finding_id in order:
    89→        order.remove(finding_id)
    90→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    91→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    92→        ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
    93→        if finding_id in ids:
    94→            ids.remove(finding_id)
    95→
    96→    return True
    97→
    98→
    99→def _prune_old_superseded(plan: PlanModel, now_dt: datetime) -> list[str]:
   100→    """Remove superseded entries older than TTL. Returns pruned IDs."""
   101→    superseded = plan.get("superseded", {})
   102→    cutoff = now_dt - timedelta(days=SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS)
   103→    to_prune: list[str] = []
   104→
   105→    for fid, entry in superseded.items():
   106→        ts = entry.get("superseded_at", "")
   107→        try:
   108→            entry_dt = datetime.fromisoformat(ts)
   109→            if entry_dt.tzinfo is None:
   110→                entry_dt = entry_dt.replace(tzinfo=UTC)
   111→            if entry_dt < cutoff:
   112→                to_prune.append(fid)
   113→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   114→            to_prune.append(fid)
   115→
   116→    for fid in to_prune:
   117→        superseded.pop(fid, None)
   118→        # Also clean up stale overrides
   119→        plan.get("overrides", {}).pop(fid, None)
   120→
   121→    return to_prune
   122→
   123→
   124→def reconcile_plan_after_scan(
   125→    plan: PlanModel,
   126→    state: StateModel,
   127→) -> ReconcileResult:
   128→    """Reconcile plan against current state after a scan.
   129→
   130→    Finds IDs referenced in the plan that no longer exist or are no longer
   131→    open, moves them to superseded, and prunes old superseded entries.
   132→    """
   133→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   134→    result = ReconcileResult()
   135→    now = utc_now()
   136→    now_dt = datetime.now(UTC)
   137→
   138→    # Collect all finding IDs referenced by the plan
   139→    referenced_ids: set[str] = set()
   140→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   141→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   142→    for override_id in plan.get("overrides", {}):
   143→        referenced_ids.add(override_id)
   144→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   145→        referenced_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   146→
   147→    # Exclude already-superseded IDs and synthetic IDs (managed by stale_dimensions)
   148→    already_superseded = set(plan.get("superseded", {}).keys())
   149→    referenced_ids -= already_superseded
   150→    referenced_ids = {
   151→        fid for fid in referenced_ids
   152→        if not fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX)
   153→    }
   154→
   155→    # Check each referenced ID
   156→    for fid in sorted(referenced_ids):
   157→        if not _is_finding_alive(state, fid):
   158→            if _supersede_id(plan, state, fid, now):
   159→                result.superseded.append(fid)
   160→                result.changes += 1
   161→
   162→    # Resurface stale temporary skips
   163→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   164→    from desloppify.engine._plan.operations import resurface_stale_skips
   165→
   166→    resurfaced = resurface_stale_skips(plan, scan_count)
   167→    if resurfaced:
   168→        result.resurfaced = resurfaced
   169→        result.changes += len(resurfaced)
   170→
   171→    # Prune old superseded entries
   172→    pruned = _prune_old_superseded(plan, now_dt)
   173→    result.pruned = pruned
   174→    result.changes += len(pruned)
   175→
   176→    return result
   177→
   178→
   179→__all__ = [
   180→    "ReconcileResult",
   181→    "reconcile_plan_after_scan",
   182→]
   183→

    57→
    58→def _apply_plan_order(
    59→    items: list[dict],
    60→    plan: dict,
    61→    *,
    62→    include_skipped: bool = False,
    63→    cluster: str | None = None,
    64→) -> list[dict]:
    65→    """Reorder items according to the living plan.
    66→
    67→    1. Items in ``queue_order`` appear first, in that order.
    68→    2. Remaining items keep their mechanical sort.
    69→    3. Skipped items are appended last (or excluded).
    70→    4. Each item is annotated with plan metadata.
    71→    """
    72→    queue_order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    73→    skipped_map: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
    74→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(skipped_map.keys())
    75→    overrides: dict = plan.get("overrides", {})
    76→    clusters: dict = plan.get("clusters", {})
    77→    active_cluster = plan.get("active_cluster")
    78→
    79→    # Build lookup
    80→    by_id: dict[str, dict] = {}
    81→    for item in items:
    82→        by_id[item["id"]] = item
    83→
    84→    # Annotate items with plan metadata
    85→    for item_id, item in by_id.items():
    86→        override = overrides.get(item_id, {})
    87→        if override.get("description"):
    88→            item["plan_description"] = override["description"]
    89→        if override.get("note"):
    90→            item["plan_note"] = override["note"]
    91→        if override.get("cluster"):
    92→            cluster_name = override["cluster"]
    93→            cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
    94→            item["plan_cluster"] = {
    95→                "name": cluster_name,
    96→                "description": cluster_data.get("description"),
    97→                "total_items": len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", [])),
    98→                "sibling_ids": cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []),
    99→            }
   100→
   101→    # Split into ordered, remaining, skipped
   102→    ordered: list[dict] = []

   110→    remaining: list[dict] = []

   112→        item_id = item["id"]
   113→        if item_id in ordered_ids:
   114→            continue
   115→        if item_id in skipped_ids:
   116→            skipped_items.append(item)
   117→        else:
   118→            remaining.append(item)

   120→    # Assign queue positions

   122→    if include_skipped:

   135→

   138→    if effective_cluster:

   140→        cluster_member_ids = set(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
   141→        if cluster_member_ids:
   142→            result = [item for item in result if item["id"] in cluster_member_ids]
   143→
   144→    return result

1→"""Plan schema types, defaults, and validation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any, Required, TypedDict
     6→
     7→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
     8→
     9→PLAN_VERSION = 2
    10→
    11→VALID_SKIP_KINDS = {"temporary", "permanent", "false_positive"}
    12→
    13→
    14→class SkipEntry(TypedDict, total=False):
    15→    finding_id: Required[str]
    16→    kind: Required[str]  # "temporary" | "permanent" | "false_positive"
    17→    reason: str | None
    18→    note: str | None  # required for permanent (wontfix note)
    19→    attestation: str | None  # required for permanent/false_positive
    20→    created_at: str
    21→    review_after: int | None  # re-surface after N scans (temporary only)
    22→    skipped_at_scan: int  # state.scan_count when skipped
    23→
    24→
    25→class ItemOverride(TypedDict, total=False):
    26→    finding_id: Required[str]
    27→    description: str | None
    28→    note: str | None
    29→    cluster: str | None
    30→    created_at: str
    31→    updated_at: str
    32→
    33→
    34→class Cluster(TypedDict, total=False):
    35→    name: Required[str]
    36→    description: str | None
    37→    finding_ids: list[str]
    38→    created_at: str
    39→    updated_at: str
    40→    auto: bool  # True for auto-generated clusters
    41→    cluster_key: str  # Deterministic grouping key (for regeneration)
    42→    action: str | None  # Primary resolution command/guidance text
    43→    user_modified: bool  # True when user manually edits membership
    44→
    45→
    46→class SupersededEntry(TypedDict, total=False):
    47→    original_id: Required[str]
    48→    original_detector: str
    49→    original_file: str
    50→    original_summary: str
    51→    status: str  # "superseded" | "remapped" | "dismissed"
    52→    superseded_at: str
    53→    remapped_to: str | None
    54→    candidates: list[str]
    55→    note: str | None
    56→
    57→
    58→class PlanModel(TypedDict, total=False):
    59→    version: Required[int]
    60→    created: Required[str]
    61→    updated: Required[str]
    62→    queue_order: list[str]
    63→    deferred: list[str]  # kept empty for migration compat
    64→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry]
    65→    active_cluster: str | None
    66→    overrides: dict[str, ItemOverride]
    67→    clusters: dict[str, Cluster]
    68→    superseded: dict[str, SupersededEntry]
    69→
    70→
    71→def empty_plan() -> PlanModel:
    72→    """Return a new empty plan payload."""
    73→    now = utc_now()
    74→    return {
    75→        "version": PLAN_VERSION,
    76→        "created": now,
    77→        "updated": now,
    78→        "queue_order": [],
    79→        "deferred": [],
    80→        "skipped": {},
    81→        "active_cluster": None,
    82→        "overrides": {},
    83→        "clusters": {},
    84→        "superseded": {},
    85→    }
    86→
    87→
    88→def ensure_plan_defaults(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    89→    """Normalize a loaded plan to ensure all keys exist.
    90→
    91→    Handles migration from v1 (deferred list) to v2 (skipped dict).
    92→    """
    93→    defaults = empty_plan()
    94→    for key, value in defaults.items():
    95→        plan.setdefault(key, value)
    96→
    97→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
    98→        plan["queue_order"] = []
    99→    if not isinstance(plan.get("deferred"), list):
   100→        plan["deferred"] = []
   101→    if not isinstance(plan.get("skipped"), dict):
   102→        plan["skipped"] = {}
   103→    if not isinstance(plan.get("overrides"), dict):
   104→        plan["overrides"] = {}
   105→    if not isinstance(plan.get("clusters"), dict):
   106→        plan["clusters"] = {}
   107→    if not isinstance(plan.get("superseded"), dict):
   108→        plan["superseded"] = {}
   109→
   110→    # Migrate deferred → skipped (v1 → v2)
   111→    deferred: list[str] = plan["deferred"]
   112→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   113→    if deferred:
   114→        now = utc_now()
   115→        for fid in list(deferred):
   116→            if fid not in skipped:
   117→                skipped[fid] = {
   118→                    "finding_id": fid,
   119→                    "kind": "temporary",
   120→                    "reason": None,
   121→                    "note": None,
   122→                    "attestation": None,
   123→                    "created_at": now,
   124→                    "review_after": None,
   125→                    "skipped_at_scan": 0,
   126→                }
   127→        deferred.clear()
   128→
   129→    # Normalize cluster fields
   130→    for cluster in plan["clusters"].values():
   131→        if isinstance(cluster, dict):
   132→            if not isinstance(cluster.get("finding_ids"), list):
   133→                cluster["finding_ids"] = []
   134→            cluster.setdefault("auto", False)
   135→            cluster.setdefault("cluster_key", "")
   136→            cluster.setdefault("action", None)
   137→            cluster.setdefault("user_modified", False)
   138→
   139→
   140→def validate_plan(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   141→    """Raise ValueError when plan invariants are violated."""
   142→    if not isinstance(plan.get("version"), int):
   143→        raise ValueError("plan.version must be an int")
   144→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
   145→        raise ValueError("plan.queue_order must be a list")
   146→
   147→    # No ID should appear in both queue_order and skipped
   148→    skipped_ids = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   149→    overlap = set(plan["queue_order"]) & skipped_ids
   150→    if overlap:
   151→        raise ValueError(
   152→            f"IDs cannot appear in both queue_order and skipped: {sorted(overlap)}"
   153→        )
   154→
   155→    # Validate skip entry kinds
   156→    for fid, entry in plan.get("skipped", {}).items():
   157→        kind = entry.get("kind")
   158→        if kind not in VALID_SKIP_KINDS:
   159→            raise ValueError(
   160→                f"Invalid skip kind {kind!r} for {fid}; must be one of {sorted(VALID_SKIP_KINDS)}"
   161→            )
   162→
   163→
   164→__all__ = [
   165→    "PLAN_VERSION",
   166→    "Cluster",
   167→    "ItemOverride",
   168→    "PlanModel",
   169→    "SkipEntry",
   170→    "SupersededEntry",
   171→    "VALID_SKIP_KINDS",
   172→    "empty_plan",
   173→    "ensure_plan_defaults",
   174→    "validate_plan",
   175→]
   176→

    17→def _plan_header(state: PlanState, stats: dict) -> list[str]:
    18→    """Build the plan header: title, score line, and codebase metrics."""
    19→    overall_score = get_overall_score(state)
    20→    objective_score = get_objective_score(state)
    21→    strict_score = get_strict_score(state)
    22→
    23→    if (

    25→        and objective_score is not None
    26→        and strict_score is not None
    27→    ):
    28→        header_score = (
    29→            f"**Health:** overall {overall_score:.1f}/100 | "
    30→            f"objective {objective_score:.1f}/100 | "
    31→            f"strict {strict_score:.1f}/100"
    32→        )
    33→    elif overall_score is not None:
    34→        header_score = f"**Score: {overall_score:.1f}/100**"
    35→    else:
    36→        header_score = "**Scores unavailable**"
    37→
    38→    metrics = state.get("codebase_metrics", {})
    39→    total_files = sum(metric.get("total_files", 0) for metric in metrics.values())
    40→    total_loc = sum(metric.get("total_loc", 0) for metric in metrics.values())
    41→    total_dirs = sum(metric.get("total_directories", 0) for metric in metrics.values())
    42→
    43→    lines = [
    44→        f"# Desloppify Plan — {date.today().isoformat()}",
    45→        "",

    49→        f"{stats.get('wontfix', 0)} wontfix | "
    50→        f"{stats.get('auto_resolved', 0)} auto-resolved",

    58→            else f"{total_loc // 1000}K"

    61→            f"\n{total_files} files · {loc_str} LOC · {total_dirs} directories\n"

    66→
    67→def _plan_dimension_table(state: PlanState) -> list[str]:
    68→    """Build the dimension health table rows (empty list when no data)."""

    70→    if not dim_scores:

    78→    ]
    79→    static_names: set[str] = set()
    80→    rendered_names: set[str] = set()
    81→    subjective_display_names = {
    82→        display.lower() for display in DISPLAY_NAMES.values()
    83→    }
    84→
    85→    def _looks_subjective(name: str, data: dict) -> bool:
    86→        detectors = data.get("detectors", {})
    87→        if "subjective_assessment" in detectors:
    88→            return True
    89→        lowered = name.strip().lower()
    90→        return lowered in subjective_display_names or lowered.startswith("elegance")
    91→
    92→    for dim in DIMENSIONS:
    93→        ds = dim_scores.get(dim.name)
    94→        if not ds:
    95→            continue
    96→        static_names.add(dim.name)
    97→        rendered_names.add(dim.name)
    98→        checks = ds.get("checks", 0)
    99→        issues = ds.get("issues", 0)
   100→        score_val = ds.get("score", 100)
```

    22→def cmd_plan_describe(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    23→    """Set augmented description on findings."""
    24→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    25→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    26→        return
    27→
    28→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    29→    text: str = getattr(args, "text", "")
    30→
    31→    from desloppify.engine.plan import describe_finding
    32→
    33→    plan = load_plan()
    34→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    35→    if not finding_ids:
    36→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    37→        return
    38→
    39→    for fid in finding_ids:
    40→        describe_finding(plan, fid, text or None)
    41→    save_plan(plan)
    42→    print(colorize(f"  Set description on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
    43→
    44→
    45→def cmd_plan_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    46→    """Set note on findings."""
    47→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    48→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    49→        return
    50→
    51→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    52→    text: str | None = getattr(args, "text", None)
    53→
    54→    from desloppify.engine.plan import annotate_finding
    55→
    56→    plan = load_plan()
    57→    finding_ids = resolve_ids_from_patterns(state, patterns, plan=plan)
    58→    if not finding_ids:
    59→        print(colorize("  No matching findings found.", "yellow"))
    60→        return
    61→
    62→    for fid in finding_ids:
    63→        annotate_finding(plan, fid, text)
    64→    save_plan(plan)
    65→    print(colorize(f"  Set note on {len(finding_ids)} finding(s).", "green"))
    66→
    67→
    68→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

    72→def cmd_plan_skip(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:

    75→        show_attestation_requirement,
    76→        validate_attestation,
    77→    )
    78→    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    79→
    80→    state = command_runtime(args).state
    81→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    82→        return
    83→
    84→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    85→    reason: str | None = getattr(args, "reason", None)
    86→    review_after: int | None = getattr(args, "review_after", None)
    87→    permanent: bool = getattr(args, "permanent", False)
    88→    false_positive: bool = getattr(args, "false_positive", False)
    89→    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)
    90→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    91→
    92→    # Determine skip kind
    93→    if false_positive:
    94→        kind = "false_positive"
    95→    elif permanent:
    96→        kind = "permanent"
    97→    else:
    98→        kind = "temporary"
    99→
   100→    # Validate requirements for permanent/false_positive

109→def remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   110→    """Suppress findings matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
   111→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   112→    matched_ids = [
   113→        finding_id
   114→        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
   115→        if is_ignored(finding_id, finding["file"], [pattern])
   116→    ]
   117→    now = utc_now()
   118→    for finding_id in matched_ids:
   119→        finding = state["findings"][finding_id]
   120→        finding["suppressed"] = True
   121→        finding["suppressed_at"] = now
   122→        finding["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
   123→    # Deferred import to avoid circular dependency with engine._state.scoring
   124→    from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
   125→
   126→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   127→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   128→    return len(matched_ids)
   129→
   130→
   131→def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   132→    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching findings."""
   133→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   134→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   135→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   136→    if pattern not in ignores:
   137→        ignores.append(pattern)
   138→    return remove_ignored_findings(state, pattern)
```

---

## Analysis: Queue Count Semantics Mismatch

I've traced through the code thoroughly. Here's the root cause and the exact mismatch:

### 1. **What `build_work_queue` returns in the `total` field** (core.py:346, 357)

```python
total = len(filtered)  # Line 346: total = COLLAPSED QUEUE SIZE
...
return {
    "items": filtered,
    "total": total,           # This is the number of top-level items after clustering
    "tier_counts": counts,    # But THIS counts individual findings within clusters
```

**`total` is the number of collapsed queue entries** (clusters count as 1, individual findings count as 1).

### 2. **What `tier_counts()` counts** (ranking.py:173-184)

```python
def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
    for item in items:
        if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
            # Count each member at its own tier ← KEY DIFFERENCE
            for member in item.get("members", []):
                tier = int(member.get("effective_tier", member.get("tier", 3)))
                counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
            continue
        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
        counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
    return counts
```

**For cluster items, `tier_counts()` iterates through `members` and counts each one individually**. So a cluster with 50 T3 findings and 20 T4 findings adds 50 to `counts[3]` and 20 to `counts[4]`, not 1+1.

### 3. **How the header displays this mismatch** (queue_render.py:74-78)

```python
total = len(items)  # items after collapse → 266 (includes collapsed clusters)
skipped_count = sum(1 for it in items if it.get("plan_skipped"))  # Count collapsed items
non_skipped = total - skipped_count  # Still collapsed count
tc_parts = " ".join(f"T{t}:{tier_counts.get(t, 0)}" for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))  # Individual findings
print(colorize(f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} items  ({tc_parts})", "bold"))
# Output: "Queue: 266 items (T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)" ← MISMATCH!
```

### 4. **How `next` displays the same data** (next_parts/render.py:46-54)

```python
def _render_tier_navigator(queue: dict) -> None:
    counts = queue.get("tier_counts", {})
    print(colorize("\n  Tier Navigator", "bold"))
    print(
        colorize(
            f"    Open: T1:{counts.get(1, 0)} T2:{counts.get(2, 0)} "
            f"T3:{counts.get(3, 0)} T4:{counts.get(4, 0)}",
            "dim",
        )
    )
```

**`next` displays the same tier_counts numbers (sum=813), which represent individual findings, not collapsed items.**

---

## The Confusion

**User sees:**
- `plan queue`: "Queue: 266 items (T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)" → 813 total
- `next`: "Open: T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260" → 813 total  
- But there are only 266 collapsed queue entries

**The semantic confusion:** Are we counting "queue entries" or "findings"? These are two different things when clustering is enabled.

---

## Recommended Fixes

You have three options, each with trade-offs:

### **Option A: Show Both Numbers Explicitly** (My recommendation)
Change the header to make the distinction crystal clear:
```
  Queue: 266 collapsed items (813 findings) (T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)
```
or
```
  Queue: 266 items  |  Findings: T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260
```

**Pros:** No logic changes; users understand exactly what they're looking at; works in both `plan queue` and `next`  
**Cons:** Slightly longer header

### **Option B: Change `tier_counts()` to Count Clusters as 1**
Modify `ranking.py` `tier_counts()` to:
```python
def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
    for item in items:
        if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
            # Cluster counts as 1 at its primary tier (min of members)
            tiers = {int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) for m in item.get("members", [])}
            primary_tier = min(tiers) if tiers else 3
            counts[primary_tier] += 1
        else:
            tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
            counts[tier] += 1
    return counts
```

**Pros:** Headers become consistent (266 = T1+T2+T3+T4)  
**Cons:** Loses information about what's *inside* clusters; `next` would show different counts than `plan queue` if you drill into a cluster (via `--cluster` flag); breaks the semantic of "how many findings to fix"

### **Option C: Keep Finding Counts Separate in Query Payload**
Update `next_parts/output.py` to include both:
```python
"queue": {
    "item_count": queue.get("total"),  # Collapsed count
    "tier_counts": queue.get("tier_counts", {}),  # Finding counts
    "tier_item_counts": {...},  # NEW: cluster-aware tier counts
    ...
}
```

**Pros:** API becomes explicit; consumers (SKILL.md, dashboards) can choose which to display  
**Cons:** More code; both numbers visible but not explained in terminal

---

## Detailed Flow Diagrams

**What Happens in `plan queue` with clusters:**

```
STATE (813 findings: T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)
↓
build_work_queue() with collapse_clusters=True
├─ build_finding_items() → 813 finding dicts
├─ _collapse_clusters() → collapses auto-clusters into meta-items
│  └─ Result: 266 top-level items (e.g., 5 clusters + 261 individual findings)
├─ tier_counts(266 items) → iterates through clusters, counts members
│  └─ Result: {1: 7, 2: 107, 3: 439, 4: 260}  (SUM = 813, not 266)
└─ return { items: 266, tier_counts: {1:7, 2:107, ...} }

queue_render.py:
  total = len(items) = 266 ← COLLAPSE COUNT
  tc_parts = "T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260" ← FINDING COUNTS
  print(f"Queue: {total} items ({tc_parts})")
  Output: "Queue: 266 items (T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)"
  ↑ User sees mismatch: 266 ≠ 813
```

**What Happens in `next`:**

```
build_work_queue(collapse_clusters=True) with count=1
  └─ Same tier_counts: {1: 7, 2: 107, 3: 439, 4: 260}

render.py._render_tier_navigator():
  counts = queue["tier_counts"]
  print(f"Open: T1:{counts[1]} T2:{counts[2]} T3:{counts[3]} T4:{counts[4]}")
  Output: "Open: T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260"
  ↑ User sees finding counts, not item counts
```

---

## Summary

**The root cause:** `tier_counts()` deliberately counts cluster members individually (good for understanding finding distribution), but the header in `plan queue` uses the collapsed item count (266), creating an apparent mismatch.

**This is not a bug—it's a semantic ambiguity:** The code is working as designed, but the UI doesn't clarify *which* count it's showing.

**My recommendation:** Go with **Option A** — show both numbers clearly. This makes the queue summary explicit without changing any logic. Example:

```python
# queue_render.py:74-78
total = len(items)
skipped_count = sum(1 for it in items if it.get("plan_skipped"))
non_skipped = total - skipped_count
finding_total = sum(tier_counts.get(t, 0) for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))
tc_parts = " ".join(f"T{t}:{tier_counts.get(t, 0)}" for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))
print(colorize(
    f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} items  ({finding_total} findings: {tc_parts})",
    "bold"
))
```

Output: `Queue: 266 items (813 findings: T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)`

This instantly clarifies that 266 is the collapsed queue size and 813 is the total finding count inside those items.

## Summary Report: Understanding "Plan Show" vs "Plan Queue" Display

Based on my thorough code analysis, I can now clarify the confusion between `plan show` and `plan queue`, and explain what the metrics actually represent.

### Key Findings

#### 1. **Plan Data Structure** (`schema.py`, lines 58-68)

The `PlanModel` TypedDict has these six fields:

```python
class PlanModel(TypedDict, total=False):
    version: int
    created: str
    updated: str
    queue_order: list[str]              # User-ordered IDs
    deferred: list[str]                 # Deprecated (migrated to skipped)
    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry]       # Explicitly deferred items
    active_cluster: str | None          # Currently focused cluster
    overrides: dict[str, ItemOverride]  # Metadata overlays
    clusters: dict[str, Cluster]        # Grouped work items
    superseded: dict[str, SupersededEntry]  # Disappeared findings
```

#### 2. **What `plan show` Displays** (`cmd.py`, lines 18-51)

The `_cmd_plan_show()` function displays a **status summary**:

```
  Living Plan Status
  ──────────────────────────────────────────
  Ordered items:    12
  Skipped:          3 (temp: 1, wontfix: 2, fp: 0)
  Clusters:         4
    cluster1: 15 items — Fix database queries
    cluster2: 8 items (focused)
  Overrides:        47
  Focus:            cluster2
  Superseded:       22
```

**What each metric means:**

- **"Ordered items: 12"** = `len(plan["queue_order"])` — items the user explicitly reordered (this list controls priority ranking in queue/next commands)
- **"Skipped: 3"** = `len(plan["skipped"])` — items explicitly deferred (temporary, permanent/wontfix, or false-positive)
- **"Clusters: 4"** = `len(plan["clusters"])` — named task groupings user created
- **"Overrides: 47"** = `len(plan["overrides"])` — This is the confusing number (explained below)
- **"Superseded: 22"** = `len(plan["superseded"])` — findings that disappeared after a scan

#### 3. **What "Overrides" Actually Represents**

**The core issue:** The term "overrides" is misleading. It's not a "count of changes to defaults" — it's a **metadata store** that tracks:

- **Descriptions** added via `plan describe` (lines 216-226 in operations.py)
- **Notes** added via `plan note` (lines 229-239 in operations.py)
- **Cluster membership** recorded when items are added to clusters (lines 292-297 in operations.py)

**The problem:** When you run `add_to_cluster()`, it **automatically creates an override entry** for every item in the cluster:

```python
# From operations.py, add_to_cluster()
for fid in finding_ids:
    if fid not in member_ids:
        member_ids.append(fid)
        count += 1
    # Update override to track cluster membership
    overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
    overrides[fid]["cluster"] = cluster_name
    overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
```

So if you have 50 items in a single auto-cluster, you automatically get 50 override entries just from cluster membership tracking. This is why the number is so large.

**In reconciliation** (`reconcile.py`, lines 78-81): Override notes are preserved when findings disappear:

```python
# Preserve any existing override note when superseding
override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(finding_id)
if override and override.get("note"):
    entry["note"] = override["note"]
```

#### 4. **What `plan queue` Displays** (`queue_render.py`)

This is a **prioritized work queue table**, completely different from `plan show`:

```
  Queue: 87 items  (T1:12 T2:34 T3:35 T4:6)
  Focus: cluster2

  #    Tier  Detector           Summary                                      Cluster
  1    T1    unused_variable    Fix unused var in app.py                     cluster1
  2    T2    copy_paste         Duplicate code block found                   
  3    T2    legacy_pattern     Old API usage in utils.py                    cluster2
  ... (top 30 by default)

  ... and 57 more (use --top 0 to show all)
```

**Key differences from `plan show`:**
- Shows actual **queue items** after reordering by `queue_order`
- Displays **tier distribution** (T1/T2/T3/T4)
- Shows **individual findings** with detector + summary
- Can filter by tier, cluster, or scope
- Can include/exclude skipped items
- **Respects the plan's ordering** — items in `queue_order` appear first

#### 5. **Where Overrides Are Used** (`_work_queue/core.py`, lines 86-99)

When building the work queue, overrides are **applied as annotations**:

```python
# Annotate items with plan metadata
for item_id, item in by_id.items():
    override = overrides.get(item_id, {})
    if override.get("description"):
        item["plan_description"] = override["description"]
    if override.get("note"):
        item["plan_note"] = override["note"]
    if override.get("cluster"):
        cluster_name = override["cluster"]
        # Add cluster info to item
        item["plan_cluster"] = {...}
```

So overrides are a **metadata lookup table** used to enrich queue items with descriptions, notes, and cluster membership info.

#### 6. **What `purge_ids()` Does** (`operations.py`, lines 406-434)

The `purge_ids()` function **removes finding IDs entirely** from the plan:

```python
def purge_ids(plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]) -> int:
    """Remove finding IDs from the plan entirely.
    
    Cleans queue_order, skipped, and all cluster memberships.
    Does NOT touch overrides (descriptions/notes are kept for history).
    """
    # Removes from:
    # - queue_order
    # - skipped
    # - cluster.finding_ids
    # 
    # Does NOT remove from overrides (preserves history)
```

This is used during reconciliation to clean up IDs that no longer exist in the state.

### Key Architecture Insights

1. **Overrides are NOT mutable state changes** — they're **metadata annotations** that accumulate as you describe/note/cluster items
2. **Cluster membership is bidirectional:** recorded both in `clusters[name].finding_ids` AND in `overrides[id].cluster`
3. **Superseded entries** are created when findings disappear from state after a scan (reconciliation)
4. **Skipped items** can be temporary (resurface after N scans), permanent (wontfix), or false-positive
5. **Queue ordering** is deterministic: items in `queue_order` come first, then remaining items sorted by tier/score

### Recommendations for UX Clarity

1. **Rename `overrides` to `metadata`** in the display and/or internally
2. **Hide or explain the large "Overrides" number** — users won't understand why it says "4311" when they only described 5 items
3. **Show override breakdown** instead of raw count:
   ```
   Overrides:        47
     - With descriptions: 5
     - With notes: 8
     - With cluster membership: 34
   ```
4. **Make `plan show` focus on "human intent":**
   - Ordered items (reordered by user)
   - Skipped items (explicitly deferred)
   - Clusters (grouped work)
   - Active focus (what we're working on)
   
   Don't show superseded/overrides unless asked for deep diagnostics

5. **Keep `plan queue` as the "what do I work on next" view** — it's already clear and useful
