Based on my thorough code inspection, here's the complete trace of ALL the ways a user can complete/dismiss a finding:

## Summary: Finding Completion/Dismissal Paths

There are **5 distinct completion/dismissal mechanisms**, serving different purposes:

### 1. **`resolve fixed` (or `resolve false_positive`)**
**File:** `desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` → `cmd_resolve()`

**What it does:**
- Calls `state_mod.resolve_findings(state, pattern, status, note, attestation)` for each pattern
- Updates the state finding's `status` field to the specified status (`fixed`, `false_positive`, `wontfix`, or `open`)
- Records `resolution_attestation` metadata with kind `"manual"`
- If status is `wontfix`, captures a snapshot of the finding's tier/confidence/detail at that moment
- Recomputes state stats + validates invariants
- **Then:** Attempts to purge the resolved IDs from the living plan (if exists) via `purge_ids(plan, all_resolved)`
- Finally, calls `save_state()` and optionally `save_plan()`

**Arguments:**
```bash
desloppify resolve {open|fixed|wontfix|false_positive} <patterns> \
  --note "explanation" \
  --attest "I have actually ... not gaming" \
  [--confirm-batch-wontfix]
```

**Validation:**
- For `status != "open"`: requires valid `--attest`
- For `wontfix`: requires `--note` (enforced in `_validate_resolve_inputs()`)
- For batch wontfix (>10 items): requires `--confirm-batch-wontfix` unless already provided

---

### 2. **`plan done` (plan subcommand)**
**File:** `desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py` → `cmd_plan_done()`

**What it does:**
- Loads state from disk
- Calls `state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "fixed", attestation=attestation)` for each pattern
- Updates the state finding's `status` field to `"fixed"`
- Records `resolution_attestation` with kind `"manual"`
- Saves state
- Then calls `purge_ids(plan, resolved)` to remove from the plan
- Saves plan

**Arguments:**
```bash
desloppify plan done <patterns> --attest "I have actually ... not gaming"
```

**Validation:**
- Requires valid `--attest`
- **NO `--note` parameter** — note is passed as `None` to `resolve_findings()`

**Key Difference from `resolve fixed`:**
- Cannot specify a note
- No batch-wontfix confirmation needed
- Entry point is plan subcommand, not top-level resolve

---

### 3. **`plan skip --permanent` (with plan-level wontfix)**
**File:** `desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py` → `cmd_plan_skip()`

**What it does:**
1. Determines `kind` as `"permanent"` when `--permanent` flag is set
2. **State layer:** Calls `state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, fid, "wontfix", note, attestation=attestation)` for each finding ID
3. **Plan layer:** Calls `skip_items(plan, finding_ids, kind="permanent", note=note, attestation=attestation, ...)`

The state-layer call:
- Sets finding's `status` to `"wontfix"`
- Records `resolution_attestation` with kind `"manual"`
- Captures `wontfix_snapshot` with tier/confidence/detail

The plan-layer call:
- Moves finding from `queue_order` to `skipped` dict with a `SkipEntry`
- SkipEntry has `kind: "permanent"`, `note`, `attestation`, and metadata

**Arguments:**
```bash
desloppify plan skip <patterns> --permanent --note "explanation" --attest "I have actually ... not gaming"
```

**Validation:**
- For `--permanent`: requires BOTH `--note` AND `--attest`

---

### 4. **`plan skip --false-positive` (with state-layer false_positive)**
**File:** `desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py` → `cmd_plan_skip()`

**What it does:**
1. Determines `kind` as `"false_positive"`
2. **State layer:** Calls `state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, fid, "false_positive", note, attestation=attestation)`
3. **Plan layer:** Calls `skip_items(plan, finding_ids, kind="false_positive", note=note, attestation=attestation, ...)`

State layer sets finding's `status` to `"false_positive"` with `resolution_attestation` kind `"manual"`.
Plan layer moves finding to `skipped` dict with `SkipEntry.kind = "false_positive"`.

**Arguments:**
```bash
desloppify plan skip <patterns> --false-positive --attest "I have actually ... not gaming"
```

**Validation:**
- For `--false-positive`: requires `--attest` (but NOT `--note`)

---

### 5. **`plan skip` (temporary, default)**
**File:** `desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py` → `cmd_plan_skip()`

**What it does:**
1. Determines `kind` as `"temporary"` (default when neither `--permanent` nor `--false-positive`)
2. **State layer:** NO state mutation (note line 123: `if kind in ("permanent", "false_positive")`)
3. **Plan layer:** Calls `skip_items(plan, finding_ids, kind="temporary", reason=reason, review_after=review_after, ...)`

The finding's state status remains `"open"` — this is purely a queue/plan-level operation.
Moves finding from `queue_order` to `skipped` dict with `SkipEntry.kind = "temporary"`.
Can optionally set `review_after=N` to auto-resurface after N scans.

**Arguments:**
```bash
desloppify plan skip <patterns> [--reason "..."] [--review-after N]
```

**Validation:**
- No attestation required
- No note required
- Optional `--reason` for documentation
- Optional `--review-after N` for temporary expiry

---

### 6. **`ignore` (pattern-based suppression)**
**File:** `desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` → `cmd_ignore_pattern()`

**What it does:**
- Adds pattern to config's `ignore` list
- Calls `state_mod.remove_ignored_findings(state, pattern)` which:
  - Marks matching findings as `suppressed: True`
  - Sets `suppression_pattern` metadata
  - Does **NOT** change `status` field
  - Recomputes stats
- Records attestation log entry
- Sets `config["needs_rescan"] = True`
- Saves both config and state

**Arguments:**
```bash
desloppify ignore "file_pattern_or_detector" --attest "I have actually ... not gaming"
```

**Validation:**
- Requires valid `--attest`

**Key Difference:**
- Findings remain with their original status but are marked suppressed
- Next scan will re-detect unless the pattern is still suppressed
- Affects scoring (suppressed findings are filtered out)

---

## Key Distinctions & Design Questions

### `resolve fixed X` vs `plan done X`
**Are they functionally identical for marking as fixed?**

Looking at the code:
- `resolve fixed`: calls `resolve_findings(state, pattern, "fixed", note=args.note, attestation=attestation)` → then `purge_ids()`
- `plan done`: calls `resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "fixed", note=None, attestation=attestation)` → then `purge_ids()`

**Difference:** `resolve fixed` allows `--note`, `plan done` doesn't.

**Design question:** Should they be unified? The note is optional for fixed findings (unlike wontfix), so this is a minor UX difference. The plan subcommand version is "plan-aware" (enters from plan context), while resolve is the main path.

---

### `resolve wontfix` vs `plan skip --permanent`
**Are they functionally identical?**

Looking at code:
- `resolve wontfix`: calls `resolve_findings(..., "wontfix", args.note, attestation)` → `purge_ids()`
- `plan skip --permanent`: calls `resolve_findings(..., "wontfix", note, attestation)` AND `skip_items(..., kind="permanent", note=note, attestation=attestation)` → saves plan

**State mutation:** Both set `status: "wontfix"` with `resolution_attestation` and `wontfix_snapshot`.

**Plan mutation:** 
- `resolve wontfix` removes from plan entirely (via `purge_ids()`)
- `plan skip --permanent` keeps metadata in `skipped` dict with `SkipEntry.kind = "permanent"`

**Design question:** Why keep permanent skips in the skipped dict if they're also marked as wontfix in state? The skipped entry preserves the note + attestation + when it was marked, providing a durable record in the plan.

---

### `resolve false_positive X` vs `plan skip --false-positive X`
**Are they functionally identical?**

- Both set `status: "false_positive"` in state
- Both record `resolution_attestation`
- `resolve false_positive` → `purge_ids()` (removes from plan)
- `plan skip --false-positive` → keeps in `skipped` dict with `kind: "false_positive"`

**Same distinction as wontfix/permanent above.**

---

### Note Requirements
| Command | --note | Behavior |
|---------|--------|----------|
| `resolve fixed` | Optional | Passed to state; used in query output |
| `resolve false_positive` | Optional | Passed to state |
| `resolve wontfix` | **Required** | Enforced in `_validate_resolve_inputs()` |
| `plan done` | Not available | Always `None` |
| `plan skip --permanent` | **Required** | Enforced in `cmd_plan_skip()` |
| `plan skip --false-positive` | Optional | Passed to state + skipped entry |
| `plan skip` (temporary) | Not applicable | Only for understanding, not stored in state |

---

### Attestation Requirements
| Command | --attest | Behavior |
|---------|----------|----------|
| `resolve fixed` | **Required** | Validated; must have "I have actually" + "not gaming" |
| `resolve false_positive` | **Required** | Same |
| `resolve wontfix` | **Required** | Same |
| `plan done` | **Required** | Same |
| `plan skip --permanent` | **Required** | Same |
| `plan skip --false-positive` | **Required** | Same |
| `plan skip` (temporary) | Not required | No validation |
| `ignore` | **Required** | Same |

---

### Batch Wontfix Confirmation
Only applies to `resolve wontfix` (not `plan skip --permanent`):
- If >10 findings match and `--confirm-batch-wontfix` is NOT set, command exits with warning
- Calculates estimated strict-score debt

---

## Should They Be Unified?

**Arguments for unification:**
1. Core mutations are identical: both set status + resolution_attestation in state
2. Note behavior could be standardized (make optional everywhere, or required everywhere)
3. Reduces cognitive load and CLI surface area

**Arguments against:**
1. Different entry points suggest different workflows:
   - `resolve` is the main path for marking findings as done without plan context
   - `plan done/skip` is for findings you're working on from within the plan
2. Plan-aware commands preserve metadata in skipped dict (useful for reasoning about historical decisions)
3. Temporary skip has no state-layer equivalent (purely plan metadata)

---

## Recommendation

Consider these refinements:

1. **Make `resolve fixed --note` more discoverable** — users might not know they can add notes to fixed findings
2. **Clarify when to use `resolve` vs `plan done`** in documentation:
   - `resolve` = bulk operations or one-off resolutions
   - `plan done` = working from within the plan/dashboard
3. **Standardize note handling**: Either require `--note` everywhere (for wontfix/false_positive) or make it optional everywhere
4. **Consider consolidating temporary skip into resolve** — a `resolve deferred --review-after N` might be clearer than `plan skip` (temporary)

The current design isn't broken, but the plan/resolve distinction could be clearer in UX and documentation.

> DEVELOPER

I need to design targeted fixes for three UX problems in the desloppify CLI. Here's the full context from exploration:

## Problem 1: `resolve fixed` vs `plan done` confusion

Every test agent flagged this as the #1 confusion point. The research shows:

- `resolve fixed X --note "..." --attest "..."` — updates state (status→fixed), then best-effort purges from plan
- `plan done X --attest "..."` — updates state (status→fixed, note=None), then purges from plan
- They perform IDENTICAL state mutations. The only difference: `resolve` accepts `--note`, `plan done` doesn't.
- `plan done` also has a bug (now fixed) where it called undefined `_validate_attestation`.

There are also overlapping wontfix paths:
- `resolve wontfix X` — sets status→wontfix, purges from plan entirely
- `plan skip --permanent X` — sets status→wontfix, keeps metadata in plan's skipped dict

And overlapping false_positive paths:
- `resolve false_positive X` — sets status→false_positive, purges from plan
- `plan skip --false-positive X` — sets status→false_positive, keeps in plan's skipped dict

**Key insight**: `plan done` is just `resolve fixed` without `--note`. They're redundant.

**Options:**
A. **Kill `plan done`** — just have `resolve` be the one way to mark things done. The `next` output already suggests `resolve`. Remove `plan done` subcommand (or make it a thin alias that calls resolve).
B. **Make `plan done` an alias** — internally delegate to `resolve fixed`, adding `--note` support.
C. **Communication fix only** — keep both, but add help text explaining the relationship and when to use which.

I lean toward B — make `plan done` a thin wrapper that calls the same resolve logic with `--note` support. This way the plan workflow sequence still reads naturally (plan → focus → next → done) while there's only one code path. The help text should say "Shorthand for `resolve fixed` within the plan workflow."

## Problem 2: Header count mismatch in `plan queue`

Currently: `Queue: 266 items (T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)` — the 266 is collapsed entries, but the tier numbers sum to 813 (individual findings within clusters).

**Root cause**: `tier_counts()` in ranking.py unpacks cluster members and counts each finding individually. But `len(items)` counts collapsed queue entries.

**Fix**: Change the header to show both numbers clearly:
```
  Queue: 266 entries (813 findings: T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)
```
This requires only a 3-line change in `queue_render.py`.

## Problem 3: `plan show` displays opaque metadata

Currently shows:
```
  Ordered items:    12
  Overrides:        4311
  Superseded:       22
```

Research revealed:
- "Ordered items: 12" = items user explicitly reordered (queue_order list). Fine but needs explanation.
- "Overrides: 4311" = metadata annotations (descriptions + notes + cluster membership tracking). The huge number is because every cluster member gets an override entry for cluster tracking. This number is meaningless to users.
- "Superseded: 22" = findings that disappeared after scan reconciliation. Useful but jargon.

**Fix**: 
- Don't show raw overrides count — it's an implementation detail. Instead show counts of items with descriptions and notes.
- Rename "Superseded" to something clearer like "Gone (disappeared after rescan)"
- Add brief inline explanations

New output:
```
  Living Plan Status
  ──────────────────────────────────────────
  Queue order:      12 items manually prioritized
  Skipped:          3 (temp: 1, wontfix: 2, fp: 0)
  Clusters:         4
    ...
  Annotations:      5 described, 8 noted
  Focus:            cluster2
  Disappeared:      22 (resolved or removed since last scan)
```

## Files involved

- `desloppify/app/commands/plan/cmd.py` — plan show display, plan done dispatch
- `desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py` — cmd_plan_done implementation
- `desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py` — queue header
- `desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_admin.py` — plan done parser, plan help text
- `desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` — resolve implementation (for reference)
- `desloppify/engine/_work_queue/ranking.py` — tier_counts (for reference, don't change)

Please design a concrete implementation plan covering all three problems. Be specific about what changes in each file.

```
   205→    )
   206→    from desloppify.engine.plan import purge_ids
   207→    from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
   208→
   209→    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   210→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   211→
   212→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   213→        show_attestation_requirement("Done", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
   214→        return
   215→
   216→    state_file = state_path(args)
   217→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   218→
   219→    resolved: list[str] = []
   220→    for pattern in patterns:
   221→        resolved.extend(
   222→            state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "fixed", attestation=attestation)
   223→        )
   224→
   225→    if not resolved:
   226→        print(colorize("  No open findings matching: " + " ".join(patterns), "yellow"))
   227→        return
   228→
   229→    state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)
   230→
   231→    plan = load_plan()
   232→    purged = purge_ids(plan, resolved)
   233→    if purged:
   234→        save_plan(plan)
   235→
   236→    print(colorize(f"  Marked {len(resolved)} finding(s) as fixed.", "green"))
   237→    if purged:
   238→        print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: {purged} item(s) purged.", "dim"))
   239→
   240→
   241→def cmd_plan_reopen(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   242→    """Reopen resolved findings from plan context."""
   243→    from desloppify import state as state_mod
   244→    from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
   245→
   246→    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
   247→
   248→    state_file = state_path(args)
   249→    state_data = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
   250→
   251→    reopened: list[str] = []
   252→    for pattern in patterns:
   253→        reopened.extend(
   254→            state_mod.resolve_findings(state_data, pattern, "open")
   255→        )
   256→
   257→    if not reopened:
   258→        print(colorize("  No resolved findings matching: " + " ".join(patterns), "yellow"))
   259→        return
   260→
   261→    state_mod.save_state(state_data, state_file)
   262→
   263→    # Remove from skipped if present, and ensure all reopened IDs are in queue
   264→    plan = load_plan()
   265→    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
   266→    count = 0
   267→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   268→    for fid in reopened:
   269→        if fid in skipped:
   270→            skipped.pop(fid)
   271→            count += 1
   272→        if fid not in order:
   273→            plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   274→            order.add(fid)
   275→            count += 1
   276→    if count:
   277→        save_plan(plan)
   278→
   279→    print(colorize(f"  Reopened {len(reopened)} finding(s).", "green"))
   280→    if count:
   281→        print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: items moved back to queue.", "dim"))
   282→
   283→
   284→def cmd_plan_focus(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   285→    """Set or clear the active cluster focus."""
   286→    clear_flag = getattr(args, "clear", False)
   287→    cluster_name: str | None = getattr(args, "cluster_name", None)
   288→
   289→    plan = load_plan()
   290→    if clear_flag:
   291→        clear_focus(plan)
   292→        save_plan(plan)
   293→        print(colorize("  Focus cleared.", "green"))
   294→        return
   295→
   296→    if not cluster_name:
   297→        active = plan.get("active_cluster")
   298→        if active:
   299→            print(f"  Focused on: {active}")
   300→        else:
   301→            print("  No active focus.")
   302→        return
   303→
   304→    try:
   305→        set_focus(plan, cluster_name)
   306→    except ValueError as ex:
   307→        print(colorize(f"  {ex}", "red"))
   308→        return
   309→    save_plan(plan)
   310→    print(colorize(f"  Focused on: {cluster_name}", "green"))
   311→
   312→
   313→__all__ = [
   314→    "cmd_plan_describe",
   315→    "cmd_plan_done",
   316→    "cmd_plan_focus",
   317→    "cmd_plan_note",
   318→    "cmd_plan_reopen",
   319→    "cmd_plan_skip",
   320→    "cmd_plan_unskip",
   321→]
   322→
```

```
    40→def _save_state_or_exit(state: dict, state_file: str) -> None:
    41→    """Persist state with a consistent CLI error boundary."""
    42→    try:
    43→        state_mod.save_state(state, state_file)
    44→    except OSError as exc:
    45→        print_error(f"could not save state: {exc}")
    46→        sys.exit(1)

    49→def _save_config_or_exit(config: dict) -> None:
    50→    """Persist config with a consistent CLI error boundary."""

    52→        config_mod.save_config(config)
    53→    except OSError as exc:
    54→        print_error(f"could not save config: {exc}")
    55→        sys.exit(1)

    58→def cmd_resolve(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    59→    """Resolve finding(s) matching one or more patterns."""
    60→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    61→    _validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)
    62→
    63→    state_file = state_path(args)
    64→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    65→    _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation(
    66→        state,
    67→        args,
    68→        attestation=attestation,
    69→        resolve_all_patterns_fn=_resolve_all_patterns,
    70→    )
    71→    prev = _previous_score_snapshot(state)
    72→    prev_subjective_scores = {
    73→        str(dim): (coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0)
    74→        for dim, payload in (state.get("subjective_assessments") or {}).items()
    75→        if isinstance(dim, str)
    76→    }
    77→
    78→    all_resolved = _resolve_all_patterns(state, args, attestation=attestation)
    79→    if not all_resolved:
    80→        status_label = "resolved" if args.status == "open" else "open"
    81→        print(
    82→            colorize(
    83→                f"No {status_label} findings matching: {' '.join(args.patterns)}",
    84→                "yellow",
    85→            )
    86→        )
    87→        return
    88→
    89→    _save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)
    90→
    91→    # Remove resolved items from the living plan queue (best-effort)
    92→    try:
    93→        from desloppify.engine.plan import has_living_plan, load_plan, purge_ids, save_plan
    94→
    95→        if has_living_plan():
    96→            plan = load_plan()
    97→            purged = purge_ids(plan, all_resolved)
    98→            if purged:
    99→                save_plan(plan)
   100→                print(colorize(f"  Plan updated: {purged} item(s) removed from queue.", "dim"))
   101→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
   102→        print(colorize("  Warning: could not update living plan.", "yellow"), file=sys.stderr)
   103→
   104→    _print_resolve_summary(status=args.status, all_resolved=all_resolved)
   105→    _print_wontfix_batch_warning(

   107→        status=args.status,
   108→        resolved_count=len(all_resolved),
   109→    )
   110→    has_review_findings = any(
   111→        state["findings"].get(fid, {}).get("detector") == "review"
   112→        for fid in all_resolved
   113→    )
   114→    _print_score_movement(

   138→

   140→    _write_resolve_query_entry(

   157→def cmd_ignore_pattern(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   158→    """Add a pattern to the ignore list."""
   159→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   160→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
   161→        show_attestation_requirement("Ignore", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
   162→        sys.exit(1)
   163→
   164→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   165→    state_file = runtime.state_path
   166→    state = runtime.state
   167→    prev = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
   168→
   169→    config = runtime.config
   170→    config_mod.add_ignore_pattern(config, args.pattern)
   171→    config["needs_rescan"] = True
   172→    _save_config_or_exit(config)
   173→
   174→    removed = state_mod.remove_ignored_findings(state, args.pattern)
   175→    state.setdefault("attestation_log", []).append(
   176→        {
   177→            "timestamp": state.get("last_scan"),
   178→            "command": "ignore",
   179→            "pattern": args.pattern,
   180→            "attestation": attestation,
   181→            "affected": removed,
   182→        }
   183→    )
   184→    _save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)
   185→
   186→    print(colorize(f"Added ignore pattern: {args.pattern}", "green"))
   187→    if removed:
   188→        print(f"  Removed {removed} matching findings from state.")

   190→    if config_warning:
   191→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
   192→    print_score_update(state, prev)
   193→

   195→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   196→    narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(
   197→        state,
   198→        context=narrative_mod.NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="ignore"),
   199→    )
   200→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
   201→    write_query(

   205→            "removed": removed,
```

```
    14→    def __call__(self, parser, namespace, values, option_string=None):

   422→def _fixer_help_lines(langs: list[str]) -> list[str]:
   423→    fixer_help_lines: list[str] = []
   424→    for lang_name in langs:
   425→        try:
   426→            fixer_names = sorted(get_lang(lang_name).fixers.keys())
   427→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError):
   428→            fixer_names = []
   429→        fixer_list = ", ".join(fixer_names) if fixer_names else "none yet"
   430→        fixer_help_lines.append(f"fixers ({lang_name}): {fixer_list}")
   431→    fixer_help_lines.append("special: review — prepare structured review data")
   432→    return fixer_help_lines
   433→
```

```
1→"""State-application helpers for resolve command flows."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations

     5→import argparse

     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.query import write_query_best_effort as _write_query_best_effort
     9→from desloppify.core.output_contract import OutputResult

    13→
    14→def _try_expand_cluster(pattern: str) -> list[str] | None:
    15→    """If pattern matches a cluster name, return its finding IDs."""
    16→    try:
    17→        from desloppify.engine.plan import has_living_plan, load_plan
    18→
    19→        if not has_living_plan():
    20→            return None
    21→        plan = load_plan()
    22→        cluster = plan.get("clusters", {}).get(pattern)
    23→        if cluster and cluster.get("finding_ids"):
    24→            return list(cluster["finding_ids"])
    25→    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError):
    26→        pass
    27→    return None
    28→
    29→
    30→def _resolve_all_patterns(
    31→    state: dict,
    32→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    33→    *,
    34→    attestation: str | None,
    35→) -> list[str]:
    36→    all_resolved: list[str] = []
    37→    for pattern in args.patterns:
    38→        # Check if pattern is a cluster name — expand to member IDs
    39→        cluster_ids = _try_expand_cluster(pattern)
    40→        if cluster_ids:
    41→            for fid in cluster_ids:
    42→                resolved = state_mod.resolve_findings(
    43→                    state,
    44→                    fid,
    45→                    args.status,
    46→                    args.note,
    47→                    attestation=attestation,
    48→                )
    49→                all_resolved.extend(resolved)
    50→            continue
    51→
    52→        resolved = state_mod.resolve_findings(
    53→            state,
    54→            pattern,
    55→            args.status,
    56→            args.note,
    57→            attestation=attestation,
    58→        )
    59→        all_resolved.extend(resolved)
    60→    return all_resolved
    61→
    62→
    63→def write_query(payload: dict) -> OutputResult:
    64→    """Backward-compatible resolve query writer seam."""
    65→    return _write_query_best_effort(
    66→        payload,
    67→        context="resolve query payload update",
    68→    )
    69→
    70→
    71→def _write_resolve_query_entry(context: ResolveQueryContext) -> None:
    72→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(context.state)
    73→    write_query(
    74→        {
    75→            "command": "resolve",
    76→            "patterns": context.patterns,
    77→            "status": context.status,
    78→            "resolved": context.resolved,
    79→            "count": len(context.resolved),
    80→            "next_command": context.next_command,
    81→            "overall_score": scores.overall,
    82→            "objective_score": scores.objective,
    83→            "strict_score": scores.strict,
    84→            "verified_strict_score": scores.verified,
    85→            "prev_overall_score": context.prev_overall,
    86→            "prev_objective_score": context.prev_objective,
    87→            "prev_strict_score": context.prev_strict,
    88→            "prev_verified_strict_score": context.prev_verified,
    89→            "attestation": context.attestation,
    90→            "narrative": context.narrative,
    91→        }
    92→    )
    93→
```

```
1→"""Validation and preflight helpers for resolve command flows."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import copy
     7→import sys
     8→from dataclasses import dataclass
     9→
    10→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    11→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    12→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    13→
    14→_REQUIRED_ATTESTATION_PHRASES = ("i have actually", "not gaming")
    15→_ATTESTATION_KEYWORD_HINT = ("I have actually", "not gaming")
    16→
    17→
    18→def _emit_warning(message: str) -> None:
    19→    """Write resolve preflight warnings to stderr consistently."""
    20→    print(colorize(message, "yellow"), file=sys.stderr)
    21→
    22→
    23→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    24→class ResolveQueryContext:
    25→    patterns: list[str]
    26→    status: str
    27→    resolved: list[str]
    28→    next_command: str
    29→    prev_overall: float | None
    30→    prev_objective: float | None
    31→    prev_strict: float | None
    32→    prev_verified: float | None
    33→    attestation: str | None
    34→    narrative: dict
    35→    state: dict
    36→
    37→
    38→def _missing_attestation_keywords(attestation: str | None) -> list[str]:
    39→    normalized = " ".join((attestation or "").strip().lower().split())
    40→    return [
    41→        phrase for phrase in _REQUIRED_ATTESTATION_PHRASES if phrase not in normalized
    42→    ]
    43→
    44→
    45→def validate_attestation(attestation: str | None) -> bool:
    46→    return not _missing_attestation_keywords(attestation)
    47→
    48→
    49→def show_attestation_requirement(
    50→    label: str,
    51→    attestation: str | None,
    52→    example: str,
    53→    *,
    54→    patterns: list[str] | None = None,
    55→    status: str | None = None,
    56→) -> None:
    57→    missing = _missing_attestation_keywords(attestation)
    58→    if not attestation:
    59→        _emit_warning(f"{label} requires --attest.")
    60→    elif missing:
    61→        missing_str = ", ".join(f"'{keyword}'" for keyword in missing)
    62→        _emit_warning(
    63→            f"{label} attestation is missing required keyword(s): {missing_str}."
    64→        )
    65→    _emit_warning(
    66→        f"Required keywords: '{_ATTESTATION_KEYWORD_HINT[0]}' and '{_ATTESTATION_KEYWORD_HINT[1]}'."
    67→    )
    68→    print(colorize(f'Example: --attest "{example}"', "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    69→    if patterns and status:
    70→        pattern_args = " ".join(f'"{p}"' for p in patterns)
    71→        print(
    72→            colorize(
    73→                f'\nCopy-paste:\n  desloppify resolve {status} {pattern_args} --attest "{example}"',
    74→                "dim",
    75→            ),
    76→            file=sys.stderr,
    77→        )
    78→
    79→
    80→def _validate_resolve_inputs(args: argparse.Namespace, attestation: str | None) -> None:
    81→    if args.status == "wontfix" and not args.note:
    82→        _emit_warning(
    83→            "Wontfix items become technical debt. Add --note to record your reasoning for future review."
    84→        )
    85→        sys.exit(1)
    86→    if args.status == "open":
    87→        return
    88→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
    89→        show_attestation_requirement(
    90→            "Manual resolve",
    91→            attestation,
    92→            ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
    93→            patterns=getattr(args, "patterns", None),
    94→            status=args.status,
    95→        )
    96→        sys.exit(1)
    97→
    98→
    99→def _previous_score_snapshot(state: dict) -> state_mod.ScoreSnapshot:
   100→    """Load a score snapshot for comparison after resolve operations."""
   101→    return state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
   102→
   103→
   104→def _preview_resolve_count(state: dict, patterns: list[str]) -> int:
   105→    """Count unique open findings matching the provided patterns."""
   106→    matched_ids: set[str] = set()
   107→    for pattern in patterns:
   108→        for finding in state_mod.match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter="open"):
   109→            finding_id = finding.get("id")
   110→            if finding_id:
   111→                matched_ids.add(finding_id)
   112→    return len(matched_ids)
   113→
   114→
   115→def _estimate_wontfix_strict_delta(
   116→    state: dict,
   117→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   118→    *,
   119→    attestation: str | None,
   120→    resolve_all_patterns_fn,
   121→) -> float:
   122→    """Estimate strict score drop if this resolve command is applied as wontfix."""
   123→    before = state_mod.get_strict_score(state)
   124→    if before is None:
   125→        return 0.0
   126→
   127→    preview_state = copy.deepcopy(state)
   128→    resolve_all_patterns_fn(preview_state, args, attestation=attestation)
   129→    after = state_mod.get_strict_score(preview_state)
   130→    if after is None:
   131→        return 0.0
   132→    return max(0.0, before - after)
   133→
   134→
   135→def _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation(
   136→    state: dict,
   137→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   138→    *,
   139→    attestation: str | None,
   140→    resolve_all_patterns_fn,
   141→) -> None:
   142→    if args.status != "wontfix":
   143→        return
   144→
   145→    preview_count = _preview_resolve_count(state, args.patterns)
   146→    if preview_count <= 10:
   147→        return
   148→    if getattr(args, "confirm_batch_wontfix", False):
   149→        return
   150→
   151→    strict_delta = _estimate_wontfix_strict_delta(
   152→        state,
   153→        args,
   154→        attestation=attestation,
   155→        resolve_all_patterns_fn=resolve_all_patterns_fn,
   156→    )
   157→    _emit_warning(f"Large wontfix batch detected ({preview_count} findings).")
   158→    if strict_delta > 0:
   159→        _emit_warning(f"Estimated strict-score debt added now: {strict_delta:.1f} points.")
   160→    _emit_warning(
   161→        "Re-run with --confirm-batch-wontfix if this debt is intentional."
   162→    )
   163→    sys.exit(1)
   164→
```

```
1→"""Plan schema types, defaults, and validation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any, Required, TypedDict
     6→
     7→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
     8→
     9→PLAN_VERSION = 2
    10→
    11→VALID_SKIP_KINDS = {"temporary", "permanent", "false_positive"}
    12→
    13→
    14→class SkipEntry(TypedDict, total=False):
    15→    finding_id: Required[str]
    16→    kind: Required[str]  # "temporary" | "permanent" | "false_positive"
    17→    reason: str | None
    18→    note: str | None  # required for permanent (wontfix note)
    19→    attestation: str | None  # required for permanent/false_positive
    20→    created_at: str
    21→    review_after: int | None  # re-surface after N scans (temporary only)
    22→    skipped_at_scan: int  # state.scan_count when skipped
    23→
    24→
    25→class ItemOverride(TypedDict, total=False):
    26→    finding_id: Required[str]
    27→    description: str | None
    28→    note: str | None
    29→    cluster: str | None
    30→    created_at: str
    31→    updated_at: str
    32→
    33→
    34→class Cluster(TypedDict, total=False):
    35→    name: Required[str]
    36→    description: str | None
    37→    finding_ids: list[str]
    38→    created_at: str
    39→    updated_at: str
    40→    auto: bool  # True for auto-generated clusters
    41→    cluster_key: str  # Deterministic grouping key (for regeneration)
    42→    action: str | None  # Primary resolution command/guidance text
    43→    user_modified: bool  # True when user manually edits membership
    44→
    45→
    46→class SupersededEntry(TypedDict, total=False):
    47→    original_id: Required[str]
    48→    original_detector: str
    49→    original_file: str
    50→    original_summary: str
    51→    status: str  # "superseded" | "remapped" | "dismissed"
    52→    superseded_at: str
    53→    remapped_to: str | None
    54→    candidates: list[str]
    55→    note: str | None
    56→
    57→
    58→class PlanModel(TypedDict, total=False):
    59→    version: Required[int]
    60→    created: Required[str]
    61→    updated: Required[str]
    62→    queue_order: list[str]
    63→    deferred: list[str]  # kept empty for migration compat
    64→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry]
    65→    active_cluster: str | None
    66→    overrides: dict[str, ItemOverride]
    67→    clusters: dict[str, Cluster]
    68→    superseded: dict[str, SupersededEntry]
    69→
    70→
    71→def empty_plan() -> PlanModel:
    72→    """Return a new empty plan payload."""
    73→    now = utc_now()
    74→    return {
    75→        "version": PLAN_VERSION,
    76→        "created": now,
    77→        "updated": now,
    78→        "queue_order": [],
    79→        "deferred": [],
    80→        "skipped": {},
    81→        "active_cluster": None,
    82→        "overrides": {},
    83→        "clusters": {},
    84→        "superseded": {},
    85→    }
    86→
    87→
    88→def ensure_plan_defaults(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    89→    """Normalize a loaded plan to ensure all keys exist.
    90→
    91→    Handles migration from v1 (deferred list) to v2 (skipped dict).
    92→    """
    93→    defaults = empty_plan()
    94→    for key, value in defaults.items():
    95→        plan.setdefault(key, value)
    96→
    97→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
    98→        plan["queue_order"] = []
    99→    if not isinstance(plan.get("deferred"), list):
   100→        plan["deferred"] = []
   101→    if not isinstance(plan.get("skipped"), dict):
   102→        plan["skipped"] = {}
   103→    if not isinstance(plan.get("overrides"), dict):
   104→        plan["overrides"] = {}
   105→    if not isinstance(plan.get("clusters"), dict):
   106→        plan["clusters"] = {}
   107→    if not isinstance(plan.get("superseded"), dict):
   108→        plan["superseded"] = {}
   109→
   110→    # Migrate deferred → skipped (v1 → v2)
   111→    deferred: list[str] = plan["deferred"]
   112→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   113→    if deferred:
   114→        now = utc_now()
   115→        for fid in list(deferred):
   116→            if fid not in skipped:
   117→                skipped[fid] = {
   118→                    "finding_id": fid,
   119→                    "kind": "temporary",
   120→                    "reason": None,
   121→                    "note": None,
   122→                    "attestation": None,
   123→                    "created_at": now,
   124→                    "review_after": None,
   125→                    "skipped_at_scan": 0,
   126→                }
   127→        deferred.clear()
   128→
   129→    # Normalize cluster fields
   130→    for cluster in plan["clusters"].values():
   131→        if isinstance(cluster, dict):
   132→            if not isinstance(cluster.get("finding_ids"), list):
   133→                cluster["finding_ids"] = []
   134→            cluster.setdefault("auto", False)
   135→            cluster.setdefault("cluster_key", "")
   136→            cluster.setdefault("action", None)
   137→            cluster.setdefault("user_modified", False)
   138→
   139→
   140→def validate_plan(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   141→    """Raise ValueError when plan invariants are violated."""
   142→    if not isinstance(plan.get("version"), int):
   143→        raise ValueError("plan.version must be an int")
   144→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
   145→        raise ValueError("plan.queue_order must be a list")
   146→
   147→    # No ID should appear in both queue_order and skipped
   148→    skipped_ids = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   149→    overlap = set(plan["queue_order"]) & skipped_ids
   150→    if overlap:
   151→        raise ValueError(
   152→            f"IDs cannot appear in both queue_order and skipped: {sorted(overlap)}"
   153→        )
   154→
   155→    # Validate skip entry kinds
   156→    for fid, entry in plan.get("skipped", {}).items():
   157→        kind = entry.get("kind")
   158→        if kind not in VALID_SKIP_KINDS:
   159→            raise ValueError(
   160→                f"Invalid skip kind {kind!r} for {fid}; must be one of {sorted(VALID_SKIP_KINDS)}"
   161→            )
   162→
   163→
   164→__all__ = [
   165→    "PLAN_VERSION",
   166→    "Cluster",
   167→    "ItemOverride",
   168→    "PlanModel",
   169→    "SkipEntry",
   170→    "SupersededEntry",
   171→    "VALID_SKIP_KINDS",
   172→    "empty_plan",
   173→    "ensure_plan_defaults",
   174→    "validate_plan",
   175→]
   176→
```

```
1→"""Plan persistence — load/save with atomic writes."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import shutil
     8→import sys
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→
    11→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import safe_write_text
    12→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
    13→    PLAN_VERSION,
    14→    PlanModel,
    15→    empty_plan,
    16→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    17→    validate_plan,
    18→)
    19→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import STATE_DIR, json_default
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→PLAN_FILE = STATE_DIR / "plan.json"
    24→
    25→
    26→def load_plan(path: Path | None = None) -> PlanModel:
    27→    """Load plan from disk, or return empty plan on missing/corruption."""
    28→    plan_path = path or PLAN_FILE
    29→    if not plan_path.exists():
    30→        return empty_plan()
    31→
    32→    try:
    33→        data = json.loads(plan_path.read_text())
    34→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError) as ex:
    35→        print(f"  Warning: Plan file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    36→        return empty_plan()
    37→
    38→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    39→        print("  Warning: Plan file root must be a JSON object. Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    40→        return empty_plan()
    41→
    42→    version = data.get("version", 1)
    43→    if version > PLAN_VERSION:
    44→        print(
    45→            f"  Warning: Plan file version {version} is newer than supported "
    46→            f"({PLAN_VERSION}). Some features may not work correctly.",
    47→            file=sys.stderr,
    48→        )
    49→
    50→    ensure_plan_defaults(data)
    51→    try:
    52→        validate_plan(data)
    53→    except ValueError as ex:
    54→        print(f"  Warning: Plan invariants invalid ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    55→        return empty_plan()
    56→
    57→    return data  # type: ignore[return-value]
    58→
    59→
    60→def save_plan(plan: PlanModel | dict, path: Path | None = None) -> None:
    61→    """Validate and save plan to disk atomically."""
    62→    from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    63→
    64→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
    65→    plan["updated"] = utc_now()
    66→    validate_plan(plan)
    67→
    68→    plan_path = path or PLAN_FILE
    69→    plan_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    70→
    71→    content = json.dumps(plan, indent=2, default=json_default) + "\n"
    72→
    73→    if plan_path.exists():
    74→        backup = plan_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    75→        try:
    76→            shutil.copy2(str(plan_path), str(backup))
    77→        except OSError as backup_ex:
    78→            logger.debug("Failed to create plan backup: %s", backup_ex)
    79→
    80→    try:
    81→        safe_write_text(plan_path, content)
    82→    except OSError as ex:
    83→        print(f"  Warning: Could not save plan: {ex}", file=sys.stderr)
    84→        raise
    85→
    86→
    87→def plan_path_for_state(state_path: Path) -> Path:
    88→    """Derive plan.json path from a state file path."""
    89→    return state_path.parent / "plan.json"
    90→
    91→
    92→def has_living_plan(path: Path | None = None) -> bool:
    93→    """Return True if a plan.json exists and has user intent."""
    94→    plan_path = path or PLAN_FILE
    95→    if not plan_path.exists():
    96→        return False
    97→    plan = load_plan(plan_path)
    98→    return bool(
    99→        plan.get("queue_order")
   100→        or plan.get("overrides")
   101→        or plan.get("clusters")
   102→    )
   103→
   104→
   105→__all__ = [
   106→    "PLAN_FILE",
   107→    "has_living_plan",
   108→    "load_plan",
   109→    "plan_path_for_state",
   110→    "save_plan",
   111→]
   112→
```

```
1→"""Compact queue table renderer for ``plan queue``."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import require_completed_scan
     9→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize, print_table
    10→from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan
    11→from desloppify.engine.work_queue import QueueBuildOptions, build_work_queue
    12→
    13→
    14→def _truncate(text: str, width: int) -> str:
    15→    if len(text) <= width:
    16→        return text
    17→    return text[: width - 1] + "\u2026"
    18→
    19→
    20→def _cluster_tier_label(item: dict) -> str:
    21→    """Compute a tier label from cluster members, e.g. 'T2' or 'T1-T3'."""
    22→    members = item.get("members", [])
    23→    if not members:
    24→        return ""
    25→    tiers = {int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) for m in members}
    26→    lo, hi = min(tiers), max(tiers)
    27→    if lo == hi:
    28→        return f"T{lo}"
    29→    return f"T{lo}-T{hi}"
    30→
    31→
    32→def cmd_plan_queue(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    33→    """Render a compact table of all upcoming queue items."""
    34→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    35→    state = runtime.state
    36→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    37→        return
    38→
    39→    top = getattr(args, "top", 30)
    40→    tier_filter = getattr(args, "tier", None)
    41→    cluster_filter = getattr(args, "cluster", None)
    42→    include_skipped = bool(getattr(args, "include_skipped", False))
    43→
    44→    plan = load_plan()
    45→    plan_data: dict | None = None
    46→    if plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("overrides") or plan.get("clusters"):
    47→        plan_data = plan
    48→
    49→    # Auto-scope to focus cluster
    50→    effective_cluster = cluster_filter
    51→    if plan_data and not cluster_filter:
    52→        active_cluster = plan_data.get("active_cluster")
    53→        if active_cluster:
    54→            effective_cluster = active_cluster
    55→
    56→    queue = build_work_queue(
    57→        state,
    58→        options=QueueBuildOptions(
    59→            tier=tier_filter,
    60→            count=None,
    61→            scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
    62→            status="open",
    63→            include_subjective=True,
    64→            plan=plan_data,
    65→            include_skipped=include_skipped,
    66→            cluster=effective_cluster,
    67→            collapse_clusters=True,
    68→        ),
    69→    )
    70→    items = queue.get("items", [])
    71→    tier_counts = queue.get("tier_counts", {})
    72→
    73→    # Header
    74→    total = len(items)
    75→    skipped_count = sum(1 for it in items if it.get("plan_skipped"))
    76→    non_skipped = total - skipped_count
    77→    tc_parts = " ".join(f"T{t}:{tier_counts.get(t, 0)}" for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))
    78→    print(colorize(f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} items  ({tc_parts})", "bold"))
    79→
    80→    focus = plan.get("active_cluster") if plan_data else None
    81→    if focus:
    82→        print(colorize(f"  Focus: {focus}", "cyan"))
    83→
    84→    if not include_skipped and skipped_count == 0:
    85→        # Check plan for skipped items not in queue
    86→        plan_skipped_count = len(plan.get("skipped", {})) if plan_data else 0
    87→        if plan_skipped_count:
    88→            print(colorize(
    89→                f"  ({plan_skipped_count} skipped item{'s' if plan_skipped_count != 1 else ''}"
    90→                " hidden \u2014 use --include-skipped)",
    91→                "dim",
    92→            ))
    93→
    94→    if not items:
    95→        print(colorize("\n  Queue is empty.", "green"))
    96→        return
    97→
    98→    # Determine which items to show
    99→    display_items = items
   100→    if top > 0 and len(items) > top:
   101→        display_items = items[:top]
   102→
   103→    # Build rows
   104→    headers = ["#", "Tier", "Detector", "Summary", "Cluster"]
   105→    rows: list[list[str]] = []
   106→    for idx, item in enumerate(display_items, 1):
   107→        pos = str(idx)
   108→        kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
   109→
   110→        if kind == "cluster":
   111→            tier_str = _cluster_tier_label(item)
   112→            member_count = item.get("member_count", 0)
   113→            detector = item.get("detector", "cluster")
   114→            summary = f"[{member_count} items] {item.get('summary', '')}"
   115→            cluster_name = item.get("cluster_name", item.get("id", ""))
   116→        else:
   117→            tier_val = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   118→            tier_str = f"T{tier_val}"
   119→            detector = item.get("detector", "")
   120→            summary = item.get("summary", "")
   121→            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   122→            cluster_name = plan_cluster.get("name", "") if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else ""
   123→
   124→        suffix = ""
   125→        if item.get("plan_skipped"):
   126→            suffix = " [skip]"
   127→
   128→        summary_display = _truncate(summary, 48) + suffix
   129→        rows.append([pos, tier_str, detector, summary_display, cluster_name])
   130→
   131→    print()
   132→    widths = [4, 4, 12, 50, 16]
   133→    print_table(headers, rows, widths=widths)
   134→
   135→    if top > 0 and len(items) > top:
   136→        remaining = len(items) - top
   137→        print(colorize(
   138→            f"\n  ... and {remaining} more (use --top 0 to show all)", "dim"
   139→        ))
   140→    print()
   141→
   142→
   143→__all__ = ["cmd_plan_queue"]
   144→
```

```
1→"""Ranking and grouping helpers for work queue selection."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import (
     6→    detail_dict,
     7→    is_review_finding,
     8→    is_subjective_finding,
     9→    primary_command_for_finding,
    10→    review_finding_weight,
    11→    scope_matches,
    12→    slugify,
    13→    status_matches,
    14→    subjective_strict_scores,
    15→    supported_fixers_for_item,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.engine.planning.common import CONFIDENCE_ORDER
    18→from desloppify.state import path_scoped_findings
    19→
    20→
    21→def subjective_score_value(item: dict) -> float:
    22→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
    23→        detail = detail_dict(item)
    24→        return float(detail.get("strict_score", item.get("subjective_score", 100.0)))
    25→    return float(item.get("subjective_score", 100.0))
    26→
    27→
    28→def build_finding_items(
    29→    state: dict,
    30→    *,
    31→    scan_path: str | None,
    32→    status_filter: str,
    33→    scope: str | None,
    34→    chronic: bool,
    35→) -> list[dict]:
    36→    scoped = path_scoped_findings(state.get("findings", {}), scan_path)
    37→    subjective_scores = subjective_strict_scores(state)
    38→    out: list[dict] = []
    39→
    40→    for finding_id, finding in scoped.items():
    41→        if finding.get("suppressed"):
    42→            continue
    43→        if not status_matches(finding.get("status", "open"), status_filter):
    44→            continue
    45→        if chronic and not (
    46→            finding.get("status") == "open" and finding.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2
    47→        ):
    48→            continue
    49→
    50→        item = dict(finding)
    51→        item["id"] = finding_id
    52→        item["kind"] = "finding"
    53→        item["is_review"] = is_review_finding(item)
    54→        item["is_subjective"] = is_subjective_finding(item)
    55→        item["effective_tier"] = (
    56→            4 if item["is_subjective"] else int(finding.get("tier", 3))
    57→        )
    58→        item["review_weight"] = (
    59→            review_finding_weight(item) if item["is_review"] else None
    60→        )
    61→        subjective_score = None
    62→        if item["is_subjective"]:
    63→            detail = detail_dict(finding)
    64→            dim_name = detail.get("dimension_name", "")
    65→            dim_key = detail.get("dimension", "") or slugify(dim_name)
    66→            subjective_score = subjective_scores.get(
    67→                dim_key, subjective_scores.get(dim_name.lower(), 100.0)
    68→            )
    69→        item["subjective_score"] = subjective_score
    70→        supported_fixers = supported_fixers_for_item(state, item)
    71→        item["primary_command"] = primary_command_for_finding(
    72→            item,
    73→            supported_fixers=supported_fixers,
    74→        )
    75→
    76→        if not scope_matches(item, scope):
    77→            continue
    78→        out.append(item)
    79→
    80→    return out
    81→
    82→
    83→_CLUSTER_ACTION_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "reorganize": 1, "refactor": 2, "manual_fix": 3}
    84→
    85→
    86→def item_sort_key(item: dict) -> tuple:
    87→    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
    88→        # Clusters sort before individual findings, ordered by action type
    89→        # (auto_fix first, then reorganize, refactor, manual_fix),
    90→        # then bigger clusters first within the same action type.
    91→        action_pri = _CLUSTER_ACTION_PRIORITY.get(
    92→            item.get("action_type", "manual_fix"), 3
    93→        )
    94→        return (
    95→            0,  # All clusters before all individual findings
    96→            action_pri,
    97→            -int(item.get("member_count", 0)),
    98→            item.get("id", ""),
    99→        )
   100→
   101→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
   102→        return (
   103→            int(item.get("effective_tier", 4)),
   104→            1,  # Subjective items sort after mechanical items within T4.
   105→            subjective_score_value(item),
   106→            item.get("id", ""),
   107→        )
   108→
   109→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   110→    review_weight = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   111→    return (
   112→        int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))),
   113→        0,
   114→        CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(item.get("confidence", "low"), 9),
   115→        -review_weight,
   116→        -int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   117→        item.get("id", ""),
   118→    )
   119→
   120→
   121→def item_explain(item: dict) -> dict:
   122→    effective_tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   123→
   124→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
   125→        return {
   126→            "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   127→            "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   128→            "subjective_score": subjective_score_value(item),
   129→            "policy": (
   130→                "Subjective dimensions are always queued as T4 and do not outrank "
   131→                "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   132→            ),
   133→            "ranking_factors": ["tier asc", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"],
   134→        }
   135→
   136→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   137→    confidence = item.get("confidence", "low")
   138→    is_subjective = bool(item.get("is_subjective"))
   139→    is_review = bool(item.get("is_review"))
   140→    ranking_factors: list[str]
   141→    if is_subjective:
   142→        ranking_factors = ["tier fixed to T4", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"]
   143→    elif is_review:
   144→        ranking_factors = [
   145→            "tier asc",
   146→            "confidence asc",
   147→            "review_weight desc",
   148→            "count desc",
   149→            "id asc",
   150→        ]
   151→    else:
   152→        ranking_factors = ["tier asc", "confidence asc", "count desc", "id asc"]
   153→    explain = {
   154→        "kind": "finding",
   155→        "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   156→        "confidence": confidence,
   157→        "confidence_rank": CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(confidence, 9),
   158→        "count": int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   159→        "id": item.get("id", ""),
   160→        "ranking_factors": ranking_factors,
   161→    }
   162→    if is_review:
   163→        explain["review_weight"] = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   164→    if is_subjective:
   165→        explain["policy"] = (
   166→            "Subjective findings are forced to T4 and do not outrank "
   167→            "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   168→        )
   169→        explain["subjective_score"] = subjective_score_value(item)
   170→    return explain
   171→
   172→
   173→def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
   174→    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
   175→    for item in items:
   176→        if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
   177→            # Count each member at its own tier
   178→            for member in item.get("members", []):
   179→                tier = int(member.get("effective_tier", member.get("tier", 3)))
   180→                counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
   181→            continue
   182→        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   183→        counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
   184→    return counts
   185→
   186→
   187→def choose_fallback_tier(requested_tier: int, counts: dict[int, int]) -> int | None:
   188→    available = [tier for tier, count in counts.items() if count > 0]
   189→    if not available:
   190→        return None
   191→    return min(available, key=lambda tier: (abs(tier - requested_tier), tier))
   192→
   193→
   194→def group_queue_items(items: list[dict], group: str) -> dict[str, list[dict]]:
   195→    """Group queue items for alternate output modes."""
   196→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   197→    for item in items:
   198→        if group == "file":
   199→            key = item.get("file", "")
   200→        elif group == "detector":
   201→            key = item.get("detector", "")
   202→        elif group == "tier":
   203→            key = f"T{int(item.get('effective_tier', item.get('tier', 3)))}"
   204→        elif group == "cluster":
   205→            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   206→            key = plan_cluster["name"] if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else "(unclustered)"
   207→        else:
   208→            key = "items"
   209→        grouped.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
   210→    return grouped
   211→
   212→
   213→__all__ = [
   214→    "build_finding_items",
   215→    "choose_fallback_tier",
   216→    "item_explain",
   217→    "item_sort_key",
   218→    "tier_counts",
   219→    "subjective_score_value",
   220→    "group_queue_items",
   221→]
   222→
```

```
1→"""Pure mutation operations on the plan model."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
     6→    Cluster,
     7→    PlanModel,
     8→    SkipEntry,
     9→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    10→)
    11→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    12→
    13→
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→# Helpers
    16→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17→
    18→def _remove_id_from_lists(plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str) -> None:
    19→    """Remove a finding ID from queue_order and skipped."""
    20→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    21→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
    22→    if finding_id in order:
    23→        order.remove(finding_id)
    24→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    25→
    26→
    27→def _resolve_position(
    28→    order: list[str],
    29→    position: str,
    30→    target: str | None = None,
    31→    offset: int | None = None,
    32→    finding_ids: list[str] | None = None,
    33→) -> int:
    34→    """Resolve a position specifier to an insertion index.
    35→
    36→    *finding_ids* are the IDs being moved — used to calculate relative
    37→    positions when they already exist in the list.
    38→    """
    39→    moving = set(finding_ids or [])
    40→
    41→    if position == "top":
    42→        return 0
    43→    if position == "bottom":
    44→        return len(order)
    45→
    46→    if position == "before" and target:
    47→        for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
    48→            if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
    49→                return i
    50→        return 0
    51→
    52→    if position == "after" and target:
    53→        for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
    54→            if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
    55→                return i + 1
    56→        return len(order)
    57→
    58→    if position == "up" and offset is not None:
    59→        if not finding_ids:
    60→            return 0
    61→        # Find current position of first item
    62→        first_id = finding_ids[0]
    63→        current_idx = None
    64→        clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
    65→        for i, item_id in enumerate(clean_order):
    66→            if item_id == first_id:
    67→                current_idx = i
    68→                break
    69→        if current_idx is None:
    70→            # Item not yet in order; treat as "insert at position offset from top"
    71→            return max(0, len(clean_order) - offset)
    72→        return max(0, current_idx - offset)
    73→
    74→    if position == "down" and offset is not None:
    75→        if not finding_ids:
    76→            return len(order)
    77→        first_id = finding_ids[0]
    78→        clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
    79→        current_idx = None
    80→        for i, item_id in enumerate(clean_order):
    81→            if item_id == first_id:
    82→                current_idx = i
    83→                break
    84→        if current_idx is None:
    85→            return len(clean_order)
    86→        return min(len(clean_order), current_idx + offset)
    87→
    88→    return len(order)
    89→
    90→
    91→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    92→# Move items
    93→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    94→
    95→def move_items(
    96→    plan: PlanModel,
    97→    finding_ids: list[str],
    98→    position: str,
    99→    target: str | None = None,
   100→    offset: int | None = None,
   101→) -> int:
   102→    """Move finding IDs to a position in queue_order. Returns count moved."""
   103→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   104→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   105→
   106→    # Remove from skipped if present
   107→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
   108→    for fid in finding_ids:
   109→        skipped.pop(fid, None)
   110→
   111→    # Remove from current position in order
   112→    for fid in finding_ids:
   113→        if fid in order:
   114→            order.remove(fid)
   115→
   116→    # Resolve insertion point
   117→    idx = _resolve_position(order, position, target, offset, finding_ids)
   118→
   119→    # Insert in original order
   120→    for i, fid in enumerate(finding_ids):
   121→        order.insert(idx + i, fid)
   122→
   123→    return len(finding_ids)
   124→
   125→
   126→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   127→# Skip / unskip
   128→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   129→
   130→def skip_items(
   131→    plan: PlanModel,
   132→    finding_ids: list[str],
   133→    *,
   134→    kind: str = "temporary",
   135→    reason: str | None = None,
   136→    note: str | None = None,
   137→    attestation: str | None = None,
   138→    review_after: int | None = None,
   139→    scan_count: int = 0,
   140→) -> int:
   141→    """Move finding IDs to the skipped dict. Returns count skipped."""
   142→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   143→    now = utc_now()
   144→    count = 0
   145→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   146→    for fid in finding_ids:
   147→        _remove_id_from_lists(plan, fid)
   148→        skipped[fid] = {
   149→            "finding_id": fid,
   150→            "kind": kind,
   151→            "reason": reason,
   152→            "note": note,
   153→            "attestation": attestation,
   154→            "created_at": now,
   155→            "review_after": review_after,
   156→            "skipped_at_scan": scan_count,
   157→        }
   158→        count += 1
   159→    return count
   160→
   161→
   162→def unskip_items(
   163→    plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]
   164→) -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
   165→    """Bring finding IDs back from skipped to the end of queue_order.
   166→
   167→    Returns ``(count_unskipped, permanent_ids_needing_state_reopen)``
   168→    where the second list contains IDs that were permanent or false_positive
   169→    and need their state-layer status reopened by the caller.
   170→    """
   171→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   172→    count = 0
   173→    need_reopen: list[str] = []
   174→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   175→    for fid in finding_ids:
   176→        entry = skipped.pop(fid, None)
   177→        if entry is not None:
   178→            if entry.get("kind") in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   179→                need_reopen.append(fid)
   180→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   181→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   182→            count += 1
   183→    return count, need_reopen
   184→
   185→
   186→def resurface_stale_skips(
   187→    plan: PlanModel, current_scan_count: int
   188→) -> list[str]:
   189→    """Move temporary skips past their review_after threshold back to queue.
   190→
   191→    Returns list of resurfaced finding IDs.
   192→    """
   193→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   194→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   195→    resurfaced: list[str] = []
   196→    for fid in list(skipped):
   197→        entry = skipped[fid]
   198→        if entry.get("kind") != "temporary":
   199→            continue
   200→        review_after = entry.get("review_after")
   201→        if review_after is None:
   202→            continue
   203→        skipped_at = entry.get("skipped_at_scan", 0)
   204→        if current_scan_count >= skipped_at + review_after:
   205→            skipped.pop(fid)
   206→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   207→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   208→            resurfaced.append(fid)
   209→    return resurfaced
   210→
   211→
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→# Describe / annotate
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→
   216→def describe_finding(
   217→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, description: str | None
   218→) -> None:
   219→    """Set or clear an augmented description on a finding."""
   220→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   221→    now = utc_now()
   222→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   223→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   224→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}
   225→    overrides[finding_id]["description"] = description
   226→    overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
   227→
   228→
   229→def annotate_finding(
   230→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, note: str | None
   231→) -> None:
   232→    """Set or clear a note on a finding."""
   233→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   234→    now = utc_now()
   235→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   236→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   237→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}
   238→    overrides[finding_id]["note"] = note
   239→    overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
   240→
   241→
   242→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   243→# Clusters
   244→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   245→
   246→def create_cluster(
   247→    plan: PlanModel,
   248→    name: str,
   249→    description: str | None = None,
   250→    action: str | None = None,
   251→) -> Cluster:
   252→    """Create a named cluster. Raises ValueError if it already exists."""
   253→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   254→    if name.startswith("auto/"):
   255→        raise ValueError(
   256→            f"Cluster names starting with 'auto/' are reserved for auto-clusters: {name!r}"
   257→        )
   258→    if name in plan["clusters"]:
   259→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} already exists")
   260→    now = utc_now()
   261→    cluster: Cluster = {
   262→        "name": name,
   263→        "description": description,
   264→        "finding_ids": [],
   265→        "created_at": now,
   266→        "updated_at": now,
   267→        "auto": False,
   268→        "cluster_key": "",
   269→        "action": action,
   270→        "user_modified": False,
   271→    }
   272→    plan["clusters"][name] = cluster
   273→    return cluster
   274→
   275→
   276→def add_to_cluster(
   277→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   278→) -> int:
   279→    """Add finding IDs to a cluster. Returns count added."""
   280→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   281→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   282→    if cluster is None:
   283→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   284→
   285→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   286→    count = 0
   287→    now = utc_now()
   288→    for fid in finding_ids:
   289→        if fid not in member_ids:
   290→            member_ids.append(fid)
   291→            count += 1
   292→        # Update override to track cluster membership
   293→        overrides = plan["overrides"]
   294→        if fid not in overrides:
   295→            overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   296→        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = cluster_name
   297→        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   298→
   299→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   300→    return count
   301→
   302→
   303→def remove_from_cluster(
   304→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   305→) -> int:
   306→    """Remove finding IDs from a cluster. Returns count removed."""
   307→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   308→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   309→    if cluster is None:
   310→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   311→
   312→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   313→    now = utc_now()
   314→    count = 0
   315→    for fid in finding_ids:
   316→        if fid in member_ids:
   317→            member_ids.remove(fid)
   318→            count += 1
   319→        # Clear cluster from override
   320→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   321→        if override and override.get("cluster") == cluster_name:
   322→            override["cluster"] = None
   323→            override["updated_at"] = now
   324→
   325→    # Mark auto-clusters as user_modified when items are manually removed
   326→    if count > 0 and cluster.get("auto"):
   327→        cluster["user_modified"] = True
   328→
   329→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   330→    return count
   331→
   332→
   333→def delete_cluster(plan: PlanModel, name: str) -> list[str]:
   334→    """Delete a cluster and clear cluster refs from overrides. Returns orphaned IDs."""
   335→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   336→    cluster = plan["clusters"].pop(name, None)
   337→    if cluster is None:
   338→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} does not exist")
   339→
   340→    orphaned = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   341→    now = utc_now()
   342→    for fid in orphaned:
   343→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   344→        if override and override.get("cluster") == name:
   345→            override["cluster"] = None
   346→            override["updated_at"] = now
   347→
   348→    if plan.get("active_cluster") == name:
   349→        plan["active_cluster"] = None
   350→
   351→    return orphaned
   352→
   353→
   354→def move_cluster(
   355→    plan: PlanModel,
   356→    cluster_name: str,
   357→    position: str,
   358→    target: str | None = None,
   359→    offset: int | None = None,
   360→) -> int:
   361→    """Move all cluster members as a contiguous block. Returns count moved."""
   362→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   363→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   364→    if cluster is None:
   365→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   366→
   367→    member_ids = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   368→    if not member_ids:
   369→        return 0
   370→
   371→    return move_items(plan, member_ids, position, target, offset)
   372→
   373→
   374→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   375→# Focus
   376→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   377→
   378→def set_focus(plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str) -> None:
   379→    """Set the active cluster focus."""
   380→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   381→    if cluster_name not in plan["clusters"]:
   382→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   383→    plan["active_cluster"] = cluster_name
   384→
   385→
   386→def clear_focus(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   387→    """Clear the active cluster focus."""
   388→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   389→    plan["active_cluster"] = None
   390→
   391→
   392→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   393→# Reset
   394→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   395→
   396→def reset_plan(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   397→    """Reset plan to empty state, preserving version and created timestamp."""
   398→    created = plan.get("created", utc_now())
   399→    plan.clear()
   400→    from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan as _empty
   401→    for k, v in _empty().items():
   402→        plan[k] = v
   403→    plan["created"] = created
   404→
   405→
   406→def purge_ids(plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]) -> int:
   407→    """Remove finding IDs from the plan entirely.
   408→
   409→    Cleans queue_order, skipped, and all cluster memberships.
   410→    Does NOT touch overrides (descriptions/notes are kept for history).
   411→    Returns count of IDs that were actually present somewhere.
   412→    """
   413→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   414→    found = 0
   415→
   416→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   417→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   418→    for fid in finding_ids:
   419→        was_present = False
   420→        if fid in order:
   421→            order.remove(fid)
   422→            was_present = True
   423→        if fid in skipped:
   424→            skipped.pop(fid)
   425→            was_present = True
   426→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   427→            ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   428→            if fid in ids:
   429→                ids.remove(fid)
   430→                was_present = True
   431→        if was_present:
   432→            found += 1
   433→
   434→    return found
   435→
   436→
   437→__all__ = [
   438→    "add_to_cluster",
   439→    "annotate_finding",
   440→    "clear_focus",
   441→    "create_cluster",
   442→    "delete_cluster",
   443→    "describe_finding",
   444→    "move_cluster",
   445→    "move_items",
   446→    "purge_ids",
   447→    "remove_from_cluster",
   448→    "reset_plan",
   449→    "resurface_stale_skips",
   450→    "set_focus",
   451→    "skip_items",
   452→    "unskip_items",
   453→]
   454→
```
