```
1→"""Terminal rendering helpers for resolve command flows."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
     9→
    10→
    11→def _print_resolve_summary(*, status: str, all_resolved: list[str]) -> None:
    12→    verb = "Reopened" if status == "open" else "Resolved"
    13→    print(colorize(f"\n{verb} {len(all_resolved)} finding(s) as {status}:", "green"))
    14→    for fid in all_resolved[:20]:
    15→        print(f"  {fid}")
    16→    if len(all_resolved) > 20:
    17→        print(f"  ... and {len(all_resolved) - 20} more")
    18→
    19→
    20→def _print_wontfix_batch_warning(
    21→    state: dict,
    22→    *,
    23→    status: str,
    24→    resolved_count: int,
    25→) -> None:
    26→    if status != "wontfix" or resolved_count <= 10:
    27→        return
    28→    wontfix_count = sum(
    29→        1 for finding in state["findings"].values() if finding["status"] == "wontfix"
    30→    )
    31→    actionable = sum(
    32→        1
    33→        for finding in state["findings"].values()
    34→        if finding["status"]
    35→        in ("open", "wontfix", "fixed", "auto_resolved", "false_positive")
    36→    )
    37→    wontfix_pct = round(wontfix_count / actionable * 100) if actionable else 0
    38→    print(
    39→        colorize(
    40→            f"\n  ⚠ Wontfix debt is now {wontfix_count} findings ({wontfix_pct}% of actionable).",
    41→            "yellow",
    42→        )
    43→    )
    44→    print(
    45→        colorize(
    46→            '    The strict score reflects this. Run `desloppify show "*" --status wontfix` to review.',
    47→            "dim",
    48→        )
    49→    )
    50→
    51→
    52→def _delta_suffix(delta: float) -> str:
    53→    if abs(delta) < 0.05:
    54→        return ""
    55→    return f" ({'+' if delta > 0 else ''}{delta:.1f})"
    56→
    57→
    58→def _print_score_movement(
    59→    *,
    60→    status: str,
    61→    prev_overall: float | None,
    62→    prev_objective: float | None,
    63→    prev_strict: float | None,
    64→    prev_verified: float | None,
    65→    state: dict,
    66→    has_review_findings: bool = False,
    67→) -> None:
    68→    new = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    69→    if (
    70→        new.overall is None
    71→        or new.objective is None
    72→        or new.strict is None
    73→        or new.verified is None
    74→    ):
    75→        print(colorize("\n  Scores unavailable — run `desloppify scan`.", "yellow"))
    76→        return
    77→
    78→    overall_delta = new.overall - (prev_overall or 0)
    79→    objective_delta = new.objective - (prev_objective or 0)
    80→    strict_delta = new.strict - (prev_strict or 0)
    81→    verified_delta = new.verified - (prev_verified or 0)
    82→    print(
    83→        f"\n  Scores: overall {new.overall:.1f}/100{_delta_suffix(overall_delta)}"
    84→        + colorize(
    85→            f"  objective {new.objective:.1f}/100{_delta_suffix(objective_delta)}",
    86→            "dim",
    87→        )
    88→        + colorize(f"  strict {new.strict:.1f}/100{_delta_suffix(strict_delta)}", "dim")
    89→        + colorize(
    90→            f"  verified {new.verified:.1f}/100{_delta_suffix(verified_delta)}", "dim"
    91→        )
    92→    )
    93→    if status == "wontfix":
    94→        strict_gap = round(new.overall - new.strict, 1)
    95→        if strict_gap > 0:
    96→            print(
    97→                colorize(
    98→                    f"  Note: wontfix items still count against strict score. "
    99→                    f"Current gap: overall {new.overall:.1f} vs strict {new.strict:.1f} ({strict_gap:.1f} pts of hidden debt).",
   100→                    "yellow",
   101→                )
   102→            )
   103→    if has_review_findings and abs(overall_delta) < 0.05:
   104→        print(
   105→            colorize(
   106→                "  Scores unchanged (review findings don't affect scores directly).",
   107→                "yellow",
   108→            )
   109→        )
   110→        print(
   111→            colorize(
   112→                "  Run `desloppify review --prepare` to get updated assessment scores.",
   113→                "dim",
   114→            )
   115→        )
   116→    elif status == "fixed":
   117→        print(
   118→            colorize(
   119→                "  Verified score updates after a scan confirms the finding disappeared.",
   120→                "yellow",
   121→            )
   122→        )
   123→
   124→
   125→def _print_subjective_reset_hint(
   126→    *,
   127→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   128→    state: dict,
   129→    all_resolved: list[str],
   130→    prev_subjective_scores: dict[str, float],
   131→) -> None:
   132→    has_review = any(
   133→        state["findings"].get(fid, {}).get("detector") == "review"
   134→        for fid in all_resolved
   135→    )
   136→    if not has_review or not state.get("subjective_assessments"):
   137→        return
   138→
   139→    stale_dims = sorted(
   140→        dim
   141→        for dim in {
   142→            str(
   143→                state["findings"].get(fid, {}).get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")
   144→            ).strip()
   145→            for fid in all_resolved
   146→            if state["findings"].get(fid, {}).get("detector") == "review"
   147→        }
   148→        if dim and dim in (state.get("subjective_assessments") or {})
   149→    )
   150→    if not stale_dims:
   151→        return
   152→
   153→    shown = ", ".join(stale_dims[:3])
   154→    if len(stale_dims) > 3:
   155→        shown = f"{shown}, +{len(stale_dims) - 3} more"
   156→    print(
   157→        colorize(
   158→            f"  Subjective scores unchanged — re-run review for updated scores: {shown}",
   159→            "yellow",
   160→        )
   161→    )
   162→    print(
   163→        colorize(
   164→            "  Next subjective step: "
   165→            + f"`desloppify review --prepare --dimensions {','.join(stale_dims)}`",
   166→            "dim",
   167→        )
   168→    )
   169→
   170→
   171→def _print_next_command(state: dict) -> str:
   172→    remaining = sum(
   173→        1
   174→        for finding in state["findings"].values()
   175→        if finding["status"] == "open"
   176→        and not finding.get("suppressed")
   177→    )
   178→    next_command = "desloppify scan"
   179→    if remaining > 0:
   180→        suffix = "s" if remaining != 1 else ""
   181→        print(
   182→            colorize(
   183→                f"\n  {remaining} finding{suffix} remaining — run `desloppify next`",
   184→                "dim",
   185→            )
   186→        )
   187→        next_command = "desloppify next"
   188→    print(colorize(f"  Next command: `{next_command}`", "dim"))
   189→    print()
   190→    return next_command
   191→
```

```
1→"""Pattern → finding-ID resolution for plan commands."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel
     6→from desloppify.engine._state.resolution import match_findings
     7→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
     8→
     9→
    10→def resolve_ids_from_patterns(
    11→    state: StateModel,
    12→    patterns: list[str],
    13→    *,
    14→    plan: PlanModel | None = None,
    15→    status_filter: str = "open",
    16→) -> list[str]:
    17→    """Resolve one or more patterns to a deduplicated list of finding IDs.
    18→
    19→    When *plan* is provided, literal IDs that exist only in the plan
    20→    (e.g. ``subjective::*`` synthetic items) are included even if they
    21→    have no corresponding entry in ``state["findings"]``.
    22→    """
    23→    seen: set[str] = set()
    24→    result: list[str] = []
    25→
    26→    # Collect all plan IDs for literal-match fallback
    27→    plan_ids: set[str] = set()
    28→    if plan is not None:
    29→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
    30→        plan_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
    31→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    32→            plan_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
    33→
    34→    for pattern in patterns:
    35→        matches = match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter=status_filter)
    36→        if matches:
    37→            for finding in matches:
    38→                fid = finding["id"]
    39→                if fid not in seen:
    40→                    seen.add(fid)
    41→                    result.append(fid)
    42→        elif pattern in plan_ids and pattern not in seen:
    43→            # Literal plan ID (e.g. subjective::foo) not in state findings
    44→            seen.add(pattern)
    45→            result.append(pattern)
    46→    return result
    47→
    48→
    49→__all__ = ["resolve_ids_from_patterns"]
    50→
```

```
1→"""Tests for desloppify.app.commands.plan_cmd — plan generation command."""
     2→
     3→import inspect
     4→
     5→import desloppify.engine.planning.core as plan_mod
     6→from desloppify.app.commands import plan_cmd
```

```
    24→
    25→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    26→class QueueBuildOptions:
    27→    """Configuration for queue construction and tier selection behavior."""
    28→
    29→    tier: int | None = None
    30→    count: int | None = 1
    31→    scan_path: str | None = None
    32→    scope: str | None = None
    33→    status: str = "open"
    34→    include_subjective: bool = True
    35→    subjective_threshold: float = 100.0
    36→    chronic: bool = False
    37→    no_tier_fallback: bool = False
    38→    explain: bool = False
    39→    plan: dict | None = None
    40→    include_skipped: bool = False

    44→
    45→class WorkQueueResult(TypedDict):
    46→    """Typed shape of the dict returned by :func:`build_work_queue`."""
    47→
    48→    items: list[dict]
    49→    total: int
    50→    tier_counts: dict[int, int]
    51→    requested_tier: int | None
    52→    selected_tier: int | None
    53→    fallback_reason: str | None
    54→    available_tiers: list[int]
    55→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]]
    56→
    57→
    58→def _apply_plan_order(
    59→    items: list[dict],
    60→    plan: dict,
    61→    *,
    62→    include_skipped: bool = False,
    63→    cluster: str | None = None,
    64→) -> list[dict]:
    65→    """Reorder items according to the living plan.
    66→
    67→    1. Items in ``queue_order`` appear first, in that order.
    68→    2. Remaining items keep their mechanical sort.
    69→    3. Skipped items are appended last (or excluded).
    70→    4. Each item is annotated with plan metadata.
    71→    """
    72→    queue_order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    73→    skipped_map: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
    74→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(skipped_map.keys())
    75→    overrides: dict = plan.get("overrides", {})
    76→    clusters: dict = plan.get("clusters", {})
    77→    active_cluster = plan.get("active_cluster")
    78→
    79→    # Build lookup
    80→    by_id: dict[str, dict] = {}
    81→    for item in items:
    82→        by_id[item["id"]] = item
    83→
    84→    # Annotate items with plan metadata
    85→    for item_id, item in by_id.items():
    86→        override = overrides.get(item_id, {})
    87→        if override.get("description"):
    88→            item["plan_description"] = override["description"]
    89→        if override.get("note"):
    90→            item["plan_note"] = override["note"]
    91→        if override.get("cluster"):
    92→            cluster_name = override["cluster"]
    93→            cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
    94→            item["plan_cluster"] = {
    95→                "name": cluster_name,
    96→                "description": cluster_data.get("description"),
    97→                "total_items": len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", [])),
    98→                "sibling_ids": cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []),
    99→            }
   100→
   101→    # Split into ordered, remaining, skipped
   102→    ordered: list[dict] = []
   103→    ordered_ids: set[str] = set()
   104→    for fid in queue_order:
   105→        if fid in by_id and fid not in skipped_ids:
   106→            ordered.append(by_id[fid])
   107→            ordered_ids.add(fid)
   108→
   109→    skipped_items: list[dict] = []
   110→    remaining: list[dict] = []
   111→    for item in items:
   112→        item_id = item["id"]
   113→        if item_id in ordered_ids:
   114→            continue
   115→        if item_id in skipped_ids:
   116→            skipped_items.append(item)
   117→        else:
   118→            remaining.append(item)
   119→
   120→    # Assign queue positions
   121→    result = ordered + remaining
   122→    if include_skipped:
   123→        result = result + skipped_items
   124→
   125→    for pos, item in enumerate(result):
   126→        item["queue_position"] = pos + 1
   127→        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
   128→            item["plan_skipped"] = True
   129→            skip_entry = skipped_map.get(item["id"])
   130→            if skip_entry:
   131→                item["plan_skip_kind"] = skip_entry.get("kind", "temporary")
   132→                skip_reason = skip_entry.get("reason")
   133→                if skip_reason:
   134→                    item["plan_skip_reason"] = skip_reason
   135→
   136→    # Filter to cluster if requested
   137→    effective_cluster = cluster or active_cluster
   138→    if effective_cluster:
   139→        cluster_data = clusters.get(effective_cluster, {})
   140→        cluster_member_ids = set(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
   141→        if cluster_member_ids:
   142→            result = [item for item in result if item["id"] in cluster_member_ids]
   143→
   144→    return result
   145→
   146→
   147→def _item_matches_tier(item: dict, tier: int) -> bool:
   148→    """Check if an item (or any of its members for clusters) matches a tier."""
   149→    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
   150→        return any(
   151→            int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) == tier
   152→            for m in item.get("members", [])
   153→        )
   154→    return int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))) == tier
   155→
   156→
   157→def _action_type_for_detector(detector: str) -> str:
   158→    """Look up the action_type for a detector from the registry."""
   159→    try:
   160→        from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
   161→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   162→        if meta:
   163→            return meta.action_type
   164→    except ImportError:
   165→        pass
   166→    return "manual_fix"
   167→
   168→
   169→_ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "reorganize": 1, "refactor": 2, "manual_fix": 3}
   170→
   171→
   172→def _collapse_clusters(items: list[dict], plan: dict) -> list[dict]:
   173→    """Replace cluster member items with single cluster meta-items."""
   174→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   175→    if not clusters:
   176→        return items
   177→
   178→    # Build mapping: finding_id → auto-cluster name
   179→    fid_to_cluster: dict[str, str] = {}
   180→    for name, cluster in clusters.items():
   181→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   182→            continue
   183→        for fid in cluster.get("finding_ids", []):
   184→            fid_to_cluster[fid] = name
   185→
   186→    if not fid_to_cluster:
   187→        return items
   188→
   189→    # Collect members for each cluster, preserving order
   190→    cluster_members: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   191→    non_cluster_items: list[dict] = []
   192→
   193→    for item in items:
   194→        cname = fid_to_cluster.get(item.get("id", ""))
   195→        if cname:
   196→            cluster_members.setdefault(cname, []).append(item)
   197→        else:
   198→            non_cluster_items.append(item)
   199→
   200→    # Build cluster meta-items
   201→    result: list[dict] = list(non_cluster_items)
   202→    for cname, members in cluster_members.items():
   203→        # Don't collapse singletons — show them as individual findings
   204→        if len(members) < 2:
   205→            result.extend(members)
   206→            continue
   207→        cluster_data = clusters.get(cname, {})
   208→        detector = members[0].get("detector", "") if members else ""
   209→        action = cluster_data.get("action") or ""
   210→        # Derive action_type from the actual cluster action, not just the detector
   211→        if "desloppify fix" in action:
   212→            action_type = "auto_fix"
   213→        elif "desloppify move" in action:
   214→            action_type = "reorganize"
   215→        else:
   216→            action_type = _action_type_for_detector(detector)
   217→            # If detector says auto_fix but cluster has no fix command, it's really refactor
   218→            if action_type == "auto_fix" and "desloppify fix" not in action:
   219→                action_type = "refactor"
   220→
   221→        # Use stored description but patch the count if it differs from visible members
   222→        stored_desc = cluster_data.get("description") or ""
   223→        total_in_cluster = len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
   224→        if stored_desc and total_in_cluster != len(members):
   225→            # Replace the stored count with the visible count
   226→            summary = stored_desc.replace(str(total_in_cluster), str(len(members)))
   227→        else:
   228→            summary = stored_desc or f"{len(members)} findings"
   229→
   230→        primary_command = cluster_data.get("action")
   231→        if not primary_command:
   232→            primary_command = f"desloppify next --cluster {cname} --count 10"
   233→
   234→        meta_item: dict = {
   235→            "id": cname,
   236→            "kind": "cluster",
   237→            "action_type": action_type,
   238→            "summary": summary,
   239→            "members": members,
   240→            "member_count": len(members),
   241→            "primary_command": primary_command,
   242→            "cluster_name": cname,
   243→            "cluster_auto": True,
   244→            "confidence": "high",
   245→            "detector": detector,
   246→            "file": "",
   247→        }
   248→        result.append(meta_item)
   249→
   250→    result.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   251→    return result
   252→
   253→
   254→def build_work_queue(
   255→    state: StateModel,
   256→    *,
   257→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
   258→) -> WorkQueueResult:
   259→    """Build ranked queue items + tier metadata."""
   260→    resolved_options = options or QueueBuildOptions()
   261→
   262→    status = resolved_options.status
   263→    if status not in ALL_STATUSES:
   264→        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported status filter: {status}")
   265→    try:
   266→        subjective_threshold_value = float(resolved_options.subjective_threshold)
   267→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   268→        subjective_threshold_value = 100.0
   269→    subjective_threshold_value = max(0.0, min(100.0, subjective_threshold_value))
   270→
   271→    finding_items = build_finding_items(
   272→        state,
   273→        scan_path=resolved_options.scan_path,
   274→        status_filter=status,
   275→        scope=resolved_options.scope,
   276→        chronic=resolved_options.chronic,
   277→    )
   278→
   279→    all_items = list(finding_items)
   280→    if (
   281→        resolved_options.include_subjective
   282→        and status in {"open", "all"}
   283→        and not resolved_options.chronic
   284→    ):
   285→        subjective_items = build_subjective_items(
   286→            state,
   287→            state.get("findings", {}),
   288→            threshold=subjective_threshold_value,
   289→        )
   290→        for item in subjective_items:
   291→            if scope_matches(item, resolved_options.scope):
   292→                all_items.append(item)
   293→
   294→    all_items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   295→
   296→    # Apply living plan ordering if provided
   297→    if resolved_options.plan:
   298→        all_items = _apply_plan_order(
   299→            all_items,
   300→            resolved_options.plan,
   301→            include_skipped=resolved_options.include_skipped,
   302→            cluster=resolved_options.cluster,
   303→        )
   304→
   305→    # Collapse auto-clusters into meta-items (unless drilling into a cluster)
   306→    should_collapse = (
   307→        resolved_options.collapse_clusters
   308→        and resolved_options.plan
   309→        and not resolved_options.cluster
   310→        and not resolved_options.plan.get("active_cluster")
   311→    )
   312→    if should_collapse:
   313→        all_items = _collapse_clusters(all_items, resolved_options.plan)
   314→
   315→    counts = tier_counts(all_items)
   316→
   317→    requested_tier = (
   318→        int(resolved_options.tier) if resolved_options.tier is not None else None
   319→    )
   320→    selected_tier = requested_tier
   321→    fallback_reason = None
   322→    filtered = all_items
   323→
   324→    if requested_tier is not None:
   325→        filtered = [
   326→            item
   327→            for item in all_items
   328→            if _item_matches_tier(item, requested_tier)
   329→        ]
   330→        if not filtered and not resolved_options.no_tier_fallback:
   331→            chosen = choose_fallback_tier(requested_tier, counts)
   332→            if chosen is not None:
   333→                selected_tier = chosen
   334→                filtered = [
   335→                    item
   336→                    for item in all_items
   337→                    if _item_matches_tier(item, chosen)
   338→                ]
   339→                fallback_reason = (
   340→                    f"Requested T{requested_tier} has 0 open -> showing T{chosen} "
   341→                    "(nearest non-empty)."
   342→                )
   343→        elif not filtered:
   344→            fallback_reason = f"Requested T{requested_tier} has 0 open."
   345→
   346→    total = len(filtered)
   347→    if resolved_options.count is not None and resolved_options.count > 0:
   348→        filtered = filtered[: resolved_options.count]
   349→
   350→    if resolved_options.explain:
   351→        for item in filtered:
   352→            item["explain"] = item_explain(item)
   353→
   354→    available_tiers = [tier for tier, value in counts.items() if value > 0]
   355→    return {
   356→        "items": filtered,
   357→        "total": total,
   358→        "tier_counts": counts,
   359→        "requested_tier": requested_tier,
   360→        "selected_tier": selected_tier,
   361→        "fallback_reason": fallback_reason,
   362→        "available_tiers": available_tiers,
   363→        "grouped": group_queue_items(filtered, "item"),
   364→    }
   365→
   366→
   367→__all__ = [
   368→    "ATTEST_EXAMPLE",
   369→    "QueueBuildOptions",
   370→    "WorkQueueResult",
   371→    "build_work_queue",
   372→    "group_queue_items",
   373→]
   374→
```

    41→```
    42→1. desloppify next              ← tells you exactly what to fix next
    43→2. Fix the issue in code
    44→3. desloppify resolve fixed "<id>"
    45→```
    46→
    47→If `next` suggests an auto-fixer, run `desloppify fix <fixer> --dry-run` to preview, then apply.
    48→
    49→**To be strategic**, use `plan` to shape what `next` gives you:
    50→```bash
    51→desloppify plan                        # see the full ordered queue
    52→desloppify plan move <pat> top         # reorder — what unblocks the most?
    53→desloppify plan cluster create <name>  # group related issues to batch-fix
    54→desloppify plan focus <cluster>        # scope next to one cluster
    55→desloppify plan defer <pat>            # push low-value items aside
    56→desloppify plan skip <pat>             # hide from next
    57→desloppify plan done <pat>             # mark complete
    58→desloppify plan reopen <pat>           # reopen
    59→```
    60→
    61→### Subjective reviews
    62→
    63→The scan will prompt you when a subjective review is needed — just follow its instructions.
    64→If you need to trigger one manually:
    65→```bash
    66→desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import
    67→```
    68→
    69→### Other useful commands
    70→
    71→```bash
    72→desloppify next --count 5                         # top 5 priorities
    73→desloppify next --cluster <name>                  # drill into a cluster
    74→desloppify show <pattern>                         # filter by file/detector/ID
    75→desloppify show --status open                     # all open findings
    76→desloppify resolve wontfix "<id>" --note "reason" # accept debt (lowers strict score)
    77→desloppify scan --path . --reset-subjective       # reset subjective baseline to 0

```
1→"""Centralized score update display for all state-changing commands."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     6→from desloppify.app.commands.scan.scan_helpers import format_delta
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.status_parts.strict_target import format_strict_target_progress
     8→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
     9→
    10→
    11→def print_score_update(
    12→    state: dict,
    13→    prev: state_mod.ScoreSnapshot,
    14→    *,
    15→    config: dict | None = None,
    16→    label: str = "Scores",
    17→) -> None:
    18→    """Print score quartet with deltas and strict target progress.
    19→
    20→    Args:
    21→        state: Current state dict (scores already recomputed by save_state).
    22→        prev: ScoreSnapshot taken before the operation.
    23→        config: Project config dict (needed for target_strict_score).
    24→        label: Prefix label (default "Scores").
    25→    """
    26→    new = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    27→    if (
    28→        new.overall is None
    29→        or new.objective is None
    30→        or new.strict is None
    31→        or new.verified is None
    32→    ):
    33→        print(colorize(f"\n  {label} unavailable — run `desloppify scan`.", "yellow"))
    34→        return
    35→
    36→    overall_s, overall_c = format_delta(new.overall, prev.overall)
    37→    objective_s, objective_c = format_delta(new.objective, prev.objective)
    38→    strict_s, strict_c = format_delta(new.strict, prev.strict)
    39→    verified_s, verified_c = format_delta(new.verified, prev.verified)
    40→
    41→    print(
    42→        f"\n  {label}: "
    43→        + colorize(f"overall {new.overall:.1f}/100{overall_s}", overall_c)
    44→        + colorize(f"  objective {new.objective:.1f}/100{objective_s}", objective_c)
    45→        + colorize(f"  strict {new.strict:.1f}/100{strict_s}", strict_c)
    46→        + colorize(f"  verified {new.verified:.1f}/100{verified_s}", verified_c)
    47→    )
    48→
    49→    # Show strict target progress when config is available
    50→    if config is not None:
    51→        from desloppify.intelligence import narrative as narrative_mod
    52→        from desloppify.intelligence.narrative.core import NarrativeContext
    53→
    54→        narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(
    55→            state, context=NarrativeContext(command="score_update"),
    56→        )
    57→        strict_target = narrative.get("strict_target")
    58→        if strict_target:
    59→            lines, _target, _gap = format_strict_target_progress(strict_target)
    60→            for message, style in lines:
    61→                print(colorize(message, style))
    62→
    63→
    64→__all__ = ["print_score_update"]
    65→
```

---

## Implementation Plan: Three UX Fixes for Desloppify CLI

### Problem 1: `plan done` as a Thin Wrapper Around `resolve fixed`

**Current state analysis:**

The current `cmd_plan_done` (at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py`, line 198) has these behavioral gaps compared to `cmd_resolve` (at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py`, line 58):

1. `cmd_plan_done` does NOT call `print_score_update` / `_print_score_movement` -- so the user never sees score deltas.
2. `cmd_plan_done` does NOT call `compute_narrative` -- so milestones are never triggered.
3. `cmd_plan_done` does NOT call `_write_resolve_query_entry` -- so `query.json` is not updated for downstream tool consumption.
4. `cmd_plan_done` does NOT support `--note`.
5. `cmd_plan_done` uses raw `state_mod.resolve_findings()` per-pattern (not per-resolved-ID from pattern expansion via `_resolve_all_patterns`), so it does not support cluster-name expansion.
6. `cmd_plan_done` does NOT call `_print_next_command` to guide the user's next step.

**Approach: Make `plan done` delegate to `cmd_resolve` internally.**

The cleanest path is to have `cmd_plan_done` construct a synthetic `argparse.Namespace` and call `cmd_resolve` directly. This gives us all the resolve output (scores, narrative, query.json, next command hint) with zero duplication.

**File changes:**

**A. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py`**

Replace the entire `cmd_plan_done` function (lines 198-238) with a thin wrapper:

```python
def cmd_plan_done(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Mark findings as fixed (delegates to resolve fixed).

    This is shorthand for ``desloppify resolve fixed <patterns> --attest ...``
    within the plan workflow. Supports all the same flags as resolve.
    """
    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd import cmd_resolve

    # Build a synthetic Namespace that cmd_resolve expects
    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)

    resolve_args = argparse.Namespace(
        status="fixed",
        patterns=patterns,
        note=note,
        attest=attestation,
        confirm_batch_wontfix=False,
        state=getattr(args, "state", None),
        lang=getattr(args, "lang", None),
        path=getattr(args, "path", None),
    )
    cmd_resolve(resolve_args)
```

This removes the direct imports of `state_mod`, `state_path`, `purge_ids`, and `ATTEST_EXAMPLE` from `cmd_plan_done` (they are only needed if the function does its own work). The `validate_attestation` and `show_attestation_requirement` imports at the top of the file can stay since they are used by `cmd_plan_skip`.

**B. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/cli_support/parser_groups_admin.py`**

Update the `plan done` parser (lines 585-596) to add `--note` and update help text:

```python
    # plan done <patterns> --attest "..." [--note "..."]
    p_done = plan_sub.add_parser(
        "done",
        help="Mark findings as fixed (shorthand for `resolve fixed`, requires attestation)",
    )
    p_done.add_argument(
        "patterns", nargs="+", metavar="PATTERN",
        help="Finding ID(s), prefix, detector name, file path, or glob",
    )
    p_done.add_argument(
        "--note", type=str, default=None,
        help="Explanation of what was done (passed to resolve)",
    )
    p_done.add_argument(
        "--attest", type=str, default=None,
        help="Attestation that findings were actually fixed (required)",
    )
```

**C. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/docs/SKILL.md`** (line 57)

Update the comment to clarify the relationship:

```
desloppify plan done <pat> --attest ..   # shorthand for resolve fixed
```

**D. Epilog in parser_groups_admin.py** (line 467)

Update the plan epilog workflow example:

### Problem 2: Header Count Mismatch in `plan queue`

**Current state analysis:**

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py`, line 78:
```python
print(colorize(f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} items  ({tc_parts})", "bold"))
```

Here `non_skipped = len(items) - skipped_count` counts collapsed queue entries, but `tc_parts` uses `tier_counts` from `ranking.py` which unpacks cluster members and counts each finding individually (lines 173-184 in ranking.py).

**Approach: Show both the entry count and the finding count.**

The fix computes a total finding count from `tier_counts` and only shows the dual-number format when they differ (i.e., when clusters are collapsed). This avoids confusing output when there are no clusters.

**File changes:**

**A. `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py`** (lines 73-78)

Replace the header block:

```python
    # Header
    total = len(items)
    skipped_count = sum(1 for it in items if it.get("plan_skipped"))
    non_skipped = total - skipped_count
    tc_parts = " ".join(f"T{t}:{tier_counts.get(t, 0)}" for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))
    finding_total = sum(tier_counts.get(t, 0) for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))

    if finding_total != non_skipped:
        # Collapsed clusters: show both entry count and finding count
        print(colorize(
            f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} entries  ({finding_total} findings: {tc_parts})",
            "bold",
        ))
    else:
        print(colorize(f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} items  ({tc_parts})", "bold"))
```

When clusters are collapsed, output becomes:
```
  Queue: 266 entries  (813 findings: T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)
```

When there are no clusters (or when the numbers match), output stays the same:
```
  Queue: 813 items  (T1:7 T2:107 T3:439 T4:260)
```

### Problem 3: `plan show` Displays Opaque Metadata

**Current state analysis:**

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cmd.py`, `_cmd_plan_show` (lines 18-51) shows:
- `Ordered items: N` -- the raw `len(queue_order)`, which is the count of ALL items ever added to the queue (not just user-reordered ones). Actually, on re-reading the code, `queue_order` is the ordered list of finding IDs. Items get added automatically during reconciliation, so the number can be huge. It is NOT "items the user explicitly reordered."
- `Overrides: N` -- the raw override dict count, which includes cluster membership tracking entries. Meaningless to users.
- `Superseded: N` -- jargon for "findings that disappeared after rescan."

Let me verify what `queue_order` actually contains.

```
1→"""Post-scan plan reconciliation — handle finding churn."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     6→from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel, SupersededEntry, ensure_plan_defaults
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX
    10→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, utc_now
    11→
    12→
    13→SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS = 90
    14→
    15→
    16→@dataclass
    17→class ReconcileResult:
    18→    """Summary of changes made during reconciliation."""
    19→
    20→    superseded: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    21→    pruned: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    22→    resurfaced: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    23→    changes: int = 0
    24→
    25→
    26→def _find_candidates(
    27→    state: StateModel, detector: str, file: str
    28→) -> list[str]:
    29→    """Find open findings that could be remaps for a disappeared finding."""
    30→    candidates: list[str] = []
    31→    for fid, finding in state.get("findings", {}).items():
    32→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    33→            continue
    34→        if finding.get("detector") == detector and finding.get("file") == file:
    35→            candidates.append(fid)
    36→    return candidates
    37→
    38→
    39→def _is_finding_alive(state: StateModel, finding_id: str) -> bool:
    40→    """Return True if the finding exists and is open."""
    41→    finding = state.get("findings", {}).get(finding_id)
    42→    if finding is None:
    43→        return False
    44→    return finding.get("status") == "open"
    45→
    46→
    47→def _supersede_id(
    48→    plan: PlanModel,
    49→    state: StateModel,
    50→    finding_id: str,
    51→    now: str,
    52→) -> bool:
    53→    """Move a disappeared finding to superseded. Returns True if changed."""
    54→    finding = state.get("findings", {}).get(finding_id)
    55→    detector = ""
    56→    file = ""
    57→    summary = ""
    58→    if finding:
    59→        detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    60→        file = finding.get("file", "")
    61→        summary = finding.get("summary", "")
    62→
    63→    candidates = _find_candidates(state, detector, file) if detector else []
    64→    # Don't include the original in candidates
    65→    candidates = [c for c in candidates if c != finding_id]
    66→
    67→    entry: SupersededEntry = {
    68→        "original_id": finding_id,
    69→        "original_detector": detector,
    70→        "original_file": file,
    71→        "original_summary": summary,
    72→        "status": "superseded",
    73→        "superseded_at": now,
    74→        "remapped_to": None,
    75→        "candidates": candidates[:5],
    76→    }
    77→
    78→    # Preserve any existing override note
    79→    override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(finding_id)
    80→    if override and override.get("note"):
    81→        entry["note"] = override["note"]
    82→
    83→    plan["superseded"][finding_id] = entry
    84→
    85→    # Remove from queue_order, skipped, cluster finding_ids
    86→    order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    87→    skipped: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
    88→    if finding_id in order:
    89→        order.remove(finding_id)
    90→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    91→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    92→        ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
    93→        if finding_id in ids:
    94→            ids.remove(finding_id)
    95→
    96→    return True
    97→
    98→
    99→def _prune_old_superseded(plan: PlanModel, now_dt: datetime) -> list[str]:
   100→    """Remove superseded entries older than TTL. Returns pruned IDs."""
   101→    superseded = plan.get("superseded", {})
   102→    cutoff = now_dt - timedelta(days=SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS)
   103→    to_prune: list[str] = []
   104→
   105→    for fid, entry in superseded.items():
   106→        ts = entry.get("superseded_at", "")
   107→        try:
   108→            entry_dt = datetime.fromisoformat(ts)
   109→            if entry_dt.tzinfo is None:
   110→                entry_dt = entry_dt.replace(tzinfo=UTC)
   111→            if entry_dt < cutoff:
   112→                to_prune.append(fid)
   113→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   114→            to_prune.append(fid)
   115→
   116→    for fid in to_prune:
   117→        superseded.pop(fid, None)
   118→        # Also clean up stale overrides
   119→        plan.get("overrides", {}).pop(fid, None)
   120→
   121→    return to_prune
   122→
   123→
   124→def reconcile_plan_after_scan(
   125→    plan: PlanModel,
   126→    state: StateModel,
   127→) -> ReconcileResult:
   128→    """Reconcile plan against current state after a scan.
   129→
   130→    Finds IDs referenced in the plan that no longer exist or are no longer
   131→    open, moves them to superseded, and prunes old superseded entries.
   132→    """
   133→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   134→    result = ReconcileResult()
   135→    now = utc_now()
   136→    now_dt = datetime.now(UTC)
   137→
   138→    # Collect all finding IDs referenced by the plan
   139→    referenced_ids: set[str] = set()
   140→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   141→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   142→    for override_id in plan.get("overrides", {}):
   143→        referenced_ids.add(override_id)
   144→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   145→        referenced_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   146→
   147→    # Exclude already-superseded IDs and synthetic IDs (managed by stale_dimensions)
   148→    already_superseded = set(plan.get("superseded", {}).keys())
   149→    referenced_ids -= already_superseded
   150→    referenced_ids = {
   151→        fid for fid in referenced_ids
   152→        if not fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX)
   153→    }
   154→
   155→    # Check each referenced ID
   156→    for fid in sorted(referenced_ids):
   157→        if not _is_finding_alive(state, fid):
   158→            if _supersede_id(plan, state, fid, now):
   159→                result.superseded.append(fid)
   160→                result.changes += 1
   161→
   162→    # Resurface stale temporary skips
   163→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   164→    from desloppify.engine._plan.operations import resurface_stale_skips
   165→
   166→    resurfaced = resurface_stale_skips(plan, scan_count)
   167→    if resurfaced:
   168→        result.resurfaced = resurfaced
   169→        result.changes += len(resurfaced)
   170→
   171→    # Prune old superseded entries
   172→    pruned = _prune_old_superseded(plan, now_dt)
   173→    result.pruned = pruned
   174→    result.changes += len(pruned)
   175→
   176→    return result
   177→
   178→
   179→__all__ = [
   180→    "ReconcileResult",
   181→    "reconcile_plan_after_scan",
   182→]
   183→
```

> AGENT

Good -- I can see that `queue_order` is populated by the reconciliation process (items are added to it by `move_items`, `unskip_items`, `resurface_stale_skips`) but notably, `reconcile_plan_after_scan` does NOT add new findings to `queue_order`. It only removes dead ones. Items get into `queue_order` via user actions (move, unskip) or the auto-cluster flow. Let me verify that auto-cluster adds to queue_order.

```
1→"""Auto-clustering algorithm — groups findings into task clusters."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→from collections import defaultdict
     7→
     8→from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS, DetectorMeta
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import Cluster, PlanModel, ensure_plan_defaults
    10→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import (
    11→    SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX,
    12→    _current_unscored_ids,
    13→)
    14→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, utc_now
    15→
    16→AUTO_PREFIX = "auto/"
    17→_MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE = 2
    18→_STALE_KEY = "subjective::stale"
    19→_STALE_NAME = "auto/stale-review"
    20→_UNSCORED_KEY = "subjective::unscored"
    21→_UNSCORED_NAME = "auto/initial-review"
    22→_MIN_UNSCORED_CLUSTER_SIZE = 1
    23→
    24→
    25→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    26→# Grouping key computation
    27→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    28→
    29→def _extract_subtype(finding: dict) -> str | None:
    30→    """Extract the subtype/kind from a finding.
    31→
    32→    Checks detail.kind first, then falls back to parsing the finding ID
    33→    (format: ``detector::file::subtype`` or ``detector::file::symbol::subtype``).
    34→    """
    35→    detail = finding.get("detail") or {}
    36→    kind = detail.get("kind")
    37→    if kind:
    38→        return kind
    39→
    40→    # Parse from finding ID — third segment is often the subtype
    41→    fid = finding.get("id", "")
    42→    parts = fid.split("::")
    43→    if len(parts) >= 3:
    44→        # For IDs like smells::file.py::silent_except, the subtype is parts[2]
    45→        # For IDs like dict_keys::file.py::key::phantom_read, subtype is last part
    46→        candidate = parts[-1]
    47→        # Skip if it looks like a file path or symbol name (contains / or .)
    48→        if "/" not in candidate and "." not in candidate:
    49→            return candidate
    50→    return None
    51→
    52→
    53→def _grouping_key(finding: dict, meta: DetectorMeta | None) -> str | None:
    54→    """Compute a deterministic grouping key for a finding.
    55→
    56→    Returns None if the finding should not be auto-clustered.
    57→    """
    58→    detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    59→
    60→    if meta is None:
    61→        # Unknown detector — group by detector name
    62→        return f"detector::{detector}"
    63→
    64→    # Review findings → group by dimension
    65→    if detector in ("review", "subjective_review"):
    66→        detail = finding.get("detail") or {}
    67→        dimension = detail.get("dimension", "")
    68→        if dimension:
    69→            return f"review::{dimension}"
    70→        return f"detector::{detector}"
    71→
    72→    # Per-file detectors (structural, responsibility_cohesion)
    73→    if meta.needs_judgment and detector in (
    74→        "structural", "responsibility_cohesion",
    75→    ):
    76→        file = finding.get("file", "")
    77→        if file:
    78→            basename = os.path.basename(file)
    79→            return f"file::{detector}::{basename}"
    80→
    81→    # Needs-judgment detectors — group by subtype when available
    82→    if meta.needs_judgment:
    83→        subtype = _extract_subtype(finding)
    84→        if subtype:
    85→            return f"typed::{detector}::{subtype}"
    86→
    87→    # Pure auto-fix (no judgment needed) → all findings by detector
    88→    if meta.action_type == "auto_fix" and not meta.needs_judgment:
    89→        return f"auto::{detector}"
    90→
    91→    # Everything else → by detector
    92→    return f"detector::{detector}"
    93→
    94→
    95→def _cluster_name_from_key(key: str) -> str:
    96→    """Convert a grouping key to a cluster name with auto/ prefix."""
    97→    # auto::unused → auto/unused
    98→    # typed::dict_keys::phantom_read → auto/dict_keys-phantom_read
    99→    # file::structural::big_file.py → auto/structural-big_file.py
   100→    # review::abstraction_fitness → auto/review-abstraction_fitness
   101→    # detector::security → auto/security
   102→    parts = key.split("::")
   103→    if len(parts) == 2:
   104→        prefix_type = parts[0]
   105→        # For review keys, keep the prefix for clarity
   106→        if prefix_type == "review":
   107→            return f"{AUTO_PREFIX}review-{parts[1]}"
   108→        return f"{AUTO_PREFIX}{parts[1]}"
   109→    if len(parts) == 3:
   110→        return f"{AUTO_PREFIX}{parts[1]}-{parts[2]}"
   111→    return f"{AUTO_PREFIX}{key.replace('::', '-')}"
   112→
   113→
   114→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   115→# Description and action generation
   116→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   117→
   118→def _generate_description(
   119→    cluster_name: str,
   120→    members: list[dict],
   121→    meta: DetectorMeta | None,
   122→    subtype: str | None,
   123→) -> str:
   124→    """Generate a human-readable cluster description."""
   125→    count = len(members)
   126→    detector = members[0].get("detector", "") if members else ""
   127→
   128→    if detector in ("review", "subjective_review"):
   129→        detail = (members[0].get("detail") or {}) if members else {}
   130→        dimension = detail.get("dimension", detector)
   131→        return f"Address {count} {dimension} review findings"
   132→
   133→    if detector == "structural":
   134→        files = {os.path.basename(m.get("file", "")) for m in members}
   135→        if len(files) == 1:
   136→            return f"Decompose {next(iter(files))}"
   137→        return f"Decompose {count} large files"
   138→
   139→    display = meta.display if meta else detector
   140→    if subtype:
   141→        label = subtype.replace("_", " ")
   142→        return f"Fix {count} {label} issues"
   143→
   144→    if meta and meta.action_type == "auto_fix" and not meta.needs_judgment:
   145→        return f"Remove {count} {display} findings"
   146→
   147→    return f"Fix {count} {display} issues"
   148→
   149→
   150→def _subtype_has_fixer(meta: DetectorMeta, subtype: str | None) -> str | None:
   151→    """Check if a subtype maps to a specific fixer."""
   152→    if not meta.fixers or not subtype:
   153→        return None
   154→    # Convention: fixer name is subtype with _ replaced by -
   155→    fixer_name = subtype.replace("_", "-")
   156→    if fixer_name in meta.fixers:
   157→        return fixer_name
   158→    # Also check if subtype is a substring (e.g. "unused" matches "unused-imports")
   159→    for fixer in meta.fixers:
   160→        if subtype in fixer:
   161→            return fixer
   162→    return None
   163→
   164→
   165→def _strip_guidance_examples(guidance: str) -> str:
   166→    """Strip subtype-specific examples from guidance text.
   167→
   168→    Many guidance strings have format "verb — specific examples".
   169→    Strip after the dash to get the core action:
   170→      "fix code smells — dead useEffect, empty if chains" → "fix code smells"
   171→    """
   172→    if " — " in guidance:
   173→        return guidance.split(" — ", 1)[0].strip()
   174→    return guidance
   175→
   176→
   177→_ACTION_TYPE_TEMPLATES: dict[str, str] = {
   178→    "reorganize": "reorganize with desloppify move",
   179→    "refactor": "review and refactor each finding",
   180→    "manual_fix": "review and fix each finding",
   181→}
   182→
   183→
   184→def _generate_action(
   185→    meta: DetectorMeta | None,
   186→    subtype: str | None,
   187→) -> str:
   188→    """Generate an action string from detector metadata.
   189→
   190→    Always returns a non-empty string — every cluster gets an action.
   191→    """
   192→    if meta is None:
   193→        return "review and fix each finding"
   194→
   195→    # For detectors with subtypes, only suggest a fixer if the subtype matches
   196→    if subtype and meta.fixers:
   197→        matched_fixer = _subtype_has_fixer(meta, subtype)
   198→        if matched_fixer:
   199→            return f"desloppify fix {matched_fixer} --dry-run"
   200→    elif meta.action_type == "auto_fix" and meta.fixers and not meta.needs_judgment:
   201→        # Pure auto-fix detector, no subtypes
   202→        return f"desloppify fix {meta.fixers[0]} --dry-run"
   203→
   204→    if meta.tool == "move":
   205→        return "desloppify move"
   206→
   207→    # Guidance available — use it (strip examples for subtyped detectors)
   208→    if meta.guidance:
   209→        if subtype:
   210→            return _strip_guidance_examples(meta.guidance)
   211→        return meta.guidance
   212→
   213→    # Final fallback: action_type template
   214→    return _ACTION_TYPE_TEMPLATES.get(
   215→        meta.action_type, "review and fix each finding"
   216→    )
   217→
   218→
   219→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   220→# Main algorithm
   221→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   222→
   223→def auto_cluster_findings(plan: PlanModel, state: StateModel) -> int:
   224→    """Regenerate auto-clusters from current open findings.
   225→
   226→    Returns count of changes made (clusters created, updated, or deleted).
   227→    """
   228→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   229→    changes = 0
   230→
   231→    findings = state.get("findings", {})
   232→    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   233→
   234→    # Set of finding IDs in manual (non-auto) clusters
   235→    manual_member_ids: set[str] = set()
   236→    for cluster in clusters.values():
   237→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   238→            manual_member_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   239→
   240→    # Collect open, non-suppressed findings and group by key
   241→    groups: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   242→    finding_data: dict[str, dict] = {}
   243→    for fid, finding in findings.items():
   244→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
   245→            continue
   246→        if finding.get("suppressed"):
   247→            continue
   248→        if fid in manual_member_ids:
   249→            continue
   250→
   251→        detector = finding.get("detector", "")
   252→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   253→        key = _grouping_key(finding, meta)
   254→        if key is None:
   255→            continue
   256→
   257→        groups[key].append(fid)
   258→        finding_data[fid] = finding
   259→
   260→    # Drop singleton groups
   261→    groups = {k: v for k, v in groups.items() if len(v) >= _MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE}
   262→
   263→    # Map existing auto-clusters by cluster_key
   264→    existing_by_key: dict[str, str] = {}  # cluster_key → cluster_name
   265→    for name, cluster in list(clusters.items()):
   266→        if cluster.get("auto"):
   267→            ck = cluster.get("cluster_key", "")
   268→            if ck:
   269→                existing_by_key[ck] = name
   270→
   271→    now = utc_now()
   272→    active_auto_keys: set[str] = set()
   273→
   274→    for key, member_ids in groups.items():
   275→        active_auto_keys.add(key)
   276→        cluster_name = _cluster_name_from_key(key)
   277→
   278→        # Representative finding for metadata
   279→        rep = finding_data.get(member_ids[0], {})
   280→        detector = rep.get("detector", "")
   281→        meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   282→        members = [finding_data[fid] for fid in member_ids if fid in finding_data]
   283→
   284→        # Extract subtype from grouping key (typed::detector::subtype)
   285→        key_parts = key.split("::")
   286→        subtype = key_parts[2] if len(key_parts) >= 3 else None
   287→
   288→        description = _generate_description(cluster_name, members, meta, subtype)
   289→        action = _generate_action(meta, subtype)
   290→
   291→        existing_name = existing_by_key.get(key)
   292→        if existing_name and existing_name in clusters:
   293→            cluster = clusters[existing_name]
   294→            if cluster.get("user_modified"):
   295→                # Merge new findings in, don't remove user's edits
   296→                existing_ids = set(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   297→                new_ids = [fid for fid in member_ids if fid not in existing_ids]
   298→                if new_ids:
   299→                    cluster["finding_ids"].extend(new_ids)
   300→                    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   301→                    changes += 1
   302→            else:
   303→                # Replace membership wholesale
   304→                old_ids = set(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   305→                new_ids = set(member_ids)
   306→                if old_ids != new_ids or cluster.get("description") != description or cluster.get("action") != action:
   307→                    cluster["finding_ids"] = list(member_ids)
   308→                    cluster["description"] = description
   309→                    cluster["action"] = action
   310→                    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   311→                    changes += 1
   312→        else:
   313→            # Handle name collision (existing auto-cluster with different key)
   314→            # If cluster_name already exists with a different key, skip
   315→            if cluster_name in clusters and clusters[cluster_name].get("cluster_key") != key:
   316→                # Append a disambiguator
   317→                cluster_name = f"{cluster_name}-{len(member_ids)}"
   318→
   319→            new_cluster: Cluster = {
   320→                "name": cluster_name,
   321→                "description": description,
   322→                "finding_ids": list(member_ids),
   323→                "created_at": now,
   324→                "updated_at": now,
   325→                "auto": True,
   326→                "cluster_key": key,
   327→                "action": action,
   328→                "user_modified": False,
   329→            }
   330→            clusters[cluster_name] = new_cluster
   331→            existing_by_key[key] = cluster_name
   332→            changes += 1
   333→
   334→        # Update overrides to track cluster membership
   335→        overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
   336→        current_name = existing_by_key.get(key, cluster_name)
   337→        for fid in member_ids:
   338→            if fid not in overrides:
   339→                overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   340→            overrides[fid]["cluster"] = current_name
   341→            overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   342→
   343→    # --- Subjective dimension clusters (unscored + stale) -------------------
   344→    all_subjective_ids = sorted(
   345→        fid for fid in plan.get("queue_order", [])
   346→        if fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX)
   347→    )
   348→    unscored_state_ids = _current_unscored_ids(state)
   349→    unscored_queue_ids = sorted(
   350→        fid for fid in all_subjective_ids if fid in unscored_state_ids
   351→    )
   352→    stale_queue_ids = sorted(
   353→        fid for fid in all_subjective_ids if fid not in unscored_state_ids
   354→    )
   355→
   356→    # -- Initial review cluster (unscored, min size 1) ---------------------
   357→    if len(unscored_queue_ids) >= _MIN_UNSCORED_CLUSTER_SIZE:
   358→        active_auto_keys.add(_UNSCORED_KEY)
   359→        cli_keys = [fid.removeprefix(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX) for fid in unscored_queue_ids]
   360→        description = f"Initial review of {len(unscored_queue_ids)} unscored subjective dimensions"
   361→        action = f"desloppify review --prepare --dimensions {','.join(cli_keys)}"
   362→
   363→        existing_name = existing_by_key.get(_UNSCORED_KEY)
   364→        if existing_name and existing_name in clusters:
   365→            cluster = clusters[existing_name]
   366→            old_ids = set(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   367→            new_ids = set(unscored_queue_ids)
   368→            if old_ids != new_ids or cluster.get("description") != description or cluster.get("action") != action:
   369→                cluster["finding_ids"] = unscored_queue_ids
   370→                cluster["description"] = description
   371→                cluster["action"] = action
   372→                cluster["updated_at"] = now
   373→                changes += 1
   374→        else:
   375→            clusters[_UNSCORED_NAME] = {
   376→                "name": _UNSCORED_NAME,
   377→                "description": description,
   378→                "finding_ids": unscored_queue_ids,
   379→                "created_at": now,
   380→                "updated_at": now,
   381→                "auto": True,
   382→                "cluster_key": _UNSCORED_KEY,
   383→                "action": action,
   384→                "user_modified": False,
   385→            }
   386→            existing_by_key[_UNSCORED_KEY] = _UNSCORED_NAME
   387→            changes += 1
   388→
   389→        overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
   390→        current_name = existing_by_key.get(_UNSCORED_KEY, _UNSCORED_NAME)
   391→        for fid in unscored_queue_ids:
   392→            if fid not in overrides:
   393→                overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   394→            overrides[fid]["cluster"] = current_name
   395→            overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   396→
   397→    # -- Stale review cluster (previously scored, min size 2) --------------
   398→    if len(stale_queue_ids) >= _MIN_CLUSTER_SIZE:
   399→        active_auto_keys.add(_STALE_KEY)
   400→        cli_keys = [fid.removeprefix(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX) for fid in stale_queue_ids]
   401→        description = f"Re-review {len(stale_queue_ids)} stale subjective dimensions"
   402→        action = f"desloppify review --prepare --dimensions {','.join(cli_keys)}"
   403→
   404→        existing_name = existing_by_key.get(_STALE_KEY)
   405→        if existing_name and existing_name in clusters:
   406→            cluster = clusters[existing_name]
   407→            old_ids = set(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   408→            new_ids = set(stale_queue_ids)
   409→            if old_ids != new_ids or cluster.get("description") != description or cluster.get("action") != action:
   410→                cluster["finding_ids"] = stale_queue_ids
   411→                cluster["description"] = description
   412→                cluster["action"] = action
   413→                cluster["updated_at"] = now
   414→                changes += 1
   415→        else:
   416→            clusters[_STALE_NAME] = {
   417→                "name": _STALE_NAME,
   418→                "description": description,
   419→                "finding_ids": stale_queue_ids,
   420→                "created_at": now,
   421→                "updated_at": now,
   422→                "auto": True,
   423→                "cluster_key": _STALE_KEY,
   424→                "action": action,
   425→                "user_modified": False,
   426→            }
   427→            existing_by_key[_STALE_KEY] = _STALE_NAME
   428→            changes += 1
   429→
   430→        overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
   431→        current_name = existing_by_key.get(_STALE_KEY, _STALE_NAME)
   432→        for fid in stale_queue_ids:
   433→            if fid not in overrides:
   434→                overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   435→            overrides[fid]["cluster"] = current_name
   436→            overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   437→
   438→    # Delete stale auto-clusters (no matching group, not user_modified)
   439→    for name in list(clusters.keys()):
   440→        cluster = clusters[name]
   441→        if not cluster.get("auto"):
   442→            continue
   443→        ck = cluster.get("cluster_key", "")
   444→        if ck in active_auto_keys:
   445→            continue
   446→        if cluster.get("user_modified"):
   447→            # Keep user-modified clusters but prune dead member IDs
   448→            alive = [fid for fid in cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   449→                     if fid in findings and findings[fid].get("status") == "open"]
   450→            if alive:
   451→                if len(alive) != len(cluster.get("finding_ids", [])):
   452→                    cluster["finding_ids"] = alive
   453→                    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   454→                    changes += 1
   455→                continue
   456→            # All members gone — delete even if user_modified
   457→        # Delete stale cluster
   458→        del clusters[name]
   459→        # Clear cluster refs from overrides
   460→        for fid in cluster.get("finding_ids", []):
   461→            override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(fid)
   462→            if override and override.get("cluster") == name:
   463→                override["cluster"] = None
   464→                override["updated_at"] = now
   465→        if plan.get("active_cluster") == name:
   466→            plan["active_cluster"] = None
   467→        changes += 1
   468→
   469→    plan["updated"] = now
   470→    return changes
   471→
   472→
   473→__all__ = [
   474→    "AUTO_PREFIX",
   475→    "auto_cluster_findings",
   476→]
   477→
```

> AGENT

Now I can see the critical insight: `auto_cluster_findings` adds every clustered finding's ID to `overrides` (lines 335-341) for cluster membership tracking. That is the source of the huge "Overrides: 4311" number. The overrides dict is inflated by cluster membership bookkeeping, not by meaningful user annotations.

I now have a complete understanding. Here is the full implementation plan.

---

## Implementation Plan

### Problem 1: Make `plan done` a Thin Wrapper Around `resolve fixed`

**Motivation:**

The current `cmd_plan_done` at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py` (lines 198-238) is a simplified reimplementation of `cmd_resolve` that misses five important behaviors:

1. No score delta display (no `print_score_update` or `_print_score_movement`)
2. No narrative milestone computation (no `compute_narrative`)
3. No `query.json` update (no `_write_resolve_query_entry`)
4. No next-step guidance (no `_print_next_command`)
5. No cluster-name pattern expansion (resolves patterns directly, not via `_try_expand_cluster`)
6. No `--note` support

It also lacks the wontfix batch safety check, but since `plan done` only does `status="fixed"` that is not relevant.

**Approach:**

Rewrite `cmd_plan_done` to construct a synthetic `argparse.Namespace` and delegate to `cmd_resolve`. This gives full feature parity with zero code duplication.

**Changes per file:**

**File 1: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/override_handlers.py`**

Replace the `cmd_plan_done` function body (lines 198-238). The new implementation:

```python
def cmd_plan_done(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Mark findings as fixed (delegates to ``resolve fixed``).

    Shorthand for ``desloppify resolve fixed <patterns> --attest ...``
    within the plan workflow.
    """
    from desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd import cmd_resolve

    patterns: list[str] = getattr(args, "patterns", [])
    attestation: str | None = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    note: str | None = getattr(args, "note", None)

    resolve_args = argparse.Namespace(
        status="fixed",
        patterns=patterns,
        note=note,
        attest=attestation,
        confirm_batch_wontfix=False,
        state=getattr(args, "state", None),
        lang=getattr(args, "lang", None),
        path=getattr(args, "path", None),
    )
    cmd_resolve(resolve_args)
```

The imports that are no longer needed by `cmd_plan_done` (`state_mod`, `state_path`, `purge_ids`, `ATTEST_EXAMPLE`) should be checked -- they are still used by `cmd_plan_skip`, so they stay at the top of the file. The `show_attestation_requirement` and `validate_attestation` imports also stay (used by `cmd_plan_skip`).

The top-level `from desloppify.engine.plan import purge_ids` import is not present at module level -- it was a lazy import inside `cmd_plan_done`. The `ATTEST_EXAMPLE` import is also lazy. So there are no top-level import removals needed.

To:
```
desloppify plan done <pat> --attest .. # shorthand for resolve fixed
```

**Edge cases to handle:**

- The `resolve_args` Namespace must include `lang` and `path` because `cmd_resolve` calls `resolve_lang(args)` which reads `args.lang`. It also uses `state_path(args)` which reads `args.state`. These are propagated from the original `args`.
- `cmd_resolve` calls `_validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)` which checks `args.status == "wontfix"` and `args.note`. Since we set `status="fixed"`, the wontfix check does not fire. The attestation validation still works.
- `cmd_resolve` already does the plan purge (lines 91-102 of resolve/cmd.py). So the delegation naturally handles plan cleanup.

### Problem 2: Header Count Mismatch in `plan queue`

**Root cause:**

In `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py`, line 74-78:
- `non_skipped` = `len(items) - skipped_count` = count of collapsed queue entries
- `tier_counts` = computed by `ranking.py:tier_counts()` which unpacks cluster members

When clusters are collapsed, `non_skipped` might be 266 while `sum(tier_counts)` is 813.

**Changes:**

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/queue_render.py`**

Replace lines 73-78:

```python
    # Header
    total = len(items)
    skipped_count = sum(1 for it in items if it.get("plan_skipped"))
    non_skipped = total - skipped_count
    tc_parts = " ".join(f"T{t}:{tier_counts.get(t, 0)}" for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))
    finding_total = sum(tier_counts.get(t, 0) for t in (1, 2, 3, 4))

    if finding_total != non_skipped:
        # Collapsed clusters make entry count differ from finding count
        print(colorize(
            f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} entries  ({finding_total} findings: {tc_parts})",
            "bold",
        ))
    else:
        print(colorize(f"\n  Queue: {non_skipped} items  ({tc_parts})", "bold"))
```

This is a 6-line change in one file. The conditional ensures no visual regression when there are no collapsed clusters.

### Problem 3: Improve `plan show` Output

**Root cause analysis from the code:**

Looking at `_cmd_plan_show` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cmd.py` (lines 18-51):

1. `Ordered items: N` -- shows `len(plan["queue_order"])`. This is ALL items in the queue, not just user-reordered ones. The queue_order list is the full ordered queue -- it is not limited to user-modified items. This label is misleading.

2. `Overrides: N` -- shows `len(plan["overrides"])`. The overrides dict is inflated by auto-cluster membership tracking (every clustered finding gets an override entry just to record which cluster it belongs to). The actual user-meaningful overrides are those with `description` or `note` fields set to non-None values.

3. `Superseded: N` -- jargon. Users don't know what "superseded" means in this context.

**Changes:**

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/plan/cmd.py`**

Replace `_cmd_plan_show` (lines 18-51) with:

```python
def _cmd_plan_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    """Show plan metadata summary."""
    plan = load_plan()
    queue_order = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    skipped = plan.get("skipped", {})
    total_skipped = len(skipped)
    temp_count = sum(1 for e in skipped.values() if e.get("kind") == "temporary")
    perm_count = sum(1 for e in skipped.values() if e.get("kind") == "permanent")
    fp_count = sum(1 for e in skipped.values() if e.get("kind") == "false_positive")
    clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
    overrides = plan.get("overrides", {})
    active = plan.get("active_cluster")
    superseded = plan.get("superseded", {})

    # Count meaningful annotations (description or note set)
    described_count = sum(
        1 for o in overrides.values()
        if o.get("description")
    )
    noted_count = sum(
        1 for o in overrides.values()
        if o.get("note")
    )

    print(colorize("  Living Plan Status", "bold"))
    print(colorize("  " + "\u2500" * 40, "dim"))
    print(f"  Queue:            {len(queue_order)} items")
    if total_skipped:
        print(f"  Skipped:          {total_skipped} (temp: {temp_count}, wontfix: {perm_count}, fp: {fp_count})")
    else:
        print(f"  Skipped:          0")
    print(f"  Clusters:         {len(clusters)}")
    if clusters:
        for name, cluster in clusters.items():
            desc = cluster.get("description") or ""
            member_count = len(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
            marker = " (focused)" if name == active else ""
            desc_str = f" \u2014 {desc}" if desc else ""
            print(f"    {name}: {member_count} items{desc_str}{marker}")
    if described_count or noted_count:
        print(f"  Annotations:      {described_count} described, {noted_count} noted")
    if active:
        print(f"  Focus:            {active}")
    if superseded:
        print(f"  Disappeared:      {len(superseded)} (resolved or removed since last scan)")
```

Key changes:
1. **"Ordered items" becomes "Queue"** -- renamed to reflect that it shows the queue size, not just user-reordered items.
2. **"Overrides: 4311" is replaced by "Annotations: 5 described, 8 noted"** -- counts only meaningful user annotations. The raw override count (inflated by cluster membership tracking) is hidden as an implementation detail. If both counts are 0, the line is omitted entirely.
3. **"Superseded: 22" becomes "Disappeared: 22 (resolved or removed since last scan)"** -- clearer label with inline explanation.

### Testing Strategy

**Problem 1 tests:**

There are currently zero tests for `cmd_plan_done` (confirmed by grepping). Add a new test class to `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/commands/test_cmd_plan.py`:

```python
class TestCmdPlanDone:
    """Verify plan done delegates to resolve fixed."""

    def test_done_delegates_to_resolve(self, monkeypatch):
        """plan done should construct correct args and call cmd_resolve."""
        captured_args = []

        def fake_resolve(args):
            captured_args.append(args)

        monkeypatch.setattr(
            "desloppify.app.commands.resolve.cmd.cmd_resolve",
            fake_resolve,
        )

        from desloppify.app.commands.plan.override_handlers import cmd_plan_done

        class FakeArgs:
            patterns = ["unused::foo.py::bar"]
            attest = "I have actually removed the import and I am not gaming the score"
            note = "removed unused import"
            state = None
            lang = None
            path = "."

        cmd_plan_done(FakeArgs())

        assert len(captured_args) == 1
        resolve_args = captured_args[0]
        assert resolve_args.status == "fixed"
        assert resolve_args.patterns == ["unused::foo.py::bar"]
        assert resolve_args.note == "removed unused import"
        assert resolve_args.attest == FakeArgs.attest
```

**Problem 2 tests:**

Add a test to verify the queue header correctly shows dual counts when clusters are collapsed. This would go in a new test file or extend existing plan command tests.

**Problem 3 tests:**

Add a test that loads a plan with overrides that include cluster-only entries versus user annotations, and verify the output shows the correct annotation counts rather than the raw override count.

### Sequencing and Dependencies

These three fixes are independent and can be implemented in parallel or any order. However, the recommended order is:

1. **Problem 2 (queue header)** -- smallest change, lowest risk, self-contained in one file
2. **Problem 3 (plan show)** -- medium change, self-contained in one file
3. **Problem 1 (plan done delegation)** -- largest change spanning 3 files, needs the most careful testing

### Potential Risks

**Problem 1:** The `cmd_resolve` function calls `resolve_lang(args)` which expects `args.lang`. If `plan done` is invoked without a `--lang` flag being defined in the plan parser, `args.lang` will be `None`. This is fine -- `resolve_lang` handles `None` by auto-detecting the language. Similarly, `args.path` defaults to `None` and is handled by auto-detection. The synthetic Namespace must include these attributes to avoid `AttributeError`.

**Problem 1 additional concern:** `cmd_resolve` calls `_validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)` which accesses `args.status` and `args.note`. Both are set in the synthetic Namespace. It also calls `_enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation` which accesses `args.status` (will be "fixed", so the check returns early). This is safe.

     ```python
     def _cluster_tier_label(item: dict) -> str:
         """Compute a tier label from cluster members, e.g. 'T2' or 'T1-T3'."""
         members = item.get("members", [])
         if not members:
             return ""
         tiers = {int(m.get("effective_tier", m.get("tier", 3))) for m in members}
         lo, hi = min(tiers), max(tiers)
         if lo == hi:
             return f"T{lo}"
         return f"T{lo}-T{hi}"
     ```

5. Problem Solving:

   - Identified that `tier_counts()` deliberately unpacks cluster members (correct for showing finding distribution) but mixing this with collapsed entry count in the header creates confusion.
